# Decisions

Choices made while building that the specification left open. Newest phase first.

## How changes are made

- **Spec signed off (2026-11-22):** [AI Workshop Platform: Spec](https://claude.ai/artifact/6mNkKPKQysWNoj7eL6Gyus), signed off by Franck, all 23 confirm-or-correct points confirmed.
- **Workflow:** each phase starts with a brief in `docs/phases/` and one round of questions.
  During a ticket, choices that can be undone are decided, recorded here and listed for veto in
  the pull request; only the cases under "Stop and ask" in `CLAUDE.md` wait for Franck. Each
  ticket is built in fresh-session tries and judged by the `checker` agent, at most 3 tries.
- **Spec deltas:** a change to signed-off behaviour is recorded here as ADDED, CHANGED or REMOVED,
  with the date and the pull request.

## Phase 3b: photos in apps, more storage, clearer refusals

Franck's answers to the brief's questions (2026-11-26, `docs/phases/phase-3b.md`, PR #65): all as suggested.

- **Photos and storage (spec delta, approved by Franck 2026-11-26):** CHANGED section 5 Security: "Each app's stored data is limited to 1 MB ..." becomes "Each app may store up to 10 MB of data and 500 MB of photos and voice recordings. Both are visible to anyone with the app's URL (apps have no logins), which participants are told." CHANGED the section 10 confirmation to match. ADDED section 4 item 1: an app may take photos with the phone's camera or pick them from the phone and keep them in the workshop's photo store; photos are shrunk and stripped of location data before they are kept.
- **Limits:** 4 MB per photo (after shrinking to 2,560 px), 500 MB and 2,000 photos per app, 500 MB uploaded per app per day; data 10 MB per app, 350 KB per value, 500 keys, 50 MB written per day.
- **Abuse cost (Stop-and-ask 3, accepted):** the 3.13 per-address WAF rate rule is extended to `/app-media/*`; the $20 Budgets alert and Cost Anomaly email stay; the remaining risk of a distributed download flood is accepted. Superseded by 3.18 (CHANGED): `/app-media/*` gets a separate per-address rule (`ssd-workshop-media-rate`) instead of extending the 3.13 rule, accepted by Franck with the 3,000 per 5 minutes limit on 2026-11-26 19:35 UTC; see "(3.18) WAF".
- **Photo content not checked by AI (accepted):** facilitators wipe an app with `npm run participant -- reset`; Clarify warns participants not to photograph people or confidential papers.
- **Language:** AI-written texts (decline sentence, readiness reasons, chat) follow the participant's language; the page's fixed buttons and messages stay English.
- **AI tasks for apps (spec delta, approved by Franck 2026-11-26, question 7):** ADDED section 4 item 1: "An app may use the AI tasks the workshop offers (suggest tags for a photo, read named fields from a photo, sort a text into categories, and with question 8 transcribe a recording), never a free prompt." CHANGED the readiness rule "AI or chat services" to "AI other than these tasks". Caps doubled from the suggestion ("these little apps will be used only briefly"): $1 per app per day, $10 a day for all apps, admin switch "Apps may use AI" off by default; app AI spend is kept apart from the participant's $5.
- **AI tasks for apps, a third limit (spec delta to question 7's answer, decided by Franck 2026-11-27 04:15 UTC):** ADDED to the caps of question 7: "Each signed-in person may spend at most $1 of app AI per UTC day across all apps", beside $1 per app per day and $10 a day for all apps (ticket 3.21 try 2, see "(3.21) Per-writer cap"). Nothing else in question 7's answer changes.
- **Media, not only photos (Franck, 2026-11-26):** the store takes voice recordings too (WebM/Opus, Ogg/Opus, MP4/AAC, at most 5 minutes), in the same 500 MB and 2,000 files per app; bucket `ssd-workshop-app-media`, served at `/app-media/*`. Video stays out (size).
- **Amazon Transcribe (new paid service, approved by Franck 2026-11-26, question 8):** a `transcribe` AI task turns an app's voice recording into text (then `sort-text` gives topics); about $0.024 a minute in eu-central-1, counted inside the $10 daily cap for all apps. Needs an access-stack change Franck applies.
- **Microphone check on both platforms (Franck, 2026-11-26):** ticket 3.19's live check covers an iPhone (Safari) and an Android phone (Chrome).
- **Retention:** photos follow their app (the phase 5 removal workflow deletes them); no automatic expiry before that.
- **(3.16) Prompts:** `guardrail-v4` (a decline or flag needs something the message says, never a guess about what someone might later type, store or photograph; ideas that send emails, texts or notifications, to the participant or a list, are on-path and Clarify offers an in-page version; collecting other people's contact or private details stays decline), `clarify-v5` (the real storage facts: the workshop keeps the data, everyone with the app's link sees and can change it, nothing stays only on the phone or browser, never promise "stays on your phone"; "send" ideas get a message board people read on the app's page, never a question about addresses, contact lists or mail servers; replies in the participant's language, "plain English" became "plain words"; everything else from clarify-v4 kept), `readiness-v3` (reasons in the participant's language, each ending with the problem's English word in brackets; no double quotes inside a reason, after a quoted message broke the JSON in the live eval).
- **(3.16) Decline in the participant's language (please veto):** the guardrail JSON gains a `decline` field: the whole sentence, written by the model in the message's language, only on a decline. It is used only when it keeps the decline's shape (one line, 20 to 250 characters, no markup, link, `@` or `/`, no `. ! ? ; : …` before the end, ending with a question mark, `localDecline` in `guardrail.ts`); otherwise the English template with the (English) `suggestion` as before. The sentence is not stored: a facilitator's Reject (no stored sentence) keeps the English template; `suggestion` and `reason` stay English, so the template and the facilitator's view never mix languages. Guardrail `maxTokens` 300 → 400 for the extra sentence.
- **(3.16) Readiness reasons' language (please veto):** the AI worker sends the participant's latest own message (from the answered history before the request, platform requests such as "Please write my spec." skipped, at most 500 characters, in `<participant>` tags, data only) before the spec, and the reasons follow its language; with no such message or a failed history read, the spec's language. Rebuild from my repository / with my edits send no message (the spec there is the participant's own text, so its language is theirs).
- **(3.16) Agreed form for readiness reasons (please veto):** each reason ends with the problem's English word in brackets, "(login)", "(payment)", "(outside service)", "(server)" or "(open question)", whatever the reason's language; `readinessProblem` (used by the tests and evals; the worker shows the reasons as written) reads it (or, for an English reason without brackets, the first of the five words it contains). The parser does not drop a reason without it (a missing bracket must not make the check "unreadable" and send the participant round in circles); the evals hold the form (French examples expect "(outside service)" and "(server)"). Participants see the bracket, as a fixed English label.
- **(3.16) Tags in untrusted text (please veto):** spec, participant and message tags are removed until none is left (`withoutTags`, `platform/api/conversation/tags.ts`), not in one pass, so nested tags (`</parti</participant>cipant>`) cannot rebuild a closing tag; used for the readiness spec, the guardrail message and (same weakness, ticket 3.6) the suggestions spec. Other text in the spec, a lone `<` or `>` included, is kept. The participant's language sample drops every `<` and `>` (it only sets the language). The localized decline also falls back to the English template when it holds a run of 6 or more digits (spaces, dots or dashes between them counted) or a dot look-alike (`．｡․﹒‧∙⋅`) that could join a domain; the Catalan middle dot `·` stays allowed.
- **(3.16) Evals:** `guardrails.json` gains the photographed documents and two sending ideas (on-path), "collect my colleagues' phone numbers and home addresses" (decline), a French on-path idea and two French declines whose `declineWords` (désolé, atelier, plutôt, mais, pourquoi) the shown sentence must name, the model's own sentence and not the template. `clarify.json` gains `mentionsEach` (every word group named) and a check `answers-question` (a question mid-conversation, at most 5 numbered questions): reminders sent by email (page + board/read/see/open, no server, addresses or API), "will my data stay on my phone?" (workshop + anyone/everyone + link/address), and an idea in French. `readiness.json` gains two French specs (headings stay English, as `checkSpec` requires) with `participantText` and `languageWords` (votre, vos, vous) that every reason must name.
- **(3.17) Data API limits:** `MAX_APP_DATA_BYTES` 10 MB (10,485,760 bytes), `MAX_VALUE_BYTES` 350 KB (358,400 bytes), `MAX_KEYS` 500, `DAILY_WRITE_BUDGET_BYTES` 50 MB (`platform/api/app-data/data.ts`), counted as in (2.4). The 413 reasons now read "This value is too big: a value can hold at most 350 KB.", "This app has no room left: an app can store at most 10 MB.", "This app already has 500 keys, the most it can keep. Remove one to add another."; the 429 budget reason is unchanged. A base64 body over 477,868 bytes (~467 KB, `MAX_RAW_BASE64_BYTES`, derived) is refused before decoding. `templates/basic/AGENTS.md`, `infra/README.md` and the code comments state the new numbers; `workshop-data.js`, the Clarify prompt and the web pages state none. Readiness prompt `readiness-v4`: "up to 1 MB per app" became "up to 10 MB per app".
- **(3.17) One bracket per reason (please veto):** with only the 1 MB → 10 MB change, the live readiness eval's "playlists after signing in to a music service" came back unreadable in 4 of 33 tries (0 of 18 on `readiness-v3`, same session): the model ended one reason with "(login), (outside service)", then wrote "Wait, ..." and a second JSON object, which the parser refuses. `readiness-v4` also says "when one part of the spec has two problems, write two reasons, one bracket each": 12 of 12 tries of that example, then three full runs of `npm run eval -- readiness` at 17/17. The parser is unchanged (a self-corrected answer stays unreadable, and the participant is asked to approve again).
- **(3.17) DynamoDB item size (checked, store.test.ts):** the largest value item (350 KB value, 20-character nickname, 64-character key) is under 350 KB + `VALUE_ITEM_OVERHEAD_BYTES` (256), ~50 KB below DynamoDB's 400 KB item limit; the totals item at its largest (500 keys of 64 characters, each `sizes` entry a 6-digit size, full totals and day count) is under `usageItemBytes`' bound, 256 + 500 x 72 = 36,256 bytes (~35.4 KB). `USAGE_ENTRY_BYTES` 8 still holds: a size of at most 358,400 has 6 digits.
- **(3.17) No infrastructure change:** the data function (256 MB, 10 s) parses a 350 KB body in milliseconds; API Gateway HTTP API takes 10 MB payloads (Lambda 6 MB), CloudFront has no body limit that matters here, and the WAF rule is rate-based only (no body inspection). `npm run synth` changes no resource but three functions' code (compared with `main`'s templates): the data function (`ssd-workshop-api-app-data`, the limits), and, because the brief's own changes live in their bundles, the AI worker (`ssd-workshop-api-ai-worker`, the readiness prompt) and the launcher (`ssd-workshop-builds-launcher`, which bundles `templates/basic/AGENTS.md`). No property, role, policy or other resource changes.
- **(3.17) Cost note (replaces the 5 MB figures in "(2.4) Daily write budget"):** a write counts what DynamoDB bills, 2 write units per KB counted in a transaction; 50 MB = 51,200 KB is ~102,400 write units, ~$0.078 per app per day at $0.7625 per million, at worst (an app driven to its budget every day): ~30 live apps ~$2.34 a day, ~$70 a month (was ~$7). A loop of 350 KB replacements stops after 145 writes (352 KB each); 50 MB is ~25,000 small writes. Without an attacker a workshop day still costs cents.
- **(3.17) Remaining worst-case cost, recomputed (for Franck, Stop-and-ask 3; please veto or accept):** the (2.4) scenario (all four routes held at their throttles for 24 hours, 30 live apps), now with 350 KB values to read back and 500 keys: DynamoDB ~$43 a day (writes capped by the budget $2.34; reads by writes, a totals item of up to 9 read units, up to 1 + 4 x 9 = 37 read units each under forced races, $29; `GET`s of a 350 KB value 45 read units each, $11.90), API Gateway $9.60 (a request under 512 KB is metered once), Lambda ~$3 to $4, logs $2.70: ~$59 a day this API's own (was ~$26). Shared with the site: CloudFront requests $10.40, `GET` egress ~620 GB $53, `PUT` bodies to the origin ~930 GB $18.60: ~$82 a day (was ~$31). In all ~$140 a day, ~$4,200 a month if sustained for a month (was ~$1,700), under the same safeguards (WAF per-address rule on `/api/*` since 3.13, $20 Budgets alert, Cost Anomaly email). The budget-capped part alone, ~$70 a month at worst, is above the $50 threshold too. Lowering it later is one constant each (`DAILY_WRITE_BUDGET_BYTES`, `MAX_VALUE_BYTES`).
- **(3.17) Cost accepted (Franck, 2026-11-26):** "I accept, keep the limits", and plan for app writes to need the workshop's sign-in while reads stay public (tickets 3.24 and 3.25 in the brief), which removes most of the deliberate-attack risk.
- **Sign-in to save, brief questions 9 to 13 (Franck, 2026-11-26):** 9 yes: 3.24 and 3.25 are built right after 3.17, before 3.18 to 3.23. 10 yes: the deployer always ships the platform's current `workshop-data.js`, plus a one-off `npm run apps -- refresh`. 12 yes (spec delta): CHANGED section 5 Security "visible to anyone with the app's URL (apps have no logins)" becomes "visible to anyone with the app's URL; saving, uploading and AI tasks need the workshop's own sign-in (the same allow-listed email code). Apps have no logins of their own and no private per-person data."
- **Who may save (question 11, Franck: "yes, but only if this is easier to build than the owner only"):** any signed-in participant. It is the simpler one: the API checks only that the token is valid for this app, while owner-only also needs the app's owner looked up on every write and a second refusal message; it also keeps shared apps (a team message board) working. The writer's nickname is recorded on each value (`updatedBy`), never an email.
- **Token in the address (question 13, Franck: "yes, but only if this is easier to build"):** the simpler design is also the one without the sharing risk, so it is chosen: `workshop-data.js` reads the token from the fragment once, removes it from the address at once (`history.replaceState`), and keeps it in memory only. A copied address never carries it. Cost: after a reload the visitor taps "Sign in to save" again; with a live workshop session that is one Continue, no new code.
- **(3.24) App write tokens (please veto):** 32 random bytes (base64url, 43 characters), stored only as SHA-256 at `APPTOKEN#<hash>` / `TOKEN` with the app (lower-cased nickname), the writer's nickname, `createdAt` and `expiresAt` (the table's TTL). Made by `POST /api/app-tokens` `{"app": "<nickname>"}` on the participant function (session check): 200 `{token, app, nickname, expiresAt}`; 404 "This app is not live." for a name outside the nickname rules (no read), a reserved one or one without a live marker; 409 `no-nickname` before a nickname is chosen; 429 `too-many`; 409 `conflict` after 3 lost races; 401 without a session. The write check every app route uses is `checkAppWrite` (`platform/api/app-token/token.ts`), for 3.18 and 3.21 too.
- **(3.24) Token life (please veto):** 12 hours, but never after the session it came from (`min(now + 12 h, session end)`); sessions last 8 hours from sign-in, so in practice a token ends with its session. DynamoDB's TTL deletes late, so the API compares `expiresAt` with the clock.
- **(3.24) Revocation (please veto):** sign-out ends all of the participant's tokens, from every session and device, before it deletes the session (a failure keeps them signed in to try again), and once more after it (try 2, below); `npm run participant -- reset <nick> --yes` ends them too ("Ended N sign-ins to save in apps"). The tokens are found through one list item per participant, `TOKENS#<participant id>` / `TOKENS` (a map hash -> expiry), never a Scan. At most 50 live tokens per participant (each Continue makes one; expired entries are dropped from the list on the next one), then 429 "You have signed in to save in many apps today. Sign out of the workshop and sign in again to start over.", so a sign-out always has a bounded list to delete.
- **(3.24) Removed from the allow-list (please veto; changed in try 2 after the access review, L1):** `npm run -s allowlist -- remove <email> --yes` also ends the person's app write tokens at once: the entry's `participantId` link (already read for the profile's `removed` role, 3.10) names their token list, which is revoked like on sign-out, after the profile update and before the entry's delete (a failure leaves the entry, so running it again finishes). It prints only the count ("Removed the allow-list entry for this address and ended N sign-ins to save in apps. ..."), never an address, id or token. A person never signed in has no link and no tokens. Their next workshop call still signs them out (3.1). Try 3 (access review L1): the token create transaction also checks the participant's profile (`ConditionCheck` on `PARTICIPANT#<id>` / `PROFILE`, `attribute_exists(#pk) AND #role <> :removed`, the role `remove` writes before it revokes; a failure answers 401 like no session), so a token request that passed its session check just before the removal can no longer commit after the profile update, and one committed before it is listed and revoked. Proved over every interleaving of a token request and the real `DynamoAllowListStore.remove` in `platform/api/app-token/store.test.ts`, with a control (the profile check ignored, try 2) that leaves a token in some order.
- **(3.24) Data API order (please veto):** nickname rules (404), live marker (404), key rules (400), then the token for `PUT`/`DELETE` (401), then the body. A write to an app that is not live answers 404, as before, token or not; a 401 reads only the live marker and the token, never the totals or the value. `DELETE` stores no writer (the value is gone), but its log line names the writer (below).
- **(3.24) `?info=1` contract (please veto):** `GET .../data/<key>?info=1` answers `{"value": <value>, "updatedBy": <nickname or null>, "updatedAt": <ISO time or null>}` (null for a missing key and for values written before 3.24); any other `info` gives the plain answer, unchanged. The value item gains `updatedBy` (in `APP_DATA_WRITE_ATTRIBUTES`).
- **(3.24) Hand-off page (please veto):** `/app-sign-in/?app=<nickname>` (a folder page like `/facilitator/`; CloudFront behaviour `/app-sign-in*` with the `ssd-workshop-site-index` function, which now also redirects `/app-sign-in` to `/app-sign-in/` keeping the query), the pages' CSP. An `app` outside the nickname rules says "This app is not live." at once and asks the API nothing; whether a valid name is live is checked when Continue is pressed (one call fewer, the question shows for a wrong but valid name). Signed out: "Sign in to the workshop first, then you come back here." and a "Sign in to the workshop" link to `/?app=<nickname>`; the workshop page then shows "Sign in to save in <nickname>'s app. You come back to it afterwards." on the email screen and, after the code is accepted, goes to `/app-sign-in/?app=<nickname>`. No nickname yet: "Choose your workshop nickname first, then open this page again from the app." and a link home. Continue opens `/apps/<nickname lower-cased>/#wt=<token>` with `location.replace` (the hand-off page is not left in the history), only when the API's answer names the same app. Under the question: "The app can then save as you for 12 hours, or until you sign out of the workshop. Everyone with the app's link sees what you save, with your nickname." Texts in `APP_SIGN_IN_TEXTS` (`platform/api/workshop/texts.ts`); button "Continue". No diagram on this page (a one-question confirmation, not an explanation).
- **(3.24) CORS:** the function's preflight and CloudFront's data API headers policy allow `content-type, accept, authorization`; never `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials` (tested on the function, in Chromium and on the synth output). The policy is created as `ssd-workshop-app-data-v2` (policies cannot be updated); v1 stays retained for Franck to delete after the deploy.
- **(3.24) IAM:** the data function gains a GetItem in `APPTOKEN#*` of `app`, `nickname`, `expiresAt` only (`dynamodb:Select` `SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES`) and `updatedBy` in its write list; the participant function gains GetItem of `APP#*` `liveAt` and `TOKENS#*` `tokens`, `version` (both projected), PutItem in `APPTOKEN#*` and UpdateItem in `TOKENS#*` of their names only (`version` included; returning nothing), DeleteItem in `APPTOKEN#*` only (a list is emptied, never deleted, since try 2), and ConditionCheckItem in `SESSION#*` naming `pk`, `sk` (try 2: the create transaction's session check; try 3 adds `sk`: a transaction item's `dynamodb:Attributes` may include the key, and a list without it would refuse every token request live) and in `PARTICIPANT#*` naming `pk`, `sk`, `role` (try 3: the profile check). Try 3 also adds `pk` and `sk` to the three projected read lists (`APPTOKEN#*`, `TOKENS#*`, `APP#*` `liveAt`), like every other read list of `infra/lib/config.ts`; the put and update lists already had them (synth test and `store.test.ts`). No other role reaches either partition (synth test). The boundary already allows DynamoDB on `ssd-workshop-*` tables: no access stack change.
- **(3.24) Popups escape the sandbox (spec delta, approved by Franck 2026-11-26):** CHANGED decision "Apps sandboxed" (with its (2.3) and (2.14) loading limits). Old: `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox allow-scripts allow-forms allow-modals allow-popups; default-src 'self'; connect-src 'self' https:; img-src 'self' data: https:; form-action 'self'`. New: `sandbox allow-scripts allow-forms allow-modals allow-popups allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox; default-src 'self'; connect-src 'self' https:; img-src 'self' data: https:; form-action 'self'`. Why (access review M1): a tab an app opens inherits the app's sandbox, so `/app-sign-in` opened from an app ran in an opaque origin, its `GET /api/me` went out cross-origin without the session cookie, and a signed-in participant was told to sign in (or that the workshop could not be reached); "Sign in to save" could never work from an app. Risk: apps could already open tabs to any site (`allow-popups`; links are not covered by the CSP, only by the deploy check's address scan); those tabs are now normal pages under their own site's CSP instead of sandboxed copies. The app itself stays sandboxed in its opaque origin and cannot read or script the tab it opened (cross-origin). A workshop page opened from an app now runs with the visitor's session, as if the visitor had typed its address: it acts only on what the visitor then presses (the hand-off page asks before making a token). Proved in Chromium (`platform/web/app-sign-in-popup.test.ts`): an app served with the real apps CSP opens the built hand-off page (pages' CSP) by a `target="_blank"` link and by `window.open`; the new tab's origin is the site's, `GET /api/me` arrives with the `__Host-` `SameSite=Strict` session cookie and no `Origin: null`, and the page asks "Save in ada's app as Bob?"; a control with the old CSP shows the opaque tab, no cookie and no question. iPhone Safari and Android Chrome are checked live (`infra/README.md`). CloudFront's apps policy is created as `ssd-workshop-apps-v3` (policies cannot be updated); v2 stays retained for Franck to delete after the deploy.
- **(3.24) No token survives a sign-out (try 2, access review M2):** the token list `TOKENS#<participant id>` carries a `version` that every change raises by one: a create's list update holds only while the version is the one it read (`attribute_not_exists(#version)` for the first token), and a revocation deletes the listed tokens and then empties the list (`SET tokens = {}`, version + 1) under the same condition, never deleting it, so the version only rises (a list the table's TTL removes after 12 idle hours starts again at 1; no request lives that long). The size-only condition of try 1 let a create that raced a revocation leave a live token unlisted. The create transaction also checks that the caller's session item still exists (`ConditionCheck` `attribute_exists(#pk)` on `SESSION#<hash>`; a failure answers 401 like no session, outcome `unauthorized`). Sign-out is `signOutWithAppTokens` (`platform/api/app-token/token.ts`): revoke, delete the session, revoke again. After the session is gone no token can be made, and every token made before is listed, so the last pass ends whatever the first missed. Tested in `platform/api/app-token/store.test.ts` against a small in-memory DynamoDB that evaluates the store's real conditions and updates, over every interleaving of a token request (with the route's retries) and a sign-out (exactly 36 orders, asserted): none leaves a token; a control with try 1's sign-out (one revoke, then the delete) leaves one in some order. Try 3 (checker): the revocation after the session's delete is tried 3 times (`LAST_REVOKE_ATTEMPTS`); if all fail, the sign-out still answers 200 (the session is gone, so there is nothing for the person to retry) and logs only the outcome `signed-out-revoke-failed`. Residual (please veto): in that case a token whose request raced the sign-out may live on until its expiry (at most the session's end, 8 hours from sign-in); `npm run participant -- reset <nick> --yes` ends it (`infra/README.md`).
- **(3.24) Optional follow-up, not built (access review INFO-1):** `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin` on the pages (the pages' response headers policy, a new `ssd-workshop-pages-v<N>`) would cut the opener link between a tab that escaped an app's sandbox and the app (Chromium already gives a `target="_blank"` link no opener; `window.open` keeps one, though the app cannot read the tab: `platform/web/app-sign-in-popup.test.ts`). Left for Franck to decide.
- **(3.24) Writer in the data API's log (try 2, access review L2; please veto):** a `PUT` or `DELETE` whose token passed the check logs the writer's nickname (`writer`, a public name the value shows with `?info=1` anyway) whatever the outcome after the check (saved, deleted, deleting a missing key, 413, 400, 415, 409, 429, 500), so every write and delete is traceable to a participant; reads and refusals before or at the token check log none. Never the app's nickname, the key, the value, the token, its hash or an email (tests in `data.test.ts` and `handlers/app-data.test.ts`). With the time of the log line, that answers "who changed or deleted this app's data, and when"; which app is not in the line (as since 2.4), so tracing a deleted value needs the time and the writer.
- **(3.24) Cost:** a write adds one consistent GetItem of a small token item (1 read unit); a token costs one read and a two-item transaction. No new service; the worst case of "(3.17) Remaining worst-case cost" falls, as strangers' writes now stop at 401 before the totals are read.
- **(3.24) Deploy order:** saves from apps already live answer 401 until 3.25's `workshop-data.js` is published (`npm run apps -- refresh`); reads keep working. Merge 3.24 with 3.25, or 3.25 right after.
- **(3.25) whoAmI() from the server (try 2, access review finding A; please veto):** the hand-off address is exactly `/apps/<nick>/#wt=<token>` again, as 3.24's criterion says. Try 1 also put `&wn=<nickname>` there; nobody checked it, so a signed-in participant could edit their own hand-off address to `wn=Ada`, the app said "You save as Ada.", and an app that stores `whoAmI()` as an item's author recorded "Ada". Now `whoAmI()` comes from the data API: `GET /api/apps/<nick>/data/<any valid key>?whoami=1` with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` answers 200 `{"nickname": "<the token's writer>"}` for a valid token for this app and 401 (the sign-in text) for no token, a malformed, unknown (revoked) or expired one, or another app's; any other `whoami` value is a plain read. The usual order holds first (404 not live, 400 bad key). It is the same `checkAppWrite` as a write and reads only the live marker and the token item (the data function's existing projected GetItem of `APPTOKEN#*` `app`, `nickname`, `expiresAt`: no IAM change), reads no value, writes nothing, counts nothing towards the budget, and logs `outcome: "whoami"` with no writer, token, hash or email (tests in `data.test.ts`). No new route, throttle or CloudFront behaviour (a query flag like `?info=1`). A GET with `Authorization` is preflighted: the function's preflight and the `ssd-workshop-app-data-v2` policy already allow the header (proved with the real handler in Chromium, `browser.test.ts`). `workshop-data.js` asks once per page load when it holds a token (key `whoami`); `whoAmI()` returns a promise (`await workshopData.whoAmI()`), null for a visitor, if the answer is not 200 and after a refused save. A 401 to that question keeps the token: if CloudFront drops the header on GETs, saving still works and only the name is missing; a refused save then signs the visitor out as before. Cost: one Lambda call and one 1-unit read per page load with a token. Unverified until the live check in `infra/README.md`: that CloudFront forwards `Authorization` on this GET (AWS documents it for caching off with the AllViewerExceptHostHeader policy, which `/api/*` uses). The `PUT` answer stays 204 (`updatedBy` is read with `?info=1`).
- **(3.25) The sign-in element (please veto):** any element with the attribute `data-workshop-sign-in`; the template and AGENTS.md use `<button class="sign-in" type="button" data-workshop-sign-in hidden>Sign in to save</button>` on a line of its own right after the saving form or button (the first live eval had one app scroll sideways by 18 px; a line of its own cannot widen a row). `workshop-data.js` shows such elements while the visitor holds no token and hides them once they do, fills an empty one with "Sign in to save", and opens `/app-sign-in/?app=<nick>` with `window.open(..., "_blank", "noopener")` on a click (links get the same `href`, `target="_blank"` and `rel="noopener"`). A 401 on a write clears the token and the nickname and shows them again; a page with none (an app built before 3.25) gets a button of its own, fixed at the bottom right, styled through the CSSOM (no `style` attribute, which the CSP would refuse). Reads never send the token.
- **(3.25) Hand-off removal:** `workshop-data.js` reads `wt` from the `&`-separated fragment as soon as it runs (try 2: the first script of `index.html`, not deferred, so before any of the app's code), removes it with `history.replaceState` (falling back to `location.replace` of the fragment), and keeps any other part (`#wt=...&screen=list` becomes `#screen=list`). A malformed token is removed and ignored (a visitor). Proved in Chromium under the real apps CSP (`platform/api/build/template.test.ts`).
- **(3.25) Who posted (rewritten in try 2):** `workshopData.getInfo(key)` reads `?info=1` and answers `{value, updatedBy, updatedAt}` (each `null` when unknown); `updatedBy` is recorded by the server from the token. The template's counter shows "Last press by <updatedBy>." and "You save as <await whoAmI()>.". AGENTS.md rule 6 shows the nickname only and says who posted comes from `getInfo(key).updatedBy`; a name an app stores inside a value is written by the app, and any signed-in participant could save any text there, so if an app stores one it must use the confirmed `await whoAmI()` (skipping it when null), and the rule prefers one key per item with `updatedBy`.
- **(3.25) Apps stay read-only for visitors:** AGENTS.md rule 6 forbids saving when the page opens or on a timer. The build eval's in-memory API refuses every write with 401 (no visitor has a token), so such an app fails its console check (Chromium logs the 401). The eval also requires at least one `data-workshop-sign-in` element reading "Sign in" that a visitor sees, and when one is really visible as the app opens, pressing the first one must open `/app-sign-in/?app=<nick>` in a new tab. Try 2 (checker, finding D; please veto): "sees" no longer means only "lacks its own `hidden`": the element must not be `display: none`, `visibility: hidden` or transparent itself, and every ancestor that hides it must do so with the `hidden` attribute only (a screen, or a result panel the app shows after an action, AGENTS.md rule 2), never with CSS (`display: none` without the attribute, opacity 0); "really visible" is `checkVisibility` with opacity and visibility and a box larger than 0 (`SIGN_IN_SCRIPT` in `platform/src/eval/logic.ts`, tested in Chromium). Not built as the finding put it (only buttons really visible when the app opens): the first try-2 live eval failed 3 of 4 correct apps that way, whose sign-in button sits, as AGENTS.md asks, next to a save button inside a result panel or screen shown after a search or a tap (`hidden` parent); an earlier try-1 app checked with the new script gives shown 1, visible 0. Try 3 (please veto): the ancestor rule is dropped. Live evals on try 2's rule failed correct apps whose screens switch with a CSS class: the fitness example's sign-in button sat in `<section class="screen hidden">` with `.screen.hidden { display: none }` (evidence: the published files of `EVAL_DUMP_DIR`, below), visible as soon as the visitor opens that screen. Now "sees" means the element reads "Sign in" and hides itself in no way: not its own `hidden`, nor `display: none`, `visibility: hidden` (its own, not inherited) or opacity 0 from its own styles, however an ancestor hides it. An app that hides the button itself, or never shows its screen, can still pass; the click-through still presses the first one really visible on load, when there is one.
- **(3.25) The platform's workshop-data.js in every app (question 10):** the deployer bundles `templates/basic/workshop-data.js` next to its code (`afterBundling`, synth test compares the bytes) and replaces the repository's `workshop-data.js` (none, any copy, a folder of that name with its contents) before the deploy check. The builder's prompts repeat the sign-in rule (`SIGN_IN_GUIDANCE`), since a change build works on a repository whose AGENTS.md and workshop-data.js may predate 3.25; the repository's own copies are not rewritten (only the published file changes).
- **(3.25) Flex rows on phones (please veto):** AGENTS.md rule 9 now says a field and a button side by side in a flex row need `flex: 1` and `min-width: 0` on the field. The Wikipedia watchlist example scrolled sideways (16 to 34 px, a search field beside its button) in 4 of 4 live builds, on `main` before this ticket too (one baseline run, 16 px); with the rule, 0 of 5. The builder's prompts give the sign-in rule after the WebFetch lines (placed before them, 3 runs of that example in a row skipped WebFetch, which its check requires). Live eval after both: `npm run eval -- build` 4/4, $1.08.
- **(3.25) `npm run apps -- refresh` (please veto):** only the deployer may write app files (bucket policy `OnlyTheDeployerWritesApps`, which refuses Franck too), so the command (Franck, `AWS_PROFILE=franck`) lists the live markers (`APP#*` / `LIVE`, a Scan with a projection of `nickname`, like `npm run builds -- list`) and sends one `{"refresh": "<nickname>"}` per app to the deploy queue, in the app's message group (behind any deploy of that app already queued), deduplication id `refresh-<nick>-<ms>`. The deployer puts only `workshop-data.js` into an app whose `index.html` is under its prefix and invalidates that one path; no record changes, no download. No new IAM right: the deployer's role already lists, puts and invalidates there, and Franck's own credentials already send to the deploy queue (the boundary's `DeploysFromTheBuilderOnly` binds workload roles, not Franck). The builder task, the queue's only workload sender, could also send a refresh; it only re-puts the platform's own file. A refresh that fails three times goes to the dead-letter queue, which the launcher's check drains and ignores (not a deploy message): run the command again. Try 2 (access review finding C): a refresh does nothing (no put, no invalidation; logged `up-to-date`) when the published `apps/<nick>/workshop-data.js` already has the bundled bytes: the ETag (the MD5 of a one-part upload under the bucket's S3-managed encryption) from the ListObjectsV2 the refresh already makes equals the MD5 of the bundled file. A deploy publishes the same bytes, so a refresh after it does nothing either. Worst case (correcting try 1, which said only "it only re-puts the platform's own file"): the builder task role can still send any number of refresh messages while it runs; each costs an SQS FIFO message, a Lambda call and one ListObjectsV2 (about $7 per million messages in all), and an invalidation path only the first time per app after `workshop-data.js` changes (CloudFront: 1,000 paths a month free, then $0.005 each), so at most one per live app and change of the file, about $0.15 for 30 apps.
- **(3.25) workshop-data.js runs first (try 2, access review finding B; please veto):** app code (`app.js`, or a script that `index.html` runs before `workshop-data.js`) could wrap `fetch` or `history.replaceState`, or read `location.hash`, and send the visitor's token away (`connect-src https:`). Built: (1) `workshop-data.js` takes `fetch`, `history.replaceState`, `location.replace` and `window.open` (with `.bind`) and the address at its very top, uses only those, and passes `fetch` its options and headers in objects without a prototype (`Object.create(null)`: `fetch` reads `signal`, `referrer`, ... and `Symbol.iterator` through the prototype chain, where a getter on `Object.prototype` would see the headers); (2) the deploy check (`checkApp`, so the builder's own check, the deployer and the eval alike) refuses an `index.html` that holds a `<script` anywhere unless it starts, after only white space, the doctype, `<html>`, `<head>`, `<meta>`, `<link>` and a plain `<title>`, with `<script src="workshop-data.js"></script>` (or `./workshop-data.js`, any quotes, no other attribute: no `defer`, `async` or `type`), so it runs while the page is read, before any other script, deferred or not. The check is strict on purpose (no comment, quoted `<`/`>`, `<base>`, SVG or raw-text element before it) so the browser cannot read the page differently; its reason: "index.html must load workshop-data.js before any other script: put <script src="workshop-data.js"></script> in <head>, with no defer or async, before every other <script> (only <meta>, <link> and <title> may come before it). ...". A page without any script passes. The template's `index.html` now loads it that way (it waits for `DOMContentLoaded` to show the sign-in elements); AGENTS.md ("The files", "The deploy check") and the builder's prompts (`SIGN_IN_GUIDANCE`) say so, the prompts also for an older app's `defer` line. Apps live before try 2 keep their `index.html` (`defer`, still first) after `npm run apps -- refresh`; their next change build must move the line, or its check fails. Proved in Chromium (`template.test.ts`): code in `app.js` wrapping `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `Headers`, `History.prototype.replaceState`, `JSON.stringify` and getters on `Object.prototype` sees no token while the save carries it; the same code run before `workshop-data.js` sees it (control). Access review of try 3 (Medium, fixed before the pull request): `nativeFetch` resolves a relative address against the page's base URL when it is called, so app code adding `<base href="https://other.example/">` after the client had run could send the next save, with its token, to another server, and point the sign-in button at a fake page. `workshop-data.js` now builds every address (data, `?whoami=1`, sign-in) absolute from the page's own server as read at its start (`startServer`); proved in Chromium (`template.test.ts`, the `<base>` test fails without the fix). A `base-uri` directive in the apps' CSP would also do, but needs a new CloudFront policy version; not added (please veto).
- **(3.25) Syntax check while building (try 3, please veto):** the builder's PostToolUse hook compiles every `.js` file the agent writes or edits as a classic script (`vm.Script`, never run; `scriptSyntaxProblem` in `platform/builder/agent.ts`) and, on a syntax error, tells the agent the file, line and message as the tool's additional context, so it fixes the file before finishing. Why: live evals on this branch left the app's script unparseable 4 times (try 1, one `--example 4` run: "Unexpected string"; try 2, runs 1 and 3: "Unexpected string", "missing ) after argument list"; try 3, run 1: "Unexpected string"; the one baseline run on `main` built one app and saw none, so how often `main` does it is unmeasured), which the deploy check does not see (it scans text, it does not parse JavaScript) and which leaves the whole app dead in the browser; try 3's first run kept the published files: line 77 of the Wikipedia example's app.js read `"No Wikipedia article found for "" + query + "". ..."` (double quotes inside a double-quoted string, "Unexpected string"), the agent's own text and not this ticket's loading order, which cannot make a file fail to parse; `'the workshop's sign-in'` gives the other message. Not a deploy check rule (no change to what the deployer refuses, so no spec delta); nothing blocks a build that still ends with an error. Cost: none (local parse, milliseconds).
- **(3.25) `EVAL_DUMP_DIR` (try 3, please veto):** `EVAL_DUMP_DIR=<absolute folder> npm run eval -- build` also writes each example's published files to a new folder under it (printed in the log), to look at a failed app afterwards; the example specs hold no personal data. Off by default.
- **(3.25) Residual: app code can reach the visitor's token (accepted by Franck 2026-11-26, "Accept, with the mitigations"):** the token is in the same page as the app's own code, so the mitigations above remove the easy routes, not the risk. What remains: (a) Chromium keeps the address the page was opened with, fragment and token included, in the navigation timing entry after `replaceState` (`performance.getEntriesByType("navigation")[0].name`, also through `PerformanceObserver`), readable by any script of the page and impossible to clear; found while building the mitigations, after Franck's answer, and proved in `template.test.ts`; hiding it by overriding `Performance.prototype` is not done, since a same-origin `about:blank` frame gives the app a fresh copy; (b) an app can save anything as the visitor through `workshopData.set`/`remove` while the visitor holds the token (by design: that is the app saving; scoped to this app, 12 hours at most, ended by sign-out); (c) apps live before try 2 run `workshop-data.js` deferred until their next build. With the token an app could send it elsewhere, and whoever receives it can save in that one app as that visitor until it expires or they sign out (no other app, no reads beyond the public ones, no workshop session). The full fix is a design change: the data client in its own origin or frame (for example a page under another path that the app talks to with `postMessage` and that alone holds the token), so the token never enters the app's page or address. Not built.
- **(3.18) Bucket name (please veto):** `ssd-workshop-app-media-<account>` (`APP_MEDIA_BUCKET_NAME`), not the brief's bare `ssd-workshop-app-media`: S3 names are global, and the site and apps buckets carry the account the same way. It lives in the apps stack (eu-central-1, beside the apps bucket); the edge stack imports it by name, as it does the apps bucket.
- **(3.18) Ids chosen by the app (please veto):** the route is `PUT /api/apps/{nickname}/media/{id}`, so the id is in the address: exactly 22 characters of `A-Z a-z 0-9 _ -` (16 random bytes in base64url, which 3.19's `workshop-data.js` makes), checked before anything else is read (400 otherwise, so no `/`, `..` or `%` ever reaches a key). Create-only: an id already used answers 409 and nothing is replaced, so an address always serves the bytes it first served (browsers and CloudFront cache by address). Since try 2 a `DELETE` keeps the record as a tombstone (status `deleted`, out of the app's bytes and files), and since try 3 so does an upload that failed (the S3 put failed, or the app stopped being live before it finished: its room and file count are given back, its record turns `deleted`), so a later `PUT` of that id answers 409 too and an address never serves other bytes than a cache may still hold; the media function's role has no `DeleteItem` any more; only `npm run participant -- reset` deletes tombstones (with the app's other records). Files are stored at `app-media/<lower-cased nickname>/<id>` and served at `https://workshop.rankly.games/app-media/<lower-cased nickname>/<id>`.
- **(3.18) Answer (please veto):** `PUT` answers 201 `{"address", "contentType", "bytes"}` (the stored size, after metadata removal). `DELETE` answers 204, also for an id that does not exist or was already deleted (no bucket call then); an upload still in progress (younger than 120 s) answers 409. Refusals: 400 bad id or no body, 401 no or wrong token (after the live check, as in "(3.24) Data API order"; since try 2 a `PUT`/`DELETE` without `Authorization: Bearer ...` gets the same 401 and message from WAF, see "(3.18) WAF"), 404 app not live, 409 id taken (or deleted) or too many lost races, 413 file over 4 MB / recording over 5:05 / app over 500 MB / 2,000 files, 415 not an accepted format or a recording whose length cannot be read, 429 daily budget spent (or, try 3, WAF's 429 "Too many uploads from this address. Please wait a few minutes and try again." after 150 uploads and deletes from one address in 5 minutes, or 300 preflights, see "(3.18) WAF"). Every answer and the preflight carry `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (the preflight allows `PUT, DELETE` and `content-type, accept, authorization`, like the data API's).
- **(3.18) Formats (please veto):** judged from the bytes only, never the declared type. JPEG: `FF D8 FF`, a frame with a size, a scan and an end marker. WebM: EBML with DocType `webm` and only audio tracks with codec `A_OPUS`. Ogg: every stream's first page starts with `OpusHead`. MP4: `ftyp` first, a `moov` whose every track is a sound track (`soun`) with `mp4a` or `Opus` samples (MP4/Opus is accepted too: some browsers record it). Anything else (PNG, GIF, SVG, HTML, script, PDF, Matroska, Vorbis, Theora, any video track, a cut file) is 415. Stored types are fixed per format: `image/jpeg`, `audio/webm`, `audio/ogg`, `audio/mp4`.
- **(3.18) Five minutes (try 2; please veto the margin and the status codes):** Franck's answer (recordings at most 5 minutes) is enforced by the server, from the file's own timing (`recordingSeconds`, `platform/api/app-media/formats.ts`): WebM, the larger of Info's Duration and the last block's time (Cluster Timecode plus the block's relative timecode; MediaRecorder writes no Duration); Ogg/Opus, the last granule position less the pre-skip, at 48 kHz; MP4, the largest of mvhd, mdhd, mehd, the sample table (`stts`) and the fragments' ends (`tfdt` plus the `trun` sample durations, or `tfhd`'s or `trex`'s default; Chromium's and Safari's MediaRecorder write fragmented MP4). A margin of 5 s (`RECORDING_MARGIN_SECONDS`): over 5:05 answers **413** "This recording is too long: a recording can be at most 5 minutes." (413 like the other limits the app shows); a recording whose length cannot be read (no block, no audio page, no duration anywhere, a cut file) answers **415** "The length of this recording could not be read. Record it again, or send it as WebM, Ogg or MP4 audio." Every walk is one bounded pass (linear, tested with 4 MB of tiny elements, huge sample counts and random bytes). Proved on Chromium's real MediaRecorder WebM and MP4 (`browser.test.ts`) and on crafted files at 5:04 and 5:06. A crafted file can still declare timings shorter than its audio; the 4 MB cap bounds that (at most ~4 minutes of AAC at 128 kbit/s or far more Opus).
- **(3.18) Metadata removed from photos (please veto):** an allow-list since try 2: the picture's own segments (SOFn, DHT, DAC, DQT, DNL, DRI, SOS, RSTn, SOI, EOI) and three metadata segments, each only by its identifier (try 3): APP0 only when its data starts `JFIF\0` (a JFXX APP0, which holds a thumbnail, or any other APP0 goes), APP2 only when it starts `ICC_PROFILE\0` (colours) and APP14 only when it starts `Adobe` (colour transform); everything else goes: Exif (APP1, location), XMP (APP1), IPTC (APP13), MPF (APP2), every other APPn, comments (COM), JPG and JPGn (C8, F0 to FD), DHP, EXP, TEM and reserved markers, plus anything after the end marker. The picture data is copied as is (no re-encoding); a photo with nothing to remove is stored byte for byte. Since try 3 the walk notes the kept byte ranges (touching ones merged) and copies them once into a buffer of the final size, and the photo is stripped once (while its format is checked): 4 MB of restart markers or tiny segments takes ~60 ms and no extra memory to speak of (was ~1.1 s and ~550 MB, above the function's 512 MB); the WebM and MP4 walks likewise visit elements and boxes one at a time without building lists (`formats.test.ts` runs each hostile file in a child process with a 32 MB heap). Proved on hand-made fixtures and on a JPEG Chromium encodes (decodes to the same size and pixels, `browser.test.ts`). Removing APP1 also removes the Exif orientation tag, so a camera file sent as is (sideways pixels plus "rotate") shows turned; 3.19's in-browser resize draws the photo through a canvas, which applies the orientation first, so template uploads are upright (`infra/README.md`).
- **(3.18) Budget counting (please veto):** the app's room (500 MB, 2,000 files) and the day's upload budget (500 MB, UTC day, only moving forward) are counted in one item (`APPMEDIA#<nick>` / `MEDIA_USAGE`), reserved in the same transaction that creates the file's record (`uploading`), before the S3 put; a lost race reads again (20 to 80 ms pause, 4 tries, then 409), so parallel uploads never pass a limit (tested with a burst). Each upload counts at least 256 KB against the day's budget (`MIN_UPLOAD_CHARGE_BYTES`), so at most 2,000 uploads a day per app whatever their size (cost cap on requests); deletes give back room and files, never budget. A failed put first deletes the key (best effort: a put cut off after S3 took the bytes may have stored it; since try 2), then gives the reservation back (since try 3 the record becomes a `deleted` tombstone, so the id is never reused: a retry needs a new id, which 3.19's client makes).
- **(3.18) Metadata in recordings (try 2; please veto):** blanked in place where it is cheap and keeps the file intact: MP4 `udta` and `meta` boxes (top level, in `moov` and in each `trak`) become `free` boxes of zeros of the same size (a plain MP4's sample offsets point into the file, so nothing may move), and WebM's Tags and Info's Title become Void elements of the same size. The size, the audio and the measured length stay the same (tested on fixtures and on Chromium's recordings). Ogg comments (OpusTags) are stored as received: changing them means recomputing page checksums, and Firefox (the recorder that writes Ogg) puts only an encoder name there. MediaRecorder writes no location; the case this covers is a phone's own recorder file (iPhone's Voice Memos names a recording after the place).
- **(3.18) An upload finishing after a reset (please veto):** the record turns `stored` only in a transaction that also checks the app's live marker; when it is gone (a reset, or the app taken down), the function deletes the file it just stored and gives the reservation back, answering 404. DELETE removes the file first, then turns the record into a `deleted` tombstone (a retry repeats both; a tombstone answers 204).
- **(3.18) Caching (try 2; please veto):** each file is stored with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400, s-maxage=3600` (was a year in browsers with `immutable`, a day in CloudFront): a photo of a person or a private paper deleted by mistake stops being served from CloudFront within an hour, without an invalidation per delete (an invalidation per API call could be driven by anyone with a token: $0.005 a path beyond 1,000 a month); browsers that loaded it keep it up to a day. The cost: each browser asks CloudFront again after a day, and CloudFront asks S3 again after an hour (an S3 GET, $0.43 a million). `npm run participant -- reset --yes` still invalidates `/app-media/<nick>/*` (one wildcard path).
- **(3.18) Media headers (please veto):** behaviour `/app-media/*` (after `/apps/*`, never matching an `/api/` path): managed CachingOptimized, GET and HEAD, HTTPS redirect, origin access control `ssd-workshop-app-media`, and a new response headers policy `ssd-workshop-app-media-v1` (created, never updated, retained): nosniff, HSTS one year, `strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, and `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`, so a file opened on its own can run nothing. No CORS (apps show media with `<img>` and `<audio>`, which need none). A missing file answers the 404 page (the distribution's 403 mapping).
- **(3.18) Apps' CSP unchanged:** `img-src 'self' data: https:` covers photos, and `media-src` falls back to `default-src 'self'`, whose `'self'` is the app's served address (`https://workshop.rankly.games`) even in the opaque sandbox: proved in Chromium with the real `APPS_CONTENT_SECURITY_POLICY` on the page and the media headers on the files (`browser.test.ts`: the photo draws at 64 x 48, the recording loads, no CSP violation). No new apps headers policy version.
- **(3.18) Who may write the bucket (please veto):** the bucket policy denies object writes (`PutObject` and its ACL, tagging, retention, legal hold, restore and replicate variants) to every principal but the media function's role (`ArnNotEquals aws:PrincipalArn`, with and without its path), Franck and the deploy role included. Deletes are not in that deny, so `npm run participant -- reset` (Franck's credentials) can remove an app's media; a synth test fails when any IAM policy but the media role's may write or delete there, or when any may change the bucket's policy, lifecycle, website, CORS or public access block.
- **(3.18) WAF (try 2, try 3; please veto; after the access review of try 3: rule 3 counts `PUT` and `DELETE`, the media path matches add `NORMALIZE_PATH` after `URL_DECODE` so `/api//apps/...` cannot skip them, and a fifth rule counts media preflights per address, so one address cannot hold every app's preflights at 429 while the preflight throttle stays 20/s, burst 40):** four rules added to the 3.13 web ACL `ssd-workshop-edge`. (1) `ssd-workshop-media-rate` (priority 1): 3,000 requests per address to `/app-media/*` in 5 minutes (was 6,000; cost limits accepted by Franck 2026-11-26), then 429. This is a separate rule, which differs from Franck's answer to the brief's question 3 ("extend the existing WAF per-address rate rule (3.13) to `/app-media/*`"): extending it would count photo downloads against the API's 15,000, sized for a whole room's polling, so a room scrolling a gallery behind one venue address would get 429 on every API call (sign-in, saving, the steps' polling) for up to 5 minutes; a separate rule keeps the two budgets apart for $1 a month more, and gives media its own, lower limit. (2) `ssd-workshop-media-auth` (priority 2): a `PUT` or `DELETE` whose path starts `/api/apps/` and holds `/media/` and whose `authorization` header does not start with `Bearer ` is answered by WAF with the API's own 401 body ("Sign in to the workshop to save in this app.") and `access-control-allow-origin: *` (a custom response), never reaching API Gateway or Lambda (not verified until the live check: whether the `/api/apps/*` response headers policy also applies its CORS header to a WAF answer; its `originOverride` would replace it, not double it). A junk filter only: a made-up `Bearer x` passes and is refused by the function. Since try 3 the header is compared after a `LOWERCASE` transformation with `bearer `, as `checkAppWrite` reads the scheme in any case (`BEARER x` reaches the function, which answers 401 itself). (3) `ssd-workshop-media-write-rate` (try 3, priority 3; please veto the limit and the answer): per address, at most 150 media uploads and deletes together (`PUT` or `DELETE` on a path starting `/api/apps/` and holding `/media/`, the rate rule's scope-down statement; `DELETE` since the access review of try 3) in 5 minutes, then WAF answers 429 with `access-control-allow-origin: *` and `{"message":"Too many uploads from this address. Please wait a few minutes and try again."}` (a 429 rather than a 401: the caller may hold a real token and just be fast). Without it, one address with a made-up token kept the upload route's shared 2/s throttle at 429 for every app; 150 per 5 minutes is 0.5/s, so that now takes at least 4 addresses. A room behind one venue address shares the 150 (~3 uploads each for 45 people in 5 minutes; 3.19's template uploads one photo or recording per press). $1 a month. A separate rule rather than a statement inside the rate rule (a rate rule's scope-down covers one path, `/app-media/*`), $1 a month. (4) `ssd-workshop-media-preflight-rate` (the web ACL's fifth rule, after the access review of try 3, priority 4; please veto the limit and the approach): per address, at most 300 media preflights (`OPTIONS` on the same media path matches as rule 3) in 5 minutes, then the same 429, CORS header and body as rule 3. The preflight route's throttle (20/s, burst 40) is shared by every app and the 3.13 rule lets one address send 50/s, so without it one address could hold every app's preflights at 429. 300 per 5 minutes is 1/s, so filling the 20/s throttle takes ~20 addresses; a room behind one venue address sends one preflight per upload (at most rule 3's 150 in 5 minutes), far below 300. A per-address rule rather than a higher throttle (a first fix raised it to 60/s, burst 120): raising a throttle raises the worst-case cost at the cap (~$12 a day instead of ~$4), beyond what Franck accepted. $1 a month. Rights: `wafv2:UpdateWebACL` on `ssd-workshop-edge` is already held by the deploy role through the standalone policy `ssd-workshop-deploy-safety-nets` (ticket 3.13, statement `WafManageWorkshopAcl`, on `global/webacl/ssd-workshop-*/*`), so no access-stack change is needed; Franck only confirms that policy is still attached before merging.
- **(3.18) Function and throttles (please veto):** `ssd-workshop-api-app-media`, 512 MB, 25 s (S3 client: 2 attempts of 1 s connect + 8 s request); route throttles PUT 2/s (burst 10; was 5/s burst 20, cost limits accepted by Franck 2026-11-26: a room of 45 taking a photo each at once passes in ~20 s), DELETE 10/s (burst 20), OPTIONS 20/s (burst 40; not raised after the access review of try 3: WAF's `ssd-workshop-media-preflight-rate` keeps one address from holding it at 429, see "(3.18) WAF"). IAM: projected GetItem in `APP#*` (`liveAt`), `APPTOKEN#*` (app, nickname, expiresAt) and `APPMEDIA#*`; ConditionCheckItem in `APP#*` (`pk`, `sk`, `liveAt`); Put/Update in `APPMEDIA#*` (no Delete since try 3: every end of a record is a tombstone) naming only `APP_MEDIA_WRITE_ATTRIBUTES` (pk and sk included); `s3:PutObject` and `s3:DeleteObject` on `app-media/*` of the media bucket only.
- **(3.18) Messages (try 3; please veto):** the `message` of each media API answer (`APP_MEDIA_MESSAGES`, `platform/api/app-media/media.ts`), which apps show their visitors: 404 "Not found."; 401 "Sign in to the workshop to save in this app." (the 3.24 text, also WAF's answer); 400 "A photo or recording id has 22 letters, digits, dashes or underscores."; 400 "Send the photo or recording as the request's body."; 413 "This file is too big: a photo or recording can be at most 4 MB."; 415 "Send a photo as a JPEG, or a voice recording as WebM, Ogg or MP4 audio."; 413 "This recording is too long: a recording can be at most 5 minutes."; 415 "The length of this recording could not be read. Record it again, or send it as WebM, Ogg or MP4 audio."; 413 "This app has no room left for photos and recordings: an app can keep at most 500 MB."; 413 "This app already keeps 2,000 photos and recordings, the most it can keep. Delete one to add another."; 429 "This app has uploaded a lot today. Uploading starts again tomorrow."; 409 "A photo or recording with this id already exists. Use a new id."; 409 "This photo or recording is still being uploaded. Please try again in a moment."; 409 "This app's photos and recordings were being changed by someone else at the same moment. Please try again."; 500 "The media service had a problem. Please try again in a moment."; and WAF's 429 "Too many uploads from this address. Please wait a few minutes and try again." (try 3; also the fifth WAF rule's answer to too many preflights).
- **(3.18) Logs (please veto):** one JSON line per request: method, outcome, status, size and kind, and the writer's nickname once the token passed (as in the data API); never the token, its hash, the app's nickname, the id, an address or an error's message (only its name).
- **(3.18) Reset (please veto):** the dry run's "App:" line also counts the app's files in the bucket ("2 photos and recordings"; tombstones are not counted); `--yes` deletes the files and their records (tombstones included) in both passes around the existing 2 s pause, then (try 2) prints "Waiting 30 s for uploads already under way, then deleting the app's photos and recordings once more.", waits `MEDIA_SETTLE_MS` = 30 s (longer than the media function's 25 s timeout, plus 5 s for a put S3 was still taking) and lists and deletes the media once more, so an upload cut off mid-flight before it could clean up is still removed; then it invalidates `/app-media/<nick>/*` and prints "Deleted N photos and recordings; CloudFront stops serving the app's media within minutes."
- **(3.18) Cost, upload worst case with the try 2 limits (downloads, part 3 below, about $290 a day per abusing address at the 3,000 per 5 minutes Franck accepted 19:35 UTC; accepted by Franck 2026-11-26; corrected figure accepted by Franck 2026-11-26 20:01 UTC: about $24 a day while abuse lasts, ~$730 a month if sustained, bound by the throttle on refused 4 MB uploads including CloudFront-to-origin transfer, with the try 3 rule of 150 uploads per address in 5 minutes):** eu-central-1 and CloudFront Europe prices: API Gateway HTTP API $1.20 a million 512 KB units, Lambda arm64 $0.0000133 per GB-second, S3 PUT $0.0054 per 1,000, DynamoDB on demand $0.7625 a million write units, CloudFront $0.085/GB to viewers, $0.02/GB to the origin, $0.012 per 10,000 requests, WAF $0.60 a million requests and $1 a rule a month. Storage: at most 500 MB per app, ~45 apps = 22.5 GB, ~$0.55 a month. (1) Token holders (any signed-in participant can get a token for any live app): every live app driven to its 2,000 uploads and 500 MB a day, 90,000 uploads and 22.5 GB a day for 45 apps (under the 2/s throttle's 172,800): API Gateway ~90,000 units $0.11, Lambda at ~0.5 s and 512 MB $0.30, S3 PUTs $0.49, DynamoDB ~10 write and 4 read units an upload $0.75, CloudFront bodies to the origin (30 GB as base64) $0.60 and requests $0.11: ~$2.40 a day; then the same holder can keep sending refused 4 MB uploads (429) at the 2/s throttle: 172,800 a day x ~5.6 MB of base64 = ~970 GB to the origin $19.40, API Gateway 11 units each $2.30, Lambda and reads ~$0.30: ~$22 a day. In all ~$24 a day, ~$730 a month if sustained for a month. Since try 3 (`ssd-workshop-media-write-rate`, 150 uploads per address in 5 minutes = 0.5/s) one address reaches at most a quarter of that throttle: ~43,200 uploads a day, ~$5.50 a day of refused uploads; the ~$24 a day needs at least 4 addresses (2/s / 0.5/s). (2) Anonymous callers: a media `PUT`/`DELETE` without `Bearer ` (any case) stops at WAF ($0.60 WAF plus $1.20 CloudFront per million); with a made-up `Bearer x` it reaches the function and costs as the refused uploads above: ~$5.50 a day per address, at most the shared 2/s throttle's ~$22 a day with 4 addresses or more (it also delays real uploads while it lasts; one address can no longer hold the throttle). The throttle does not bound what CloudFront sends to the origin before API Gateway refuses: that transfer ($0.02/GB) is shared by every `/api/*` route since ticket 1.2, bounded per address only by the 3.13 rule (15,000 requests per 5 minutes) and API Gateway's 10 MB body limit, up to ~150 GB per 5 minutes, ~$3 per 5 minutes per address; 3.18 does not change it (not verified: whether CloudFront keeps sending a body after API Gateway's 429). (3) Downloads: at the WAF limit of 3,000 per address per 5 minutes and the 4 MB file size, 12 GB per 5 minutes, ~$1 per 5 minutes, ~$290 a day per abusing address (cache hits; S3 GETs at most one per file and hour); a distributed flood stays the risk accepted under "Abuse cost". The four new WAF rules add $4 a month (the fifth rule, `ssd-workshop-media-preflight-rate`, is $1 of it; the preflight throttle stays 20/s, so the preflight cost at the cap is unchanged). Safeguards as before: the $20 Budgets alert and the Cost Anomaly email; the levers are `WAF_MEDIA_RATE_LIMIT`, `WAF_MEDIA_WRITE_RATE_LIMIT`, `WAF_MEDIA_PREFLIGHT_RATE_LIMIT`, `APP_MEDIA_UPLOAD_THROTTLE` and `MAX_MEDIA_FILE_BYTES`.
- **(3.19) Microphone in the apps' sandbox (finding):** Chromium 158 refuses `getUserMedia` in a page under the apps' CSP with `SecurityError: Invalid security origin`, with a fake microphone and the permission prompt answered yes; the same page without `sandbox` records (`platform/api/build/template-media.test.ts`, "finding" test and its control). The apps send no `Permissions-Policy` header, and none would help: the refusal comes from the opaque origin. No apps headers policy change. So Chrome (desktop and Android) records through the fallback, `<input type="file" accept="audio/*" capture>` (the phone's own recorder, or a file chooser); Safari on iPhone and Chrome on Android are checked live by Franck (`infra/README.md`, "Live check of photos and voice recordings on phones"). Adding `allow-same-origin` to the sandbox would let the microphone work but give apps the platform's origin: not considered.
- **(3.19) recordVoice() shape (please veto):** `const recording = await workshopData.recordVoice()` (called from a click), then `recording.stop()` and `await recording.done` (the address; `stop()` also returns it). `recording.microphone` is `true` for MediaRecorder, `false` for the fallback, so an app can word its status; one Record/Stop button works for both (in the fallback, Stop or the input's `cancel` event ends it with "No recording was chosen."). It tries `getUserMedia` first and opens the file input when that fails for any reason (refused, no microphone, the visitor said no); the input is clicked after the refusal, inside the tap's user activation in Chromium (tested). Not verified: whether Safari still counts the tap then (step 2 of the live check).
- **(3.19) Recording settings (please veto):** MediaRecorder with the first supported of `audio/webm;codecs=opus`, `audio/mp4`, `audio/ogg;codecs=opus` (else the browser's default) at 64 kbit/s, so 5 minutes is ~2.4 MB, under the 4 MB cap (Chromium's default 128 kbit/s would make ~4.8 MB); it stops itself after 5:00 (`setTimeout`, tested with Playwright's clock; the server allows 5:05).
- **(3.19) savePhoto() (please veto):** `createImageBitmap(file)` (which turns the photo the way the camera held it, before the media API drops Exif) drawn on an `OffscreenCanvas` (a hidden canvas element where there is none or it has no 2D context) on white (a transparent PNG would otherwise turn black), at most 2,560 px on the long side, encoded as JPEG at quality 0.85, then 0.7 and 0.5 while over 4 MB. No `blob:` or `data:` address is ever made (the apps' CSP refuses `blob:` images); the page shows the photo from the address the API answers. A file the browser cannot decode (HEIC in Chromium, a text file) gets "This browser cannot read this photo. Try another photo, or take it again with the camera." and nothing is sent; HEIC therefore depends on the browser (Safari decodes it; iPhones usually hand Chrome a JPEG anyway).
- **(3.19) Uploads (please veto):** each upload gets a new id (16 bytes from `crypto.getRandomValues`, base64url, 22 characters), a retry included, as 3.18's create-only ids need; `PUT` with `content-type: application/octet-stream` (infra/README.md 3.18 advice), the Bearer token and `accept: application/json`, to an address built from the page's server at start (3.25's `<base>` rule, tested). The answer's `address` must lie under `<server>/app-media/<nick>/` with a 22-character id, else "The workshop answered something unexpected. Please try again." A refusal shows the API's `message` (WAF's 429 too); 401 signs the visitor out and shows the sign-in button, as `set()` does; 404 shows the data API's "not live" text. Without a token, `savePhoto`, `recordVoice` and `deleteMedia` send nothing: they show the sign-in button and fail with "Sign in to the workshop to save in this app." at once (a visitor never records 5 minutes to learn that). A file over 4 MB (a long phone recording) fails before it is sent with the API's own "too big" text.
- **(3.19) Upload id guard (try 2, please veto):** `upload` checks the id `newMediaId()` made against the 22-character pattern before building the address (app code shares the page and could replace `btoa` or `String.prototype.replace`); a bad one sends nothing and fails with "Something on this page stopped the upload. Please reload the page and try again."
- **(3.19) deleteMedia(address) (please veto):** takes only an address under `<server>/app-media/<nick>/` with a 22-character id ("This is not the address of one of this app's photos or recordings." otherwise, nothing sent) and sends `DELETE /api/apps/<nick>/media/<id>` with the token.
- **(3.19) AGENTS.md and the builder's prompts (please veto):** AGENTS.md gains rules 14 (photos: a file input with `accept="image/*" capture="environment"` behind a label styled as a button, `savePhoto`, the address kept with the item's other fields, `<img loading="lazy">` thumbnails from the address, never `blob:`/`FileReader` previews, `deleteMedia`) and 15 (voice: the Record/Stop click handler shown in full, with a `starting` flag that ignores taps while `recordVoice()` still waits for the microphone, so a second tap cannot start a second recording (try 2, tested by running the example from AGENTS.md itself), `<audio controls preload="none">`); both say to put the sign-in button just after the photo or record button. The builder's prompts add `MEDIA_GUIDANCE` (the same in three lines, after the sign-in rule, which stays after the WebFetch lines), since a change build may work on an older AGENTS.md. 3.20 (the prompts that decide which ideas are ready) is not touched.
- **(3.19) Build eval media cases (please veto):** `platform/api/eval/build.json` holds a photo gallery ("Drawing Gallery", tags) and a voice-message app ("Voice Notes", topics), run after the four workshop examples (`--example 5` and `6`), not in the readiness file (the readiness prompt learns about photos only in 3.20). Besides the usual checks, each is opened signed in on an in-memory workshop whose media API runs the real format check and serves files back: the eval picks a generated JPEG in the first file input accepting images (or, after pressing the button reading "Record", a MediaRecorder WebM in the recorder Chromium opens), first opening the screen that holds it when the app hides it (a tab or button naming the screen's id, else reading Add/Take/Photo or Record; the Record button itself is never a tab, nav or `aria-controls` element), presses a Save/Add/Upload/Post/Send button next to it when nothing was sent within 3 s (empty text fields there get "cat"/"Ideas"), and requires an upload, a saved value holding the address, an `<img>`/`<audio>` showing that address (looked for again after reopening the app, as the next visitor, when the app redraws its list only when that screen opens), a tag (photo) or topic (voice) filter, and no console error. The filter (try 2, `HAS_FILTER` in `build.ts`) is a `<select>`, a text or search field, or a button, link, tab, radio or checkbox whose own words (text, label, `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby`, title, placeholder, id, name, class, `data-*` names), the element just before it, or its group (fieldset and legend, `role=group|radiogroup|toolbar|tablist`, nav, menu, else its parent: their own words and the element before them) hold "tag"/"topic" together with "filter", "show", "only", "browse" or "search"; a `<select>` holding the word whose first option starts with All/Any/Every counts too. A Tags field in the add form, or a tag list without "All", fails (tested); hidden controls count. It does not prove that choosing a filter changes the list (please veto: a static check, as the spec's filter values need not match the eval's "cat"/"Ideas"). The check itself is tested on hand-written apps, right and wrong (`platform/src/eval/media-check.test.ts`). The eval's Chromium now starts with a fake microphone and sound allowed without a tap.
- **(3.20) Prompts:** `readiness-v5` (was `readiness-v4` since 3.17; the brief's `readiness-v3` was already used): the template can take or pick photos and record voice messages up to 5 minutes, kept by the workshop with the app, up to 500 MB of photos and voice messages per app, seen and heard by everyone with the address (photos made smaller, location data removed); videos (recording, uploading or keeping them), recordings over 5 minutes and uploads of other files (documents, PDFs) stay "(server)", replacing "uploads of large files or videos"; "a spec that keeps its data, photos or voice messages in the app ... is ready". `clarify-v6`: the same facts in plain words, no videos (photos or voice messages suggested instead), and the reminder (below). `spec-v3`: one more rule in the shared spec rules: photos and voice messages may be kept (500 MB, beside 10 MB of other information, seen by everyone with the link), named in "Data it keeps" with their details and in "Screens" where they are taken or recorded; never videos. `change-v3`: the change prompt embeds those shared rules, so its version moved too (nothing else changed in it).
- **(3.20) The photo reminder (please veto the wording):** Clarify says, right after its restatement, in one short sentence in the participant's language, that anyone with the app's link can see the photos, so it is best not to photograph people or confidential papers; only the first time photos come up (an earlier reply that said it means not again). The first live run (`clarify-v6` without "right after your restatement ... in the participant's language") skipped it for a French idea; with it, 0 misses in two full runs. Voice messages get the "everyone with the link can hear them" fact but no extra reminder (the brief asks it for photos only).
- **(3.20) A personal app needs no login of its own (please veto):** the first live run told "a gallery of my drawings" that it "needs a sign-in or private account to keep your drawings separate from others, which the workshop cannot do". `clarify-v6` says an app for the participant alone is fine with a shared link: never say it needs a login or personal accounts of its own (the workshop's own sign-in to save is already built in, as in the approved 3.24 delta), and do not bring up privacy unless they ask. Try 2 (checker): try 1's wording "never say it needs a sign-in or an account" contradicted 3.24 (saving and uploading do need the workshop's sign-in), so it was reworded. The eval case avoids only the false claim ("needs a sign-in", "need a sign-in", "requires a sign-in", "needs a login", "need a login", "private account", "personal account", "separate from others"), not the word "sign-in", so a correct mention of the workshop's sign-in passes; run 1's reply still fails.
- **(3.20) The workshop's sign-in to save in readiness (please veto):** `readiness-v5` says saving needs the workshop's own sign-in, which every app already has (3.24), and that it "is not a login problem" (in the login line too), so a spec saying "someone signed in to the workshop adds ..." (as 3.19's build specs do) is not refused as a login. The login example (personal accounts) still fails with "login" in every run.
- **(3.20) No sizes in Clarify (please veto; departs from the brief's wording):** the brief's 3.20 text names "up to 500 MB per app" among what `clarify-v6` says; Clarify leaves that figure out on purpose and names only the 5-minute recording limit (as decided in 3.17, Clarify states no storage numbers; a participant never needs them to describe an idea). Readiness and the spec rules carry 10 MB and 500 MB.
- **(3.20) Where the media cases live (please veto):** the readiness suite now runs `readiness.json` and then `build.json`'s photo and voice specs (`readinessSuiteExamples`), so a spec the builder is proved to build must also pass the check; the build suite still builds `readiness.json`'s four examples and `build.json`'s two, unchanged. `readiness.json` gains its own cases rather than copies: "a gallery of my drawings, taken with the phone camera, with my own tags" (category `photo`, ready), "team voice board by topic" (`voice`, ready) and "team videos uploaded from the phone" (`server`, not ready, keyword `server`). A readiness example's category `photo` or `voice` does not make the build suite build it (it takes category `example` only).
- **(3.20) Clarify eval: `atMostOnce` (please veto):** a clarify example may list words each named at most once in the reply (start of a word, any case), failing with "says more than once (confidential 2 times)". The photo cases: "drawing gallery with phone photos" (first reply: anyone/everyone + link/address + the people part of the reminder as a phrase ("photograph people", "photos of people", "pictures of people", "photographing people", ...; try 2, checker: try 1's single words "people"/"person"/"someone" let a reply without the reminder pass on "share with other people") + confidential/private/sensitive/secret, "confidential" at most once, no "server", "megabyte" or false login claim (above)), "photo reminder not repeated" (a later reply after a history that holds the reminder: answers the question, none of "confidential", "sensitive", "photograph people", "photos of people", "pictures of people", "faces") and "photo idea in French" (whiteboard photos: tout le monde/toute personne/... + lien/adresse + the people part as a phrase ("photographier des personnes", "photos de personnes", "photographier des gens", ...; try 2, as above) + confidenti/document/papier/sensible, "confidenti" at most once).
