The `atMostOnce` list of the drawing gallery was narrowed to ["confidential"] after the first live run: it held ["confidential", "private", "sensitive", "secret"], and run 1's reply used "private" twice, once in the false sign-in claim and once in "keep it private", neither of them the reminder, so those other words counted things besides the reminder (a repeat of the reminder always repeats "confidential", the word it was written with); they stay accepted words for the reminder's second part, and the false claim is now caught by `avoids`. The try-2 phrases were checked against the logged live replies: runs 2 and 3 (`5c140f1`) still pass all three photo cases, run 1's gallery reply fails on the false claim and its French reply on every part. "Once" across a conversation is checked by that second case, not by reading a whole conversation.
- **(3.20) Spec eval:** `spec.json` gains "drawing gallery: first spec" (keywords photo, tag) and "voice messages: first spec" (record, topic).
- **(3.20) Live evals (Claude Sonnet 4.6, `eu.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6`):** readiness 22/22 twice (`f6af4cf`, `5c140f1`; the readiness prompt is the same in both), clarify 9/12 on `f6af4cf` (the misses above plus "reminders sent by email" naming a board but not the word "page", which then passed twice), 12/12 twice on `5c140f1`, and 12/12 twice on `6a97434` (try 2's reworded login rule and phrase-level checks; $0.087 each); spec 8/8 twice; change 1/1 once. Guardrail and suggestions prompts are unchanged and were not run.
- **(3.21) Admin switch (please veto):** `appAiEnabled` on `SETTINGS` (absent = off), written only by the admin function through `PUT /api/admin/app-ai` `{ "enabled": true|false }` (same pattern as Stop/Resume); the facilitator page shows a checkbox "Apps may use AI" with a state line (on, off, or on but held off by the Stop, texts in `ADMIN_TEXTS.appAi*`) under the Stop box. The global Stop wins over the switch.
- **(3.21) Answer shapes (please veto):** `tag-photo` -> `{ "tags": [...] }` (only from the app's list when it gives one, at most 20 given and 5 returned, each at most 50 characters), `read-photo` -> `{ "values": { <field>: string | number | "YYYY-MM-DD" | { "amount", "currency" } | null } }` (field types `text`, `number`, `date`, `money`; names at most 50 characters), `sort-text` -> `{ "categories": [...] }` (only from the app's list), `transcribe` -> `{ "text", "language" }`. Each string value is cut to 200 characters; an answer that does not fit the request is 502 "The AI could not read this". The model gets the read-photo fields as tool properties `f1`..`fn` (Bedrock tool property names must be identifiers) and the route maps them back to the app's names.
- **(3.21) Request limits (please veto):** request body at most 16 KB (400 above), tags at most 20, field and tag names at most 50 characters, besides the brief's 12 fields, 2,000 characters and 20 categories. Unknown request fields are refused (400), so a `prompt` field is refused like any other.
- **(3.21) Reservation (please veto):** each call reserves its worst cost before Bedrock (2,000 prompt tokens + 1,800 for a photo + 2 tokens per text byte in, 1,024 out at Haiku 4.5's EU price, about $0.010-0.016) and settles to the real token cost afterwards; Bedrock errors that are never billed (validation, access, throttling, quota, model not ready, not found) give the whole reservation back. The three day counters (`APPAI#<nick>`/`AI_USAGE`, since try 2 `APPAI_WRITER#<writer>`/`AI_USAGE`, and `APPAI_TOTAL`/`AI_USAGE`) move in one transaction; after 4 lost races the answer is 409. A budget can be passed by at most one reservation (a call is accepted while the counter is under the cap).
- **(3.21) Transcribe (please veto):** charged up front at $0.024 a minute on the recording's measured length rounded up to Transcribe's 15-second minimum, bounded by 5 minutes plus the media API's 5-second margin; languages detected among en-US, fr-FR, de-DE, es-ES, it-IT, nl-NL, pt-PT; job `ssd-workshop-app-ai-<nickname>-<media id>`, result under `transcripts/` (deleted with the job after reading). Since try 2 only the request that started the job polls it (every 2 s: the last wait between polls begins by 10 s into the request, so the last status call starts by ~12 s and ends by ~15 s, and the reading and clean-up still fit before the deadline: "(3.21) Time limits"); while the job runs it answers 202 `{ "status": "working" }` and the app asks again with the same recording, which joins the same job: a join answers 202 at once, with no wait, no charge and no budget read (try 1 let a join wait up to 15 s of Lambda time for free). The finished job's text is read, then the job and then the result file are deleted (in that order, see "(3.21) Two readers of one transcript").
- **(3.21) Transcribe jobs are not tagged (please veto):** passing `Tags` on `StartTranscriptionJob` may need `transcribe:TagResource`, beyond the three actions the brief allows, so jobs carry no `project=ssd-workshop` tag. As with Bedrock's on-demand calls ("(3.13)", infra rules), their spend is therefore not visible to the Cost Anomaly monitor `ssd-workshop-cost`, which watches that tag: only the $20 Budgets alert on the whole account and the app counters (at most $10 a day of app AI) bound it. Adding `TagResource` on `transcription-job/ssd-workshop-*` to the AI role and the boundary would make it visible; the boundary has 13 characters to spare, so it would need another statement shortened first.
- **(3.21) Leftovers when an app never asks again (please veto):** a job started by a request whose app never comes back stays in Transcribe until Transcribe's own retention removes the job record (90 days by default; it holds no audio, only the job's settings and status), and its result file `transcripts/<nick>/<id>.json` (the text, with word timings) stays in the media bucket until the lifecycle rule `ssd-workshop-transcripts-expiry` deletes it, at most a day (plus S3's lifecycle delay of up to a day). Neither is served (CloudFront maps only `/app-media/*`). Asking again with the same recording within that time reads the text and deletes both, free. Recorded in `infra/README.md`.
- **(3.21) Media bucket (please veto):** a lifecycle rule `ssd-workshop-transcripts-expiry` deletes `transcripts/` after 1 day (a safety net when a reading fails); the bucket policy's write deny for everyone but the media function now uses `NotResource transcripts/*`, leaving that prefix to identity policies (only the AI role may write there, synth-tested); CloudFront's read is narrowed to `app-media/*`.
- **(3.21) Who else can reach `transcripts/` (please veto, low risk):** the media bucket's write deny exempts `transcripts/*` (`NotResource`, since Transcribe writes the result with the AI role's rights and a bucket deny would refuse it). So the deploy role, whose policy allows `s3:*` on `ssd-workshop-*` buckets, could write a fake transcript there (it only deploys from `main`), and the Claude user (session) can read transcripts with its bucket reads (`GetObject*` on `ssd-workshop-*`): a text a participant recorded, for at most the day the lifecycle rule keeps a leftover ("Leftovers" above; normally the file lives a few seconds). CloudFront does not serve `transcripts/`. A fake transcript would be read only by a request for that recording while its job exists and is done, and would only become the text answered to that app. Recorded for Franck; no change.
- **(3.21) Throttles and function (please veto):** route throttles POST 2/s (burst 10) and OPTIONS 10/s (burst 20) since try 3 (chosen by Franck 2026-11-27 05:25 UTC, "Tighten, then accept"; try 2 had 5/s burst 10 and 20/s burst 40, see "(3.21) Cost"); filling them takes at least 4 and 20 addresses under the WAF rule's 0.5/s per address; function `ssd-workshop-api-app-ai`, 512 MB, 28 s; one Bedrock attempt (a retry would bill twice) of at most 20 s and never past the request's deadline less one settlement ("(3.21) Time limits").
- **(3.21) Time limits (try 2, please veto):** try 1's S3 client (2 attempts of 1 s + 8 s) plus a 20 s Bedrock attempt could pass the function's 28 s and API Gateway's 30 s. Now every call is bounded and every paid step starts only when it and what follows it fit before `REQUEST_DEADLINE_MS`, 26 s after the request started (2 s under the function's timeout for the log line): table calls 2 attempts of 1 s + 1.5 s (`TABLE_CALL_LIMIT_MS` 5.5 s with the SDK's backoff), S3 one attempt raced and aborted at 4 s body included (`S3_CALL_LIMIT_MS`; a socket timeout alone does not bound a body that trickles in), Transcribe one attempt aborted at 3 s, the model aborted at `min(20 s, time left - 5.5 s)` so its settlement still fits, and started only with at least 8 s (`MIN_MODEL_CALL_MS`), else 503 "The AI service is slow right now. Please try again in a moment." (outcome `out-of-time`) and the reservation goes back; a Transcribe job starts only with its start call, one status call and a refund left (11.5 s since try 3, else the same 503); the starting request's last wait between polls begins by 10 s in (`TRANSCRIBE_LAST_POLL_MS` = 26 - 2 wait - 3 status - 11 for reading 4 s, deleting the job 3 s and the file 4 s), so its last status call starts by ~12 s and ends by ~15 s, and a done job found later than 15 s in answers 202 (ask again) instead of reading. The free steps before a paid one (about seven table calls, the S3 read, up to 4 reservation tries) are not cut short: only if all of them took their full limits could the function time out first, and then nothing was billed and at most a reservation stays counted (never under the cost).
- **(3.21) A failed Transcribe start (try 3, please veto):** try 2 gave the reservation back on any error from `StartTranscriptionJob`, but our 3 s limit (TimeoutError), an aborted or broken connection or a 5xx can come after Transcribe created the job, which would then run uncharged (and the app's next requests would get free 202s and the text). Now only errors that prove nothing started give it back at once (`UNSTARTED_TRANSCRIBE_ERRORS`: `BadRequestException`, `LimitExceededException`, `AccessDeniedException`, as `UNBILLED_MODEL_ERRORS` for Bedrock); after any other error the request asks `GetTranscriptionJob` once (within the deadline: the start needs 3 s + 3 s + a 5.5 s refund left) and gives the charge back only when the job is missing. When the job is there the charge stays and the answer is 202 working (logged as `start:<error name>`), so the app asks again and joins it; when the status call fails too the charge stays and the answer is 500 (never under the cost). `ConflictException` still answers `exists` and gives this request's reservation back: the job exists only because a start succeeded, or failed without proof and was then found present, and in both cases that request kept its charge. Remaining gap: a status call that answers missing for a job Transcribe created a moment before (not seen in its documentation; Transcribe's job reads are not documented as eventually consistent) would leave that one job uncharged, at most $0.122.
- **(3.21) Two readers of one transcript (try 2, please veto):** two requests can both find the job done; the first reads the text and cleans up, and the second then finds no result file. The clean-up deletes the job first, then the file, so a request that finds the file gone and then the job gone too (DeleteTranscriptionJob's not-found, `cleanUp` answering `gone`) answers 202 "ask again" rather than 502; asking again finds no job and starts (and charges) a new one, within the budgets. A done job with no result file that this request deletes itself is a real failure: 502. A job that vanishes while the starting request polls also answers 202. No cache of the text (it would need a new item kind or a longer-lived file).
- **(3.21) MP4 declared length (try 2):** `mp4Seconds` also takes each track's `tkhd` duration and the sum of its edit list's (`edts`/`elst`) segment durations, in the movie's timescale (`mvhd`), since an edit can repeat or hold media and so declare more playback than the samples; "unknown" (all ones) and zero values are ignored like the other declared lengths, an entry count past its box refuses the file (checked before looping), and edits in a file without a movie timescale refuse it. The bound stays the largest of every declared length and the counted samples.
- **(3.21) Field names (try 2):** read-photo's answer object has no prototype (`Object.create(null)`), so a field the app names `__proto__`, `constructor` or `prototype` comes back as a field (try 1 lost `__proto__`).
- **(3.21) WAF (please veto):** rule `ssd-workshop-ai-rate` at priority 5, 150 requests per 5 minutes per address, POST and OPTIONS on paths starting `/api/apps/` and ending `/ai` (after URL decode and path normalisation), answering 429 with the CORS header. No new paid service (WAF is already on, one more rule is $1/month); the deploy role updates the web ACL through `ssd-workshop-deploy-safety-nets`.
- **(3.21) CORS policy v3:** CloudFront's `ssd-workshop-app-data-v3` adds POST to the allowed methods (its `originOverride` replaces the function's own list); v2 stays retained for Franck to delete.
- **(3.21) Access stack (please veto):** the boundary gains a `Transcribe` statement (Start, Get, Delete, `aws:RequestedRegion` eu-central-1). To fit the 6,144-character limit, `GitHubKeyForTheLauncherOnly` uses one `role*/ssd-workshop-builds-launcher` value and the boundary drops `ses:SendRawEmail` (no workshop code sends raw email). Size now 6,131.
- **(3.21) Logging (please veto):** one JSON line per request: method, task, outcome, status, cost, and the writer's nickname once the token passed (as the data and media APIs); never the token, media id, text or answer.
- **(3.21) Live eval (Claude Haiku 4.5, `eu.anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0`):** `npm run eval -- app-ai` reads two synthetic receipts drawn in Chromium (a German bakery, a French café; made-up shops) through the route's steps: 2/2 twice (`ff9566f`), 2,488 tokens in and 186 out, $0.0038 a run. Transcribe was not run from the session (denied): Franck's live check in `infra/README.md`.
- **(3.21) Cost (try 3, recomputed for the tightened throttles; accepted by Franck 2026-11-27 05:25 UTC with the tightened throttles):** worst case with the switch on all day, eu-central-1 and CloudFront Europe prices as in "(3.18) Cost" (API Gateway HTTP $1.20 a million, Lambda arm64 $0.0000133 per GB-second and $0.20 a million requests, DynamoDB $0.1525 a million read units and $0.7625 a million write units, WAF $0.60 a million requests, CloudFront $0.012 per 10,000 requests, $0.02/GB to the origin and $0.085/GB to viewers, CloudWatch Logs $0.63/GB ingested, S3 GET $0.00043 per 1,000), with POST held at its 2/s throttle (172,800 a day) and OPTIONS at 10/s (864,000 a day), 1,036,800 requests; try 2's 5/s and 20/s (432,000 and 1,728,000, 2,160,000 requests) in brackets for comparison. (1) AI: the $10 all-apps cap plus at most one reservation ($0.122 for the longest recording), $10.13 [$10.13]; the per-writer and per-app caps only split it. (2) Lambda: each POST that does not reach the AI can still read a 4 MB file and walk it (a photo too big for Bedrock, a recording over 5 minutes or a hostile file, refused 400 with no charge, any number of times): measured 140 ms on a full core for the worst 4 MB MP4, ~0.5 s at 512 MB plus the S3 read, so at most ~1 s x 0.5 GB per POST: $1.15 [$2.87]; OPTIONS ~10 ms each $0.06 [$0.12]; requests $0.21 [$0.43]; the paid calls' own run time on top, assuming the worst mix the $10 allows: real model calls settle at ~$0.0006 at the cheapest (a short sort-text) and Haiku answers such a call in 1 to 3 s, so at most ~16,700 calls of up to ~5 s each with their table calls = 41,700 GB-s, $0.55 [$0.55]; transcriptions are fewer per dollar (the cheapest is 15 s for $0.006, ~1,700 a day, each up to 26 s of polling and reading = 22,100 GB-s, $0.29), and a model call cut off at 20 s keeps its whole reservation (at least $0.010, so at most ~1,000 a day x 26 s, $0.17); only if Bedrock itself answered every cheap call in ~20 s would this part reach ~$2.94 (17,000 x 26 s), which no caller controls. Lambda in all $1.96 [$3.97]. (3) API Gateway: $1.24 [$2.59]. (4) DynamoDB: a POST reads at most 6 small items (live marker, token, settings, three counters; more only with a reservation), 1.04 M read units $0.16 [2.6 M, $0.40]; a paid call writes 3 counters and settles 3, ~9 write units, ~17,000 calls $0.12 [$0.12]; with lost races ~$0.35 [~$0.60] in all. (5) WAF: $0.62 [$1.30] (plus $1 a month for the rule). (6) CloudFront: requests $1.24 [$2.59], bodies to the origin (16 KB at most per POST) 2.6 GB $0.05 [6.6 GB, $0.13], answers to viewers (~0.5 KB each) 0.5 GB $0.04 [1 GB, $0.09]; $1.34 [$2.82]. (7) Logs: the function's own JSON line (~0.25 KB) plus Lambda's START, END and REPORT lines (~0.3 KB) for every invocation, ~0.55 KB each: 0.54 GB $0.34 [1.1 GB, $0.71] (two weeks' storage is a few cents a month). (8) S3 GETs of media: $0.07 [$0.19]. Transcribe's job status and delete calls are not billed. In all about **$16 a day** (about $6 of it requests and functions, not AI), **~$490 a month** if held every day of a month; with try 2's throttles it was about **$22 a day** (about $12 not AI, ~$680 a month; the checker's corrected figure, which try 2's ~$21 understated by leaving out Lambda's own log lines and the paid calls' run time). Both are above the $50 a month of "Stop and ask": Franck's acceptance of the tightened figure is awaited (the owner's decision on the throttles is pending; the recommended 2/s and 10/s are built). Filling the POST throttle with costly requests needs a signed-in participant (an app write token) and at least 4 addresses (the WAF rule's 150 per 5 minutes per address is 0.5/s); OPTIONS and token-less POSTs (refused 401 after two small reads, no file read) need no sign-in and at least 24 addresses for both throttles together [50], the "distributed flood" risk already accepted under "Abuse cost". Without an attacker a workshop day costs cents plus what apps really use (at most $10.13). Nothing is spent while the switch is off (the default). Levers: `APP_AI_THROTTLE`, `APP_AI_PREFLIGHT_THROTTLE`, `WAF_AI_RATE_LIMIT`, the three budgets.
- **(3.21) Per-writer cap (try 2; decided by Franck 2026-11-27 04:15 UTC, recorded as ADDED to question 7's answer above):** each signed-in writer (the nickname stored with the app write token, ticket 3.24) may spend at most $1 of app AI per UTC day across all apps (`WRITER_AI_DAILY_BUDGET_USD` in `platform/api/app-ai/budget.ts`, one constant to change or set to 10 to switch it off in effect), so one participant holding tokens for many apps cannot use the whole $10 alone. Its counter `APPAI_WRITER#<lower-cased nickname>` / `AI_USAGE` (day, costUsd) is reserved in the same TransactWriteItems as the app's and the all-apps counter, with the same rules: the same-day condition `#day = :today AND #costUsd < :budget` or the forward-only day start, settled and refunded with the other two, a broken or later-day counter read as spent (fails closed), passed by at most one reservation. When spent, every task answers 429 "You have used today's AI allowance for apps. It starts again tomorrow." (outcome `writer-budget`) before any file is read or AI called; checked after the app's budget and before the workshop's. The AI role's DynamoDB statements gain the leading key `APPAI_WRITER#*` (which neither `APPAI#*` nor `APPAI_TOTAL` matches; synth-tested). A token whose writer is not a valid nickname (it cannot be, by 3.24) is refused 401.
- **(3.22) Helpers (please veto):** `workshopData.ai.tagPhoto(address, tags?)`, `ai.readPhoto(address, fields)`, `ai.sortText(text, categories)` and `ai.transcribe(address)` (transcribe included: question 8 was answered yes and 3.21 built it; the brief's 3.22 text predates that). Each takes the address `savePhoto` or `recording.done` gave (the same check as `deleteMedia`: under `<page's server>/app-media/<nick>/`, 22-character id, else "This is not the address of one of this app's photos or recordings."), sends the route its path `/app-media/<nick>/<id>` (which the route accepts whatever the page's host, so it also works on a test server) as `POST <page's server at start>/api/apps/<nick>/ai` with `content-type: application/json`, `accept: application/json` and the Bearer token through the captured `fetch` (3.25's `<base>` rule, tested). They answer: `tagPhoto` the list of tags, `readPhoto` an object of the asked names (no prototype, as the route's) whose values are text, a number, `"YYYY-MM-DD"`, `{ amount, currency }` or `null`, `sortText` the list of categories, `transcribe` `{ text, language }`.
- **(3.22) Checks before sending (please veto):** the route's limits, from `platform/api/app-ai/tasks.ts`: at most 20 given tags, 1 to 12 fields typed text/number/date/money with only `name` and `type`, 1 to 2,000 characters of text (code points), 1 to 20 categories, every name 1 to 50 characters once cleaned as the route cleans it (control and format characters to spaces, spaces collapsed, trimmed) and different ignoring case; the cleaned names are sent, so the answer uses them. Messages: "Give at most 20 tags to choose from, each 1 to 50 characters long and all different.", "Ask for 1 to 12 fields, each with a name of 1 to 50 characters (all different) and a type: text, number, date or money.", "Give 1 to 20 categories, each 1 to 50 characters long and all different.", "Give a text of 1 to 2,000 characters to sort." A visitor (no sign-in) gets the sign-in offer and "Sign in to the workshop to save in this app." with nothing sent, as for uploads.
- **(3.22) Checks after answering (please veto):** an answer that does not fit the request (another key, a tag or category not asked, more than 5 suggested tags, a text over 200 characters, a value of the wrong type, a missing or extra field, a currency not three capital letters, a 202 for a photo task, not JSON) is refused with "The workshop answered something unexpected. Please try again." A refusal shows the route's own `message` (503 switched off, 429 for each of the three allowances, 401 also shows the sign-in again like a refused save); a 429 without a message (the firewall's rate rule) says "Too many AI requests right now. Please try again in a minute."
- **(3.22) transcribe asks again (please veto):** on 202 it waits 3 s and asks again with the same recording, and gives up after 3 minutes in all (61 asks at most: 0 s, 3 s, ... 180 s) with "The recording is still being turned into text. Please try again in a minute." (the job goes on; a later call joins it and gets the text). Each ask that starts the job may itself take up to ~15 s on the route (3.21's own polling), which counts within the 3 minutes; a joining ask answers at once (3.21 try 2), so the waiting costs no Lambda time beyond one short request every 3 s.
- **(3.22) AGENTS.md rule 16 and the builder's prompts (please veto):** rule 16 "AI suggestions (only when `SPEC.md` asks for them)" lists the four helpers and what they give, says the AI does nothing else (no chat, no writing texts, no free questions), that a suggestion is shown for the visitor to accept or change (in the form's fields or as choices to tap) and saved only on Save, asked only on the visitor's action (right after a photo is saved, or from a button), shown with `textContent` or a field's `value`, never `innerHTML`, and that every call can fail (AI switched off, today's allowance spent): show `error.message` and let the visitor fill the fields by hand. Its "Suggest tags" example is run as written by `template-ai.test.ts`. `AI_GUIDANCE` in `platform/builder/agent.ts` says the same in three lines, last in both the build and the change prompt (after the media guidance).
- **(3.22) Build eval AI cases (please veto):** `platform/api/eval/build.json` gains "Sketch Wall" (category `ai-tags`: tags suggested right after the photo is saved, kept or changed, then Save) and "Expense Log" (`ai-receipt`: shop, date and total read from the receipt photo into the form, checked, then Save), examples 7 and 8. They stay out of the readiness suite (`readinessSuiteExamples`) until ticket 3.23 teaches the readiness prompt the AI tasks. The eval's in-memory workshop answers `POST /api/apps/<nick>/ai` (sign-in required, request judged by the real `parseAppAiRequest`, photo must be stored) with fixed answers: tags `sunflower`, `crayon` (or the first two given), read-photo text "Bäckerei Sonnenschein", date 2026-11-14, money 12.40 EUR, number 12.4; no Bedrock. The receipt case picks the synthetic receipt of `src/eval/app-ai.ts` (German bakery). `exerciseAi` passes a build when its code calls `workshopData.ai.tagPhoto(` / `.readPhoto(`, the picked photo is uploaded, the app asks the AI for it (by itself, else from a button reading suggest/read/scan/auto/AI/fill, else after Save), the answer shows (a field's value or the page's text: a suggested tag, or for receipts 12.4/12.40/12,40 or "Sonnenschein"), no saved value holds it 1.5 s later, and after accepting as a visitor would (fields already hold it; else an Accept/Apply/Use button, else tapping the suggested tags) and pressing Save, a saved value holds a suggested tag or the total. Tested on hand-written apps (`src/eval/ai-check.test.ts`).

## Phase 3: iterate and supervise

Franck's answers to the brief's questions (2026-11-25, `docs/phases/phase-3.md`, PR #49): all as suggested except the load test.

- **Dashboard scope (spec delta, approved 2026-11-25):** every facilitator sees and acts on every participant; the dashboard has a Group column and a filter that starts on the facilitator's own group. CHANGED, section 2, facilitator row: "see their group's dashboard" becomes "see the dashboard of every participant (filtered to their own group by default)". CHANGED, section 5 Security: "facilitators only see their own group" becomes "facilitators see every participant, never their email addresses".
- **Budget:** all AI spend counts toward the $5 (conversations and builds), checked before each AI call and before a build is queued; a running build always finishes.
- **Editing SPEC.md (spec delta, approved by Franck 2026-11-26):** ADDED, section 3 step 4: beginner-track participants can edit their spec on the page ("Edit my spec", ticket 3.15) and rebuild from it; it counts as a change request and goes through the same checks as Rebuild from my repository. Advanced-track participants can link a personal GitHub account, invited as a collaborator with write access to their own repository only, to edit `SPEC.md` on GitHub (planned in the phase 4 brief). Until then only the organisation owner and the platform can write to the repositories.
- **Rebuild from GitHub (spec delta, approved 2026-11-25):** CHANGED, section 3 step 4: "edit the spec file there to trigger a rebuild" becomes "edit the spec file there and press Rebuild from my repository". No webhook; the rebuild counts as a change request.
- **Allow-list:** stays on Franck's command line (`npm run allowlist -- import`); the admin page holds only the caps and the global Stop, so no email address is shown on a page.
- **Safety nets (new paid service, approved):** AWS WAF with one per-IP rate rule on `/api/*` (about $6 to $8 a month) and Cost Anomaly Detection on `project=ssd-workshop` (free), ticket 3.13.
- **Auto-stop:** an EventBridge rule and Lambda stop any builds-cluster task outside the build subnets (about $0), ticket 3.14. Changed by Franck on 2026-11-26 to the launcher's minute check (see "3.14 Auto-stop outside the build subnets").
- **Bedrock quota and load test:** no load test for now (Franck: "we'll see later"). Builds at once starts at 15, builds retry Bedrock throttling with backoff, and the admin can lower the cap on the day. The Sonnet 4.6 EU requests-per-minute raise stays open until a load test sizes it.

### 3.1 Sessions follow the allow-list, and sign-out

- **(3.1) How a session finds its allow-list entry:** nothing pointed from a participant or a session to the entry (its key is the address; only the entry points to the participant). The verify function now stores on the session item `allowRef`: the normalized address sealed with AES-256-GCM (random 12-byte IV) under a key derived from the session token (HKDF-SHA256, info `ssd-workshop session allow-list link v1`). The token lives only in the browser's cookie and the table keeps its SHA-256, so the table alone never shows the address; the session check opens it with the caller's cookie, does one consistent GetItem on the existing `EMAIL#<address>`/`ALLOW` key and forgets it (never logged, returned or stored elsewhere). Rejected alternative (for veto): a global secondary index on `participantId` (copies the `EMAIL#` keys into the index, reads are eventually consistent, so a person signing in for the first time could be refused for a moment).
- **(3.1) Session check:** every protected route (participant and conversation functions) reads the session, then in parallel the allow-list entry (projection `role`, `group`, `participantId` only, consistent) and the profile. An entry that is gone, has no valid role or group, or is not linked to the session's participant, and a session without a sealed link (from before this ticket, or one that does not open with the cookie's token), deletes the session item and answers `401 {"message":"Please sign in."}` (log outcome `removed` on the participant routes). Role and group in every answer and decision come from the entry, not the profile; the profile's copies are refreshed only at sign-in as before. Sessions created before the deploy are signed out at their next call (people sign in again).
- **(3.1) Rights:** the participant and conversation roles gain `dynamodb:GetItem` in `EMAIL#*` limited to `dynamodb:Attributes` `pk`, `sk`, `role`, `group`, `participantId` with `dynamodb:Select` `SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES` (never `email`; `SESSION_ALLOW_READ_ATTRIBUTES` in `infra/lib/config.ts`), and `dynamodb:DeleteItem` in `SESSION#*`; the verify role may also put `allowRef` on a session. A role holding the sign-in code role's put rights could overwrite an `ALLOW` item without `role` (see the infra rule), which now signs that person out: still a denial of service only, as before.
- **(3.1) Sign-out route:** `POST /api/sign-out` on the participant function, behind the same session check; needs `content-type: application/json` like the other writes (415 otherwise, session kept). Answers `200 {"message":"You're signed out."}` after deleting the session item, and every answer to this route except 415 and 500 (also the 401 for no, a forged, an expired or a removed session) sets `__Host-ssd_session=; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Path=/; Max-Age=0`. Logged as `{"event":"participant","route":"sign-out","outcome":"signed-out"}`.
- **(3.1) Sign out link (please veto the place and wording):** a small "Sign out" link-styled button under the title, on the right, shown only on signed-in screens (nickname, steps 1 to 4); it sends `POST /api/sign-out` with `{}`, and on 200 or 401 stops polling and shows the email screen with "You're signed out."; any other answer stays on the step and shows the usual error under it.
- **(3.1) Junk hint:** the code screen's "Not in your inbox? Check your Junk folder." becomes "No code after a minute? Check your Junk or Spam folder." (the brief's wording). The neutral message after asking for a code keeps its "check your Junk folder too".
- **(3.1) Removal command (try 2, please veto the wording):** `npm run -s allowlist -- remove <email> [--yes]`, run by Franck with his own credentials (`dynamodb:GetItem` and `dynamodb:DeleteItem` on `ssd-workshop-data`). The address is trimmed, lower-cased and checked like an import row; refusals never repeat what was typed. Without `--yes` it only looks (consistent GetItem of `pk` on `EMAIL#<address>`/`ALLOW`) and says "This address has an allow-list entry. Nothing was changed: run again with --yes to remove it." or "This address has no allow-list entry. Nothing was changed."; with `--yes` it deletes that one item (condition `attribute_exists(pk)`, so "removed" and "there was none" differ without returning the item) and says "Removed the allow-list entry for this address. Their sessions end at their next call; their profile and app are kept." It never prints an address (not even the typed one), and AWS errors go through the usual redaction. Nothing else is deleted: the code items expire on their own, and the profile, conversation and app stay. Re-adding the address creates a new entry without a participant link, so the next sign-in starts a new participant (the old data stays in the table, unlinked). A removed person's nickname claim stays held and their app stays public at `/apps/<nickname>/` until the phase 5 removal workflow.
- **(3.1) Removal does not delete live sessions (try 2):** session items are keyed by the token's SHA-256 (`SESSION#<hash>`) and the entry links only to the participant id; finding a person's sessions would take a table scan (or a new index), so the command does not try. The next protected call of each of their sessions finds the entry gone, deletes that session and answers 401 (criterion 1); an unused session expires with its 8-hour TTL and opens nothing meanwhile.
- **(3.1) Logs (try 2):** the conversation function also logs outcome `removed` (instead of `unauthorized`) when the session check signs a removed person out, like the participant function.
- **(3.1) Missing profile (try 2):** a session whose allow-list entry is valid but whose profile is missing is also deleted (still 401, reason `no-session`), so no refusal leaves a session item behind; the entry is untouched.
- **(3.1) Session delete returns nothing (try 2):** the participant and conversation roles' `DeleteItem` on `SESSION#*` now also requires `StringEqualsIfExists` `dynamodb:ReturnValues` = `NONE` (the store sends no `ReturnValues`). Live check for the ticket: `infra/README.md` "Check after the sign-out deploy (ticket 3.1)".

### 3.2 Workshop settings and per-person limits

- **(3.2) Settings item:** one item at `SETTINGS` / `SETTINGS` (its own partition, `settingsKey` in `platform/api/keys.ts`) with `type` `settings`, `aiBudgetUsd`, `changeRequestsPerPerson`, `buildsAtOnce`, `stopped` and `updatedAt`. Every reader goes through `settingsFromItem` (`platform/api/workshop/settings.ts`): a missing item reads as $5, 5, 15 and Stop off, and each missing or out-of-range value reads as its own default (a malformed value can never stop or block anyone). Reads are consistent (`DynamoSettingsStore.getSettings`), so a change applies at the next read.
- **(3.2) Admin routes in their own function:** `GET` and `PUT /api/admin/settings` are served by a new function `ssd-workshop-api-admin` (not the participant function), so only its role may write `SETTINGS` (synth test), and the participant function, reachable by everyone, gets no settings rights. Its role: GetItem in `SESSION#*`, `PARTICIPANT#*` and `SETTINGS`, the session check's rights, and UpdateItem in `SETTINGS` only of `SETTINGS_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` (`infra/lib/config.ts`) with `ReturnValues` `NONE` (never PutItem, so the item is never replaced). Ticket 3.5's Stop and Resume and 3.12's page use the same function.
- **(3.2) Stop stays out of PUT:** `PUT /api/admin/settings` changes the three caps only and keeps `stopped` as it is (a `stopped` field in the body is ignored, and `stopped` is not in the role's attribute list); `GET` (and the PUT's answer) shows `stopped`. Ticket 3.5 adds its own Stop and Resume routes and `stopped` to the list.
- **(3.2) PUT rules (please veto):** a JSON body (415 otherwise) with all three caps as numbers: `aiBudgetUsd` 0 to 50 (any amount, stored to whole cents), `changeRequestsPerPerson` 0 to 20 and `buildsAtOnce` 1 to 30 (whole numbers), ends included. Anything else answers `400 {"message": "Send aiBudgetUsd (0 to 50 dollars), changeRequestsPerPerson (0 to 20) and buildsAtOnce (1 to 30), as numbers.", "invalid": [names]}` and stores nothing (the answer never repeats a value). A saved PUT answers 200 with the settings as read back.
- **(3.2) Who may call them:** the role comes from the allow-list through `requireSession` (3.1), never from the profile. No session answers 401; a participant or facilitator gets the plain `404 {"message":"Not found."}` (as an unknown route) before the body is read, and nothing is read or written. Logs are `{"event":"admin","route":"settings-get|settings-put","outcome":...}` only.
- **(3.2) Per-person limit fields:** the profile gains `extraBudget` (dollars), `paused`, `changeRequestsUsed` and `stepSince`, read through `limitsFromItem`: absent (every profile from before this ticket) reads as 0, false, 0 and no `stepSince`. No role may write `extraBudget`, `paused` or `changeRequestsUsed` yet: tickets 3.6 and 3.10 add them to the roles that need them. `budgetFor` gives a participant's budget (settings plus extra budget, in dollars rounded to whole cents) for ticket 3.3.
- **(3.2) stepSince:** every update that changes `step` sets `stepSince` to the same time in the same update: the nickname claim (nickname to clarify), Write my spec (clarify to spec), Approve spec (spec to approved), the readiness outcome (approved to building, or back to spec; AI worker, and the conversation function on a queue failure), and the operator resets `npm run conversation -- reset` and `npm run participant -- reset` (back to clarify, also from clarify: step 1 starts again). `stepSince` was added to `PARTICIPANT_PROFILE_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES`, `CONVERSATION_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` and `AI_WORKER_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES`. The builder, launcher and deployer never change `step`.
- **(3.2) No stepSince at sign-in (please veto):** the sign-in's profile upsert does not write `stepSince`: in one update it cannot tell a new profile from an old one, so `if_not_exists` would give every existing profile the time of its next sign-in as its time in step. A new profile starts its first step at `createdAt`; the dashboard (3.10) shows time in step from `stepSince`, else `createdAt`.
- **(3.2) Phase 3 texts (please veto the wording beyond the brief):** one module, `platform/api/workshop/texts.ts`, holds the participant-facing messages (button and label texts stay in the web code) (budget reached, paused, workshop paused, spec unchanged, facilitators only) and the counted ones: "You've used your 5 changes." ("1 change" for one), "Changes left: 3 of 5" (never below 0), "Your build is in the queue: 3 builds ahead of you." ("1 build ahead", and "Your build is next in the queue." when none is ahead). Plain text, no amount spent, no address.

### 3.3 AI budget enforced

- **(3.3) Where the budget is checked (please veto: routes do not refuse ahead, the worker answers with the budget text):** in the AI worker only, for every job: once before the first AI call (guardrail, clarify or spec answer, readiness check; also a facilitator-approved flag and Write my spec's own request), again before the answer call counting the guardrail call's cost, and before a passed readiness check queues the build counting that check's cost (spend read once per job from the `USAGE` item's `costUsd`, plus what the job's earlier calls cost, not yet stored). The rule is `budgetOf`/`overBudget` in `platform/api/workshop/budget.ts` with `ConversationStore.getBudgetFacts` (settings, the profile's `extraBudget`, the spend; all consistent reads), for ticket 3.6's suggestions to call before their AI call. The send, Write my spec and Approve spec routes do not refuse ahead: the message is stored and queued as before and the worker answers it without an AI call, so the page's polling flow is unchanged and the reply appears where the AI's would.
- **(3.3) The budget answer (please veto):** at or over the budget (spend >= workshop budget + extra budget, in dollars; a $0 budget means no AI at all) the message ends with status `failed` and a reply of the new kind `budget` holding exactly "You've used your AI budget for today. Your facilitator can add more." (`PHASE_3_TEXTS.budgetReached`). `failed`, not a new status, so the page and the API need no new state: the participant can send again at once, and after a facilitator adds budget (ticket 3.10) they resend; a held message is not answered later by itself. Budget replies never go back to the model as history (only `answer` replies do). A guardrail call that ran before the budget was found used is counted and its verdict kept on the message. Worker log outcome `over-budget` (no amount).
- **(3.3) Readiness over budget (please veto: an over-budget Approve spec goes back to step 2):** an Approve spec over budget (or a readiness check whose own cost used the last of the budget) goes back to step 2 like a failed check (approval cleared, the budget text as the reply, any tokens counted) and queues no build; the participant approves again once budget was added.
- **(3.3) A queued or running build always finishes:** the launcher, the builder and the deployer never read the budget (a test fails if they start to), and a redelivered readiness job only re-sends its build id. So spend can pass the budget by at most one build plus the readiness check that queued it (a few cents).
- **(3.3) `budgetLeft` (please veto):** on `GET /api/conversation` only (the step 1 and 2 page load and the reload after each reply), in dollars rounded down to whole cents, never below 0; the caller's own only. Not on `/api/me`: the participant function stays without any `SETTINGS` right (3.2). Because it is on `GET /api/conversation` only, ticket 3.8's step 4 should read it there. If the budget read fails, the answer is still 200 without `budgetLeft` (the error is logged by name only) and the page shows no budget line.
- **(3.3) Budget left on the page (please veto the wording and place):** a small help line under the Send form on steps 1 and 2, "AI budget left: $3.20" (two decimals), shown once the conversation answer carries `budgetLeft`. Known quirk: with less than one cent left the line shows "$0.00" (rounded down) while the AI still answers, because the check compares the exact spend; the next call uses the budget up.
- **(3.3) Rights:** the AI worker and the conversation function may `GetItem` the `SETTINGS` item only with a projection of `SETTINGS_READ_ATTRIBUTES` (`pk`, `sk`, the three caps and `stopped`; `dynamodb:Select` `SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES`; `infra/lib/config.ts`, synth test); `readSettings` in `platform/api/workshop/store.ts` projects exactly these. Their existing `PARTICIPANT#*` reads cover the profile's `extraBudget` and the `USAGE` item (both read projected). No new write.
- **(3.3) A broken spend fails closed:** a `costUsd` that is present but negative or not a finite number reads as the budget reached (no AI call, no build); a missing `USAGE` item or `costUsd` still reads as nothing spent.
- **(3.3) No eval case:** the budget answer is fixed text written without a model call, so criterion 4 is proven by tests (`platform/api/conversation/budget.test.ts`, `platform/api/workshop/budget.test.ts`), not by `npm run eval`.
- **(3.4) Build slots:** a counter item (`BUILD_SLOTS`/`COUNT`: `running`, `version`, `repairMisses`) plus one holder item per running build (`BUILD_SLOTS`/`BUILD#<id>`). The launcher's claim adds both in the same transaction that moves the build `queued` -> `building`, under the condition `attribute_exists(running) AND running >= 0 AND running < buildsAtOnce` (read from `SETTINGS`, default 15), so two launchers can never pass the cap, and a missing or negative counter reads as full (no build starts) until the minute check repairs it (try 2; was: a missing counter counted as 0). The deployer (live and failed) and the launcher (its failures and its check's) free the slot after their own terminal write, in one transaction: the build is no longer `building`/`deploying`, the holder exists and is deleted, `running` goes down. Keyed by the holder, so a slot is freed exactly once whoever comes first. A failed release is left to the minute check (`platform/api/build/slots.ts`).
- **(3.4) The builder task does not free slots (try 2, please veto):** the task runs next to the AI agent, so it has no right on `BUILD_SLOTS` at all (before: it could update the counter, so code running in the task could set the shared counter to any value). A build the runner marks failed keeps its slot until the launcher's minute check sees it is no longer `building`/`deploying` and frees it: up to about a minute of one slot unused per such failure.
- **(3.4) The check repairs the slots (please veto):** every minute the launcher's check (1) deletes malformed items of `BUILD_SLOTS` (anything but the counter that is not a `build-slot` with a valid `buildId` at `BUILD#<that id>`: such an item at a build's holder key would otherwise block that build's claim forever); (2) frees the holders of builds that are no longer `building`/`deploying` (failed by the builder, a release that failed, a build gone, or one queued again by a retry); (3) sets `running` to the number of holders left, under the condition that `version` has not moved since it read it (a claim or release in between makes it wait for the next minute), and resets `repairMisses` to 0. A refused repair adds 1 to `repairMisses` (only the launcher may name it). The repair is written without the condition when the counter is missing or not a whole number, or negative (no race can explain those, and no claim passes then), or after 3 refused repairs in a row (`SLOT_REPAIR_FORCE_AFTER`): a writer that keeps moving `version` (the deployer may write the counter) cannot keep a wrong counter for more than about 4 minutes. A forced write can miss a claim or release made in the same few milliseconds; the next minute's repair corrects it, so the cap can be passed by the claims made in that instant until the next successful repair. Known gap for ticket 3.5: a repair write that meets a running claim transaction (`TransactionConflictException`) aborts that minute's check, which then recovers at the next minute. Normal races keep the conditional path: three refused repairs in a row need a claim or release inside the check's few-millisecond window three minutes running. Builds already running when this ships have no holder and are not counted: for their last minutes the cap can be passed by that many builds. Until the first check after the deploy creates the counter, no build starts (at most a minute).
- **(3.4) Waiting via the message's visibility (please veto):** a build that finds no slot stays `queued`; the launcher sets its message's visibility to 30 s (`LAUNCH_WAIT_SECONDS`) and reports it as a batch item failure (`reportBatchItemFailures` on the event source), never throwing, so it is not logged as an error. The launcher may not send to the launch queue (only the AI worker may), so it does not re-send. The launch queue now keeps messages 2 days (was 1) and moves one to the dead-letter queue after 1,000 deliveries (was 3, SQS's maximum): a waiting message lasts over 8 hours of deliveries; a message the launcher keeps failing on reaches the dead-letter queue after about 42 hours, where the check marks its build failed as before. Builds placed in the waiting line are failed much sooner by the queued time limit (next entry); only a launch that fails before its first placement (reading the build, the profile, the conversation or the settings, or the placement itself) still waits for the dead-letter queue. One participant's messages share a FIFO group, so a waiting build holds back only that participant's own later messages.
- **(3.4) Queued time limit (try 2, please veto the number):** the minute check fails a build still `queued` 4 hours (`QUEUE_WAIT_MAX_MS`) after the launcher first placed it in the waiting line (the time in its `queueKey`), with "The build waited too long in the queue and did not start. Your facilitator can restart it.", and removes its entry; its next delivery is then a duplicate. This covers waiting and launches that keep failing before their claim (counting thrown deliveries was the alternative: it needs a counter per build and still misses builds that wait). 4 hours is above the longest sensible wait (about 40 participants at a lowered cap of 3, each build up to 12 minutes plus its deploy: about 3.5 hours) and below a waiting message's 8 hours of deliveries. A retried build gets a new place, so a new 4 hours. Ticket 3.5 (Stop) must keep a paused workshop from running into this limit.
- **(3.4) Start order and "builds ahead" (please veto):** the launcher places a build in a waiting line (`BUILD_QUEUE`, sort key `<first seen ISO time>#<build id>`, the same value stored on the build as `queueKey`) the first time it sees it, and refreshes the entry's `lastSeenAt` on each delivery. "Ahead" = entries with an earlier place seen in the last 4 minutes (`QUEUE_SEEN_MS`, try 2: was 2; above the launch queue's 150 s visibility timeout, after which a message comes back when setting its 30 s failed or a launcher was busy, so such a build never loses its turn; an older entry belongs to a message that is gone and blocks the line for at most 4 minutes). A build starts only while `running + ahead < cap`, so builds start in arrival order (near FIFO: delivery timing can still swap two builds that wait the same 30 s). The check removes entries whose build is no longer queued. A retried or reset build loses its `queueKey` and gets a new place at its next launch.
- **(3.4) "Builds ahead" on the page (please veto):** `GET /api/build` adds `ahead` (a COUNT query: only the number comes back) only for the caller's own queued build that the launcher has placed; another participant's build answers 404 before anything is counted (tested: the count is never computed). The count is a `Query` of `BUILD_QUEUE` with `Select` `COUNT` (IAM: `StringEquals` `dynamodb:Select` `COUNT`, names `pk`, `sk`, `lastSeenAt` only), paged and consistent; DynamoDB still reads the entries before the build's place to count them (read capacity for about 40 small items at most, every 2 s per queued participant's page: negligible on demand). Step 3 then shows `queuePositionText` from `platform/api/workshop/texts.ts` ("Your build is in the queue: 3 builds ahead of you.", "1 build ahead", "Your build is next in the queue." for 0); the web code imports that text. Before the first placement, or when the count fails (answer still 200, logged by name), the page shows the earlier "queued" text.
- **(3.4) Bedrock throttling (please veto the numbers):** the builder's Bedrock proxy retries 429 and 503 itself, signed again each try, with full-jitter backoff (a random pause up to 1 s x 2^try, at most 20 s) for up to 2 minutes per request (`THROTTLE_RETRY_MS`, under Claude Code's 10-minute request and 3-minute idle limits), then passes the status on; it stops when the agent hangs up. Claude Code then retries the request itself: its retry count is pinned to its default, 10 (`CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_RETRIES`, found in the SDK's bundled Claude Code), so 11 tries of over 100 s each outlast the 12-minute build limit: a throttled build ends with "The build took too long." rather than an early failure. Logged as outcome `throttled`.
- **(3.4) Rights:** the launcher gets `GetItem` on `SETTINGS` (projected), reads and writes of `BUILD_SLOTS` (`LAUNCHER_SLOT_ATTRIBUTES`, now with `repairMisses`) and `BUILD_QUEUE` (`LAUNCHER_QUEUE_ATTRIBUTES`; `UpdateItem`/`DeleteItem`, no `PutItem`), `ConditionCheckItem` on builds, and `queueKey` on builds. Its reads of both partitions are statements of their own with `StringEquals` `dynamodb:Select` (`SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES` for `BUILD_SLOTS`; `SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES` or `COUNT` for `BUILD_QUEUE`), so a read of whole items is refused (try 2; synth test); writes carry no Select, so they are separate statements. The deployer gets only the release (`ConditionCheckItem` of a build's `status`; `UpdateItem`/`DeleteItem` in `BUILD_SLOTS` of `pk`, `sk`, `running`, `version`); the builder task gets nothing on the slots (try 2); the conversation function a COUNT-only `Query` of `BUILD_QUEUE`. Known residual: IAM cannot narrow a `DeleteItem` or `UpdateItem` by sort key, so the deployer could delete or rewrite the counter item, or write a stray item in `BUILD_SLOTS`; a deleted counter now reads as full (no build starts, never the cap passed) until the check recreates it within a minute, a wrong value is repaired (forced after 3 refused repairs), and a stray item is deleted by the check. The deployer runs only platform code. No change to the access stack (its boundary already allows these).
- **(3.5) Stop and Resume routes (please veto):** `POST /api/admin/stop` and `POST /api/admin/resume`, admin only (anyone else, facilitators included, gets 404 before anything is read), with a JSON content type (415 otherwise) and no body needed. Each writes `stopped` plus `stoppedAt` or `resumedAt` and `updatedAt` on `SETTINGS` in one `UpdateItem` (only the admin function may write `SETTINGS`, as before) and answers 200 with the settings, as the caps route does. Pressing Stop twice is harmless (the same state, a new time). A 500 from Stop or Resume may come after the switch was applied (the write landed, the answer did not): 3.12's admin page should read the settings again after any error rather than assume nothing changed.
- **(3.5) The AI worker's hold (please veto):** the worker reads the settings at the start of every job (with the budget facts it already read). While Stop is on it makes no Bedrock call: in one transaction it refreshes the message's and the conversation's `pendingSince` (only while the message is still `pending` and still the conversation's pending one; otherwise the job is dropped as `skipped`), then sends the same job again to its own queue with a 30 s delay (`STOP_HOLD_SECONDS`) and consumes the delivery (outcome `held`). A new message each time, so neither the AI queue's `maxReceiveCount` (3) nor its dead-letter queue is ever reached, however long the Stop; and the refreshed `pendingSince` keeps the 20-minute stale rule from failing a held message, so the page keeps showing it pending and the participant cannot send another. A failed send throws, so SQS redelivers the job and the next delivery holds it again. Readiness jobs (Approve spec) are held the same way: no AI call and no build until Resume. A job already past its first AI call when Stop is pressed finishes (the check is at the job's start only); a redelivered readiness job that only re-sends its build id still does (no AI call). Cost: one SQS send and one small transaction per held message every 30 s.
- **(3.5) The launcher while Stop is on (please veto):** a launch message still places its build in the waiting line (its `queueKey`, so its order is kept), then waits like a build without a slot (outcome `paused`, message back after 30 s, no slot claimed, no GitHub or ECS call). The queued time limit (4 h) is not applied while Stop is on, and afterwards a build's wait counts from the later of its place and the last Resume (`resumedAt`), so a long Stop never fails a waiting build. Known limit: a launch message waiting more than about 8 hours in total (1,000 deliveries of 30 s, ticket 3.4) reaches the dead-letter queue, and the check then fails its build; a Stop of a whole working day can do that. Retry restarts such a build.
- **(3.5) Running builds on Stop (please veto):** the minute check, while Stop is on, puts every `building` build that has not pushed its commit back to `queued` in one conditional write (`#status = :building AND #taskArn = :taskArn AND attribute_not_exists(#pushedCommit)`): attempt id, launch time, task and feed removed, its place in the waiting line = its claim's time (so it starts again before builds queued after it), then frees its slot and sends `ecs:StopTask`. The builder's writes are all conditional on the removed attempt id, so the stopped task can no longer write, and the next launch starts from the beginning (the repository is prepared again with the template and spec on top). A build that already pushed its commit (handed over) or is `deploying` finishes: no AI runs there, and stopping it would waste a finished build. A claim whose task is not recorded yet is stopped by the next check. So a running task gets StopTask within about a minute of Stop (criterion 2). Residual: StopTask takes effect a moment after the requeue, and the old task may still push commits to the participant's repository until it does (its record writes are refused, so it can never hand over); the relaunch prepares the repository again on top of `main`, so it may build on those commits. Nothing from the old attempt is ever deployed: the deployer only takes the commit a current attempt handed over. Tested: the old attempt's claim, feed, hand-over and failure writes are refused both right after the requeue and after the relaunch's claim (`launcher.test.ts`).
- **(3.5) How a stopped build starts again (please veto):** the access boundary lets only the AI worker and the scheduler send to the launch queue (`BuildsFromTheirSendersOnly`), and this ticket may not change it, so the launcher cannot give the build a new launch message. Instead the build's watch entry (with its stopped task) is kept as the marker of a build with no launch message; each check keeps its place in the waiting line seen, and once Stop is off the check launches those builds itself (`processLaunch` with the launcher's own deps), oldest place first, after freeing slots, while at least 60 s of the invocation is left (`RELAUNCH_MIN_LEFT_MS`; the launcher's timeout is 120 s). A build that must wait for a slot (or a Stop pressed again) stops the round; the rest wait for the next minute. The claim replaces the old watch entry. So after Resume, stopped builds start again within about a minute or two (several per minute, as time allows), before queued builds placed after them.
- **(3.5) "Paused" on the page (please veto):** `GET /api/conversation`, the message poll and `GET /api/build` add `paused: true` while Stop is on (read from `SETTINGS`), on every message status (pending, flagged, answered). A failed read answers 200 with `pausedUnknown: true` instead (try 3) and logs the error name; the page then keeps the line as it shows it, and only an answer without either flag clears it. Try 2 adds `GET /api/status` on the conversation function (which already reads `SETTINGS`, so no IAM change; the participant function gets no `SETTINGS` right): any signed-in person, the nickname screen included, gets `{ paused: true }` or `{}`; a failed read answers 500, so the page keeps what it showed instead of clearing the line wrongly. Every signed-in screen (nickname, steps 1 to 4) asks it every 30 s (`STATUS_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`) while the tab is visible; hiding the tab or leaving the signed-in screens (signing out) stops it, showing the tab starts it again; a 401 or an error changes nothing. The page shows `PHASE_3_TEXTS.workshopPaused` ("The workshop is paused for a moment.") in one line under the title on the signed-in screens, a `role="status"` element, and hides it at the next read without `paused`. So every page shows and clears it within 30 s (pages that poll every 2 s, sooner). Cost: one small `SETTINGS` read per visible signed-in tab every 30 s (about 2 reads a second for 60 people).
- **(3.5) The 3.4 repair gap closed:** the check's slot repair treats DynamoDB's `TransactionConflictException` (the repair met a running claim transaction) as a refused repair (`false`, counted in `repairMisses` like a failed condition), and recording that miss ignores the same exception, instead of aborting that minute's check. Tested on the request stubs.
- **(3.5) Rights:** the admin function may write `stopped`, `stoppedAt` and `resumedAt` on `SETTINGS` (`SETTINGS_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES`); every `SETTINGS` reader may read `resumedAt`; the AI worker may `sqs:SendMessage` to its own queue (the hold); the launcher may remove `feed` from a build (the requeue; `LAUNCHER_BUILD_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES`). Residual: IAM's `dynamodb:Attributes` cannot tell a `REMOVE` from a `SET`, so the launcher role could also SET `feed`; only the launcher's own code writes it that way (never), and the page renders the feed with `textContent` only. The launch queue keeps the AI worker as its only sender (synth test unchanged). `GET /api/status` (try 2) is one more route on the conversation function, which already reads `SETTINGS`: no new right. No change to the access stack: the boundary already allows each of these, and the launcher's `ecs:StopTask` already existed.
- **(3.6) Step 4 is the step `building` with a live build (please veto):** the profile's `Step` type is unchanged: change messages are stored at step `building`, and `POST /api/conversation/messages` takes one only when the caller's current build (`conversation.buildId`) is `live`, or `failed` when it was a change build. A queued, building or deploying build answers 409 "Your app is being built right now. You can ask for another change once it is live." (`PHASE_3_TEXTS.changeWhileBuilding`); no build yet, or a failed first build (the facilitator's retry), answers 409 "You can ask for changes once your app is live." (`changeBeforeLive`). With all changes used the route answers 409 "You've used your 5 changes." (reason `no-changes-left`) and the worker, should a message still arrive, stores the same text (reply kind `limit`, message `failed`) without an AI call.
- **(3.6) The change conversation (please veto):** an on-path change gets one model call (prompt `change-v1`, the spec prompt's format and rules shared through `SPEC_FORMAT_AND_RULES`, so `spec-v2` is byte-identical) with the live spec (`approvedSpecVersion`, the version last sent to build) and, while one is open, the proposed version. The answer holds a few words, the whole next spec and a `<changes>` block of 1 to 5 lines (kept at most 5, each one line of at most 160 characters). The version is stored as the next `SPEC#nnnn` with `whatChanges`, and the conversation's `proposedSpecVersion` points at it; `GET /api/conversation/spec` returns `whatChanges`. A later message refines the open proposal (a new version again), and the list always compares with the live spec. A question gets an answer without a spec; a cut-off block, a spec of the wrong shape or a spec without its list fails the message with the neutral reply and changes nothing.
- **(3.6) Build this change and Not this one (please veto):** `POST /api/conversation/change/build` and `POST /api/conversation/change/discard`, body `{ "version": n }` (JSON, 415 otherwise; 400 for a bad body). A version that is not the caller's open proposal answers 404 (so another participant's version, or a stale one, is a 404). Build this change stores the message "Please build this change." marked `request: "change-build"` (an attribute only this route writes) and approves the version (`approvedAt`, so the unchanged launcher accepts it) in one transaction conditional on the proposal, the current build and nothing in flight, then queues the AI job and answers 202. The worker recognises the job by that mark plus the exact text at step `building`, never by the text alone. It runs the readiness check on the proposed version; when ready, one transaction stores the reply, makes the version the live one (`approvedSpecVersion`, `buildId`), clears the proposal, counts the change and creates the build (`kind: "change"`, `changeNumber`, `specVersion`, id from `buildIdFor(message id)` so a redelivery re-sends the same build). Not ready, a failed check, the budget or the limit keep the proposal open with their reply. Not this one removes `proposedSpecVersion` and keeps the version item as history; version numbers keep rising.
- **(3.6) Counting a change (please veto):** `changeRequestsUsed` goes up only in the transaction that queues the change build: a SET to the value read plus one, conditional on that exact value and on being under the cap (`changeRequestsPerPerson`, 5 by default), with a `ConditionCheckItem` that the build it follows is `live` or `failed`. Declined, flagged, abandoned, not-ready and failed requests write nothing to it, and the facilitator's retry requeues the same build without touching the profile (the requeue keeps `kind`, `changeNumber` and `specVersion`). Only the AI worker may write `changeRequestsUsed` (synth test).
- **(3.6) A failed change build (please veto):** the version sent to build stays `approvedSpecVersion`, so the next change starts from that failed version (the participant asked for it), while the app still serves the last live build. Retry (facilitator) builds it again without counting. Known limit, for 3.7/3.8: there is no "go back to the live spec" yet.
- **(3.6) Suggested changes (please veto):** generated once per participant from the spec of the first live build, by one model call (prompt `suggestions-v1`, temperature 0.7, strict JSON, each at most 60 characters asked, exactly 3 kept, each 3 to 79 characters, all different; anything else is unreadable and stores nothing). They are asked lazily: `GET /api/conversation` or `GET /api/build` on a live app marks `suggestionsRequestedAt` (conditional: none stored and none asked in the last 10 minutes) and sends a suggestions job to the AI queue; the worker checks Stop (hold, 30 s), the budget, then stores `suggestions` only if none exist. They are never refreshed. `GET /api/conversation` returns them at step `building`, with `changeRequestsUsed` and `changeRequestsPerPerson`. The eval (`npm run eval -- suggestions`) runs the 4 workshop example specs of `readiness.json` (a superset of the criterion's 3) and checks each suggestion with the real guardrail.
- **(3.6) Interim until 3.7:** a change build is launched and built like a first build (template plus the new spec); 3.7 changes the builder to start from the live code. The launcher is unchanged. (Replaced by "(3.7) A change build starts from `main`".)
- **(3.6) Rights:** the conversation function may write `proposedSpecVersion`, `request` and `suggestionsRequestedAt`, and names `suggestions` in a condition (residual: IAM cannot tell a name used in a condition from a write; only the worker's code writes the list). The AI worker may write `whatChanges`, `proposedSpecVersion`, `changeRequestsUsed` and `suggestions` in participants, `kind`, `changeNumber` and `specVersion` on new builds, and `ConditionCheckItem` on `BUILD#*` naming only `pk`, `sk`, `status`. Two new routes on the conversation function. No change to the access stack. Residuals (access review): IAM cannot require `attribute_not_exists(pk)`, so the AI worker role could also change `kind`, `changeNumber` and `specVersion` on an existing build (only its code's create-only writes do); the ready transaction accepts a failed previous build and leaves the "failed first build is not live" rule and the cap with a missing count to the worker's own checks; `approvedAt` stays on a version whose readiness check failed, so only `approvedSpecVersion` ever means "passed the check". To revisit in the release security review.
- **(3.7) A change build starts from `main` (please veto):** for a build with `kind: "change"` the launcher no longer calls `prepareRepository`: it reads `main`'s current commit with a one-repository token (`readRepositoryMain`, checked against `^[0-9a-f]{40}$`, nothing written to the repository) and passes it as `BUILDER_BASE_COMMIT`; the runner still fails unless `main` equals it when it starts ("The app's repository changed after the build was started..."). A repository without `main` fails the build ("GitHub: The app's repository has no main branch to change."). A change build whose `specVersion` is not the conversation's `approvedSpecVersion`, or that has no `specVersion` or `changeNumber`, is ignored like any other mismatch. A first build (and a rebuild after `conversation reset`) still starts from the template. This replaces "(3.6) Interim until 3.7".
- **(3.7) The change's input travels on the build (please veto):** the launcher reads the version's text and `whatChanges` from that participant's `SPEC#<specVersion>` (only an approved version with a non-empty list; otherwise "The approved spec could not be found") and copies them onto the build as `changeSpec` and `changeList` (one conditional write while the build is `building` under this launch without a task), before it starts the task. The builder task reads them with its build (`BUILDER_BUILD_READ_ATTRIBUTES` gains `kind`, `changeNumber`, `changeSpec`, `changeList`), so its role still reads nothing outside `BUILD#*` (a read of `SPEC#` items would have let the role next to the AI agent read every participant's messages, which share the `text` attribute). Only the launcher may write the two attributes (synth test). A change build missing any of number, text or list fails with the service reason before the agent runs.
- **(3.7) What the agent gets (please veto):** the workspace is the repository at `main` with `SPEC.md` replaced by the new version (email-shaped text removed, as in every published `SPEC.md`); the prompt (`changePrompt`) names the change's number and its "What changes" list (one line each through `plainLineWithoutEmails`, at most 200 characters), says the app is already built (AGENTS.md's "replace the starter" does not apply), and asks for the smallest edits that make the app match `SPEC.md`, keeping every other screen, label, style and stored data key. After the agent, the builder writes the approved `SPEC.md` back before the deploy check, so the commit always holds the approved version.
- **(3.7) The change commit (please veto):** one commit on top of `main`, pushed without force, holding the whole app: "Change <n>: <summary>", where `n` is the build's `changeNumber` (1 for a participant's first change, so the number matches the count the step 4 page shows: the brief's "Change 2: add a weekly chart" is a participant's second change, with "Changes left: 3 of 5" after it) and the summary is the first non-empty line of the "What changes" list after `plainLineWithoutEmails` (control and format characters out, then email-shaped text removed; see "(3.7) Plain lines for the prompt and the commit"), a leading "- " dropped, the whole message at most 72 characters (code points, cut on a word with "..."), "update the app to the new spec" when the list gives nothing. This is the first commit message with text derived from a participant's request (AI-written, guardrail-checked, emails removed); every other commit keeps its fixed message. A change where the agent changed nothing but `SPEC.md` fails with "The AI did not change the app, so there was nothing to publish."
- **(3.7) A failed change build:** the runner pushes only after the agent and the deploy check passed, and sends the deploy message only after storing `pushedCommit`, so a failure before the push leaves `main`, the deployer and the live build's record as they were (tested); the deployer is unchanged. A failure after the push (the hand-over or the deploy) leaves the change's commit on `main` while the app still serves the last live build, as for first builds; the next change starts from `main` (matches "(3.6) A failed change build").
- **(3.7) The change prompt's wording (3.6 checker note):** `change-v1` called the live spec "the app was built from it", which is wrong after a failed change build. `change-v2` says "the live spec below is the version they last sent to build" and labels it "The live spec (the spec last sent to build)"; nothing else in the prompt changed.
- **(3.7) Email addresses in a change's input (access review of try 1):** the launcher removes email-shaped text (`removeEmailAddresses`) from the version's text and makes every "What changes" line one plain line without email addresses (`plainLineWithoutEmails`) before `setChangeInput`, so `changeSpec` and `changeList` on `BUILD#` hold none, also when a control character sits inside an address (`ada@example<U+0001>.org`, `ada<U+0000>@example.org`; try 3, tested); the runner's `changePrompt` cleans each line again (`plainLineWithoutEmails`) as defence in depth (tested). The stored `SPEC#` version's `whatChanges` keeps what the change conversation wrote (only the participant and the platform's trusted functions read it), as the version's `text` already does.
- **(3.7) Plain lines for the prompt and the commit (access review of try 1):** `plainLineWithoutEmails` (`platform/api/github/repository.ts`) removes format characters (`\p{Cf}`: bidi controls, zero-width characters) and lone surrogates, turns tabs and line breaks into spaces, removes every other control character (so `ada@example<U+0001>.org` becomes an address and is caught), collapses spaces, and only then removes email-shaped text. The change commit's summary is the first list line that is not empty after this, cut on whole code points (an emoji at the cut is kept or dropped whole). Not caught: an address split by a space or line break ("ada@example .org", any Unicode space included), which no longer reads as one.
- **(3.7) Read-only token for reading `main` (access review of try 1):** `readRepositoryMain` asks for `contents: read` on the one repository and checks the answer grants exactly that plus `metadata: read` (one repository, `selected`, within an hour); a `contents: write` answer is refused. The builder's token is unchanged (contents write, it pushes).
- **(3.7) Residual: the change input stays on the build (access review of try 1):** `changeSpec` and `changeList` stay on the `BUILD#` record after the build ends, and the builder task role may `GetItem` any `BUILD#` (IAM cannot bind it to its own build). Not cleared by the builder: removing them in its terminal write would add the two names to `BUILDER_BUILD_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES`, and IAM's `dynamodb:Attributes` cannot tell a `REMOVE` from a `SET`, so the role beside the AI agent could then write another running change build's input (a prompt injection into someone else's build), which is worse than the retention. Accepted because the stored text holds no email address (above), `changeSpec` is the same text published as `SPEC.md` in the app's public repository, and `changeList` is the AI-written summary of a published change. To revisit in the release security review (a clean-up by a trusted writer, e.g. the deployer or the scheduled check, would remove it).
- **(3.7) Residual: the base commit is `main`, not the live build's `pushedCommit` (access review of try 1):** a change build starts from whatever `main` holds when the launcher reads it, not from the last live build's `pushedCommit`. Kept on purpose: a failed change whose commit was pushed (the hand-over or the deploy failed) must be the next change's base, per "(3.7) A failed change build". Only the platform's builder token (one repository, one hour, handed to the build's own task) can move `main`, and the runner fails when `main` moved after the launch.
- **(3.7) The change eval (please veto):** `npm run eval -- change` builds the fitness example (Run Log) with the real runner and agent, asks the real change conversation (`answerChange`, `change-v2`) for "Please add a weekly chart of the distance I ran each week.", then runs the change build from the resulting `main` with the new version and list. It passes when both builds pass the build eval's checks (deploy check, 375 px, no console error, no sideways scroll, the new spec's title and screens), every screen of the previous spec is still shown, and the change is one commit "Change 1: ..." whose `SPEC.md` is the new version. It prints the time, tokens and cost of the first build, the change call and the change build.
- **(3.8) Step 4's layout (please veto):** top to bottom: "Your app is live", a failed change build's notice (when there is one), the address, QR code and repository link (as in 2.11), then "Ask for a change": "Changes left: 3 of 5", one line on how a change works, the three suggestions, the change conversation, the spec update, the box (label "Describe a change") with **Send**, the AI budget line, and last the "Behind the scenes" box. The 2.11 line "Next: you will be able to ask for changes..." is gone. No API change: the page uses only what 3.6 and 3.7 answer.
- **(3.8) Suggestions (please veto):** each is a full-width button that puts its text in the box and moves the focus there; nothing is sent until **Send** (the participant can edit it first). They are hidden while a proposal is open (a message then adjusts the proposal) and once every change is used. While none are stored yet (the AI writes them a few seconds after the app first goes live) and changes are left, step 4 reads `GET /api/conversation` again every 5 s, at most 12 times per visit (one minute), only while step 4 shows and nothing is in flight.
- **(3.8) The spec update (please veto):** a box headed "Spec update" with "Version n, not built yet", the "What changes" list (at most 5 lines, as stored), the whole proposed spec as text under a fold ("Read the whole updated spec": title and sections, no diagram), the buttons **Build this change** (filled) and **Not this one** (outlined), and the line "Not quite right? Write what to adjust in the box below and press Send: the AI updates this proposal." A new proposal after a reply is scrolled into view and focused. While a message is in flight both buttons and Send are off; during the readiness check the typing line says "The AI is checking your change…" (recognised by the request's text, `CHANGE_BUILD_REQUEST`, now kept in `platform/api/workshop/texts.ts` and re-exported by `change.ts`).
- **(3.8) After Build this change (please veto):** once the request's reply arrives (and after any reply or lost message on step 4), the page reads `GET /api/build` first: a queued or running build shows step 3's feed, with "Change n: the AI is changing your app to match your updated spec. Your current version stays online until the new one is live." in place of the first build's introduction and a change build's "Behind the scenes" text (it starts from the repository, one commit); a live build (or not ready: the reply says why, the proposal stays) shows step 4 with the conversation read again. When the change build is live, step 4 comes back with the count from the API.
- **(3.8) A failed change build on the page (please veto):** the page goes back to step 4 (no stop on step 3's failure screen) with a notice at the top: "Change n could not be built, so your app still shows the version before it. Your facilitator can try that build again, or you can ask for another change." and the reasons as a text list. It stays while that failed build is the current one (a reload shows it too). The count is the API's: the change counted when its build was queued (decision "(3.6) Counting a change"); a failed first build keeps step 3's failure screen.
- **(3.8) All changes used, and the refusals (please veto):** with changes used equal to the cap, step 4 shows "Changes left: 0 of 5" and "You've used your 5 changes." (`changesUsedText`), with no box, no suggestions and no proposal. A change request answered 409 `no-changes-left` shows the API's words and reads the conversation again (the box goes); `build-running` or `not-live` shows the words and reads the build (step 3 when one runs). Build this change or Not this one answered 404 (not the open proposal any more, e.g. another tab) shows "This proposed change is no longer open. The page now shows where your changes stand." and reads the conversation again. Declined and flagged change requests look as in step 1 (the reply in the conversation; "waiting for your facilitator" with Send off).
