- **(2.6) Opening refused (try 3):** a 422 to the opening `PUT` (README.md is there already and GitHub asks for its blob id) means the repository is not empty: `main` is read again, this time with the retries of "(2.6) Retry" (step `get-branch`), and handled as for a found `main` (a lone opening commit is replaced, anything else gets a rebuild commit on top). A `main` still not found then fails with GitHub's status for `get-branch`. This also covers an opening commit GitHub made but answered 404 or 409 to.
- **(2.6) A commit answer without a parents list (try 3):** `main`'s commit is taken for a lone opening commit only when its answer holds a parents list that is empty; an answer without one is treated as having parents (kept, rebuild on top).
- **(2.6) For 2.7 to 2.9: where the nickname comes from.** The nickname passed to `builderToken` and `prepareRepository` must come from the stored build record or the participant's profile (read by the caller with its own session or the record's key), never from a request body, a query string or a queue message's fields: the nickname decides which repository a token reaches.
- **(2.6) For 2.9: one preparation at a time.** The forced update of `main` (only over a lone, parentless opening commit) is not compare-and-swap: GitHub's ref `PATCH` cannot name the expected old commit. The launcher runs at most one `prepareRepository` per participant at a time, and starts the builder only after it has returned, so no push can land between the check and the forced update. Unverified live: whether GitHub's Contents API can answer a parentless commit while `main` exists in a brief inconsistent state (the code trusts GitHub's own answer).
- **(2.7) Readiness request as a message:** Approve spec stores, in its own transaction, a participant message with the platform's fixed text "I approve my spec. Please check that it is ready to build." (`READINESS_REQUEST`) at step `approved` as the conversation's message in flight, and queues its ids on the existing AI queue. The AI worker runs the readiness check (not the guardrail and answer calls) for exactly that text at that step (nobody can send a message at step `approved`), so the existing at-least-once rules, the page's polling of the message in flight and the reply in the conversation all work unchanged. The approve answer adds the message `id`.
- **(2.7) Readiness verdict:** prompt `readiness-v1` (`platform/api/conversation/readiness.ts`) sees only the approved spec, between `<spec>` tags (tags inside removed), at temperature 0, at most 600 tokens, and answers strict JSON `{"verdict":"ready"|"not-ready","reasons":[...]}`; the worker parses it and stores only the reasons (one line each, control characters removed, at most 5 of at most 400 characters). Not ready: one to three reasons, each naming login, payment, outside service, server or open question. "Server" (a program running when nobody has the page open, a database of its own) is checked too, as the brief's "no server" asks.
- **(2.7) No verdict, no build:** an answer that is not that JSON (or `not-ready` without a reason), a Bedrock error, the deadline or a missing spec ends the request as `failed` with "Sorry, the check of your spec did not work this time. Please press Approve spec again." and sends the participant back to step 2 with the approval cleared (as a failed check); the tokens of a call that answered are counted.
- **(2.7) Outcome in one transaction (`finishReadiness`):** the message `pending` to `done` (`failed` without a verdict), the reply (`kind` `answer`, with prompt version, model, tokens; `failure` without a verdict), the conversation (condition: this message in flight and this version approved; message in flight removed), the profile's step (condition `step = approved`) and the usage `ADD`. Pass: step `building`, the conversation's `buildId`, and the build `BUILD#<id>`/`BUILD` created by `UpdateItem` only if absent (`type: "build"`, `buildId`, `nickname` exactly as stored on the profile, `participantId`, `status: "queued"`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`). Fail: step `spec` and `approvedSpecVersion` removed. The final write is tried 3 times; after that the check's tokens are added on their own and the error is thrown (as ticket 1.7).
- **(2.7) One build per approval, by conditions:** the build id is the readiness message id with `:` and `.` turned into `-` (`buildIdFor`, e.g. `2026-11-24T10-00-00-000Z-0101010101010101`, matches `BUILD_ID_PATTERN`), so a duplicate delivery names the same build, whose create is conditional on its absence; the profile's `step = approved` and the conversation's message-in-flight conditions let only one outcome land; two approvals at once let only one approval transaction land (the other answers 200 like a second press, since the conversation then holds that version). Tested with a redelivery, two parallel deliveries, two parallel approvals and approve pressed after the pass.
- **(2.7) Launch queue:** FIFO `ssd-workshop-builds.fifo` in the builds stack (SQS-managed encryption, content-based deduplication off, 1 day retention, 3 deliveries, then the FIFO dead-letter queue `ssd-workshop-builds-dlq.fifo`, 14 days; visibility 330 s until 2.9 sets it just above the launcher's timeout). Body exactly `{"buildId": "<id>"}`; deduplication id the build id; message group the participant id (one participant's launches one at a time, which "(2.6) For 2.9: one preparation at a time" needs). The AI worker is the only sender (`sqs:SendMessage` on the queue's fixed ARN; synth test checks every policy); 2.9 adds the consumer. No cross-stack reference: the api stack names the queue by its ARN.
- **(2.7) Send after the transaction:** the build id is sent after the outcome is stored. A failed send is thrown, so SQS delivers the readiness job again; the redelivery finds the message `done` with the conversation holding that build and only sends the build id again (outcome `resent`), with no second check and no second build. Every later redelivery of that job re-sends too: SQS drops a duplicate within 5 minutes, and **for 2.9** the launcher must act only on a build that is still `queued` (conditional update) and treat any other message as a duplicate. A send that fails on all 3 deliveries leaves the build `queued` with the job in the AI queue's dead-letter queue: 2.9's stuck-build check (or `npm run builds -- retry`) must also cover a build left `queued`.
- **(2.7) Approve again:** after a failed check the participant is at step 2 with the approval removed; the same version (or a newer one) can be approved again, which runs a new check (the spec's `approvedAt` stays as history, and `GET /api/conversation/spec` reports `approved` only while the conversation still holds that version). At step `approved` with the same version and nothing in flight (an approval from before 2.7, or a check whose job was lost and went stale), Approve spec asks for the check again (`requestReadiness`: condition this version approved, no `buildId`, nothing in flight or only a stale message); at step `building` it answers 200 and does nothing. If the AI queue refuses the job, the approve route ends the request at once as `failed` (back to step 2, the neutral reply) and answers 500.
- **(2.7) `GET /api/build`:** in the conversation function (its role now reads `BUILD#*`), behind `requireSession`. It reads the caller's own conversation for `buildId`, then the build, and answers only when the build's `participantId` is the caller: `{ buildId, status }`, plus `failureReasons` when `failed`. Otherwise (no build yet, another's build) 404 "Not found.". 2.10 extends the answer with the feed.
- **(2.7) Step `building`:** a new `Step` value (types, session check, the conversation's prompt choice treats it like step 2); sends are refused there like at `approved`; the page shows the step 2 screen with "Your spec is ready and the build is starting: the AI now turns your spec into your app." until 2.10's step 3 screen. `npm run conversation -- reset` accepts a participant at `building` ("step 3 (Build)"): the conversation goes as before, the build record stays (it is not the conversation's), and a new approval makes a new build in the same repository (2.6).
- **(2.7) Page:** the approved notice now reads "Spec approved. Next comes the build: first the AI checks that your spec is ready to build (no login, payments or outside services, and no open questions). This takes a few seconds." while the check runs, with the typing line "The AI is checking your spec…"; the existing 2-second polling of the message in flight picks up the outcome. A failed check's reasons show as the AI's reply (plain text) and Approve spec shows again.
- **(2.7) Widened AI worker rights (please veto if unwanted):** the worker may now name `step`, `buildId` and `approvedSpecVersion` in `PARTICIPANT#*` (each write conditional on step `approved` with that version approved and the readiness message in flight) and `UpdateItem` in `BUILD#*` with only `pk, sk, type, buildId, nickname, participantId, status, createdAt, updatedAt` (`AI_WORKER_BUILD_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES`, ReturnValues limited). It still never names `approvedAt`, `approvedTextHash`, `role`, `group`, `nickname` (profile), `id`, nor the deployer's or builder's build attributes. The conversation function's list gains `buildId` (only compared, in `requestReadiness`). The tests that said "the worker never writes `step` or `approvedSpecVersion`" now say this.
- **(2.7) Model:** the readiness check uses the one `MODEL_ID` (the EU profile every role uses today) and its prices; the per-role model table and `--model` for evals ("Models per role") are not built yet (no eval takes `--model` today) and stay for a later ticket.
- **(2.7) Eval:** `npm run eval -- readiness` runs `platform/api/eval/readiness.json`: the three workshop example specs (run log, movie watchlist, calorie counter; written for this eval, since no example spec was stored) must be `ready`; a login, a payment, two outside services (live weather API, email reminders), an open question and a night-time server must be `not-ready` with a reason containing one of the example's keywords. Live runs 2026-11-24: 9/9 passed (7,300 tokens in, 553 out, $0.0332), and 9/9 again after reasons were capped at 40 words (7,363 in, 460 out, $0.0319).
- **(2.7) For 2.9 (checker and access review, try 1):** the AI worker role's `UpdateItem` on `BUILD#*` cannot be limited by IAM to creating (no `attribute_not_exists` condition key), so code running as that role could change `status`, `nickname` or `participantId` on an existing build; only the worker's own code holds the role (accepted, like the deployer's stray `APP#` items). So the 2.9 launcher MUST start a build only when (1) it moves it from `queued` with a conditional update, (2) its `nickname` equals the stored profile nickname of its `participantId`, and (3) its `buildId` equals that participant's conversation `buildId` (which also skips a build left `queued` by `conversation reset` at `building`). The stuck-build check also moves a build left `queued` after its launch message reached the dead-letter queue to `failed`. Proposed to Franck, not applied: an access-stack deny of `sqs:SendMessage`/`SetQueueAttributes`/`PurgeQueue` on `ssd-workshop-builds*` for Claude sessions, like `NoLauncherForSessions` (the launch queue becomes the launcher's trigger in 2.9).
- **(2.7) Readiness eval keywords (try 1):** each failing example passes only on a reason naming its problem (`login`/`sign in`, `payment`/`pay`, `outside service`/`API`/`external`, `server`/`background`/`schedule`, `open question`/`decide`/`unclear`), not on words that merely echo the spec (`account`, `card`, `email`, `night`). Spec tags are removed in any letter case and form before the check.
- **(2.8) Builder layout:** `platform/builder/` (`runner.ts` the flow, `agent.ts` the Claude Agent SDK run, `permissions.ts` the tool check, `records.ts` the build record, `repository.ts` the commits, `queue.ts` the deploy message, `main.ts` the task's entry point). Job from the environment: `BUILD_ID` and `BUILDER_GITHUB_TOKEN` (deleted from `process.env` as soon as it is read, which keeps it out of later copies of the environment; the agent never gets it because its environment is an explicit allow-list, and `/proc/<pid>/environ` still holds the original, out of the agent's reach only because its file tools are confined to the workspace); nickname and participant come from the stored build only. Exit code 1 only when the build failed; the process calls `process.exit` with the code as soon as the outcome is recorded (`exitWhenDone`), so a stray child process cannot keep the Fargate task alive.
- **(2.8) New dependency `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk`:** the ticket's coding agent is the Claude Agent SDK (its file tools, edit loop and hooks); writing that loop on the plain Bedrock API would be more code and less tested.
- **(2.8) Agent's tools and permissions:** tools `Read`, `Write`, `Edit`, `Glob` only (no `Grep`, which shells out to ripgrep), every other built-in in `disallowedTools`; `permissionMode: "dontAsk"`, `settingSources: []`, `strictMcpConfig` with no MCP servers, no skills, plugins or subagents, `persistSession: false`, at most 120 turns. A PreToolUse hook (`onFailure: "block"`) runs the deny-by-default check, and is the only gate (the SDK never consults `canUseTool` for tools in `allowedTools`, so it is not set): a path must resolve (realpath of the nearest existing folder; a dangling link is refused) inside the workspace's real path, with no `..`, `~`, backslash or NUL; Glob patterns and folders must be plain (try 2): any `{`, `}`, `[`, `]`, any `..` substring, a leading `/` or `~` is refused (`.{.,}/*`, `[.][.]/*` and `{/etc,x}/*` got through try 1), so `*.{js,css}` is refused too and the agent globs twice; try 3 also refuses a pattern segment made only of `.`, `?` and `*` that holds a dot and a wildcard (`.?`, `.*`, `*.*`, `?.`: each could match `.` or `..`), so `.*` for hidden files is refused too (an app needs none). `AGENTS.md`, `SPEC.md` and `workshop-data.js` may be read but not written. Tool and CI settings may not be written either (try 2): any path with a `.git`, `.github` or `.claude` folder at any depth, and any file named `CLAUDE.md`, `.mcp.json` or `.gitattributes` in any folder, case ignored (stricter than the first segment only: an app needs none of them).
- **(2.8) Agent's environment (try 2):** built from an allow-list, never `process.env`: `PATH` only from the runner's environment, a fresh temporary `HOME`/`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK=1`, `ANTHROPIC_BEDROCK_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:<port>/<prefix>` (the Bedrock proxy below, with its run's secret path prefix since try 3), `CLAUDE_CODE_SKIP_BEDROCK_AUTH=1`, `AWS_REGION=eu-central-1`, every model alias (`ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, small-fast, haiku, sonnet, opus) set to the build model so nothing calls another model, and nonessential traffic, autoupdater, telemetry and error reporting off. No AWS credential, profile or `AWS_CONTAINER_CREDENTIALS_*` variable, no proxy or CA variable, no GitHub token, no Anthropic key or base URL (`agent.test.ts`: `AWS_REGION` is the only `AWS_` name).
- **(2.8) Bedrock proxy (try 2, access review):** `platform/builder/bedrock-proxy.ts`, started by `ClaudeAgent` for each run on 127.0.0.1 (random port) and closed after it, in the task and in the eval alike. It forwards only `POST /model/<url-encoded build model id>/invoke` and `/invoke-with-response-stream` (exact path, no query) to `https://bedrock-runtime.eu-central-1.amazonaws.com`, SigV4-signed (service `bedrock`) with the runner's own credentials (`defaultProvider()` of `@aws-sdk/credential-provider-node`: the task role on Fargate, the caller's keys in the eval), passing on only `content-type` and `accept`, and streams the answer back; anything else gets 403, a body over 20 MB 413, an unreachable Bedrock 502. Logs: method, path shape (`/model/<build>/...`, `/model/<other>/...` or the first segment), outcome and status, never a header or body; the task logs only refused and failed calls. The live run showed what Claude Code calls: `POST /model/<build>/invoke-with-response-stream` for every turn, and one `GET /inference-profiles` at start (a control-plane listing, refused with 403; Claude Code carries on without it). `count-tokens` and `converse` stay refused.
- **(2.8) New direct dependencies (try 2):** `@smithy/signature-v4` (SigV4 signing for the proxy) and `@aws-sdk/credential-provider-node` (the SDK's default credential chain), both already in the lock file as dependencies of the AWS SDK clients, same versions; SHA-256 is `node:crypto` (`NodeSha256`), not `@aws-crypto/sha256-js` (not installed).
- **(2.8) Residual risk of the proxy:** on Fargate the task's credential endpoint (169.254.170.2) is reachable by any process in the task, the agent's included, and any local process can call the proxy. The agent has no network tool (no shell, no fetch: tools Read, Write, Edit, Glob behind the path check) and its process gets no `AWS_CONTAINER_CREDENTIALS_*` variable, so it has no way to ask for or use credentials; the proxy itself allows only the build model's two invoke calls.
- **(2.8) Models per role (started):** `MODELS` in `platform/api/settings.ts` holds the build role only (`eu.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6`, EU prices 3.3 / 16.5 / 0.33 / 4.125 $ per million for input, output, cache read, cache write); the other roles keep `MODEL_ID` until they move.
- **(2.8) Clone:** the repository's tarball at `main`'s commit (codeload, through the deployer's capped `downloadArchive` and `readTarGz`), written into a fresh temporary folder; an entry that is not a plain file or folder fails the build.
- **(2.8) Feed:** one `feed` list attribute on the `BUILD#` item (`{kind: "step", step, at}`, `{kind: "file", path, at}`, `{kind: "commit", sha, message, at}`), appended with `list_append`; steps `preparing`, `writing`, `checking`, `committing`, `deploying`; each written file once, at most 200 file events. Paths and failure reasons are stored through `storedReason` with email-shaped text replaced by `[email removed]`.
- **(2.8) Build record writes:** claim (`queued` or `building`, nickname equal, no `pushedCommit`) sets `building` and a new feed; every later write is conditional on `building`; the hand-over sets `pushedCommit` and the `deploying` step but leaves status `building` (the deployer moves it to `deploying`); failure sets `failed`, `failureReasons`, `finishedAt`. Attribute names for 2.9's IAM (try 2, split): `BUILDER_BUILD_READ_ATTRIBUTES` (GetItem: `pk`, `sk`, `nickname`, `participantId`, `status`), `BUILDER_BUILD_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` (UpdateItem: exactly the names in the update and condition expressions, `participantId` not among them) and `BUILDER_USAGE_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` in `infra/lib/config.ts`; `aws.test.ts` parses the requests' expressions and checks each list both ways.
- **(2.8) Timeout:** 12 minutes for the agent (`BUILD_AGENT_TIMEOUT_MS`), an `AbortController` plus a race, so an agent that ignores the signal is still cut off; reason "The build took too long." Other plain reasons: "The AI could not finish building the app this time." and a reason for nothing changed, unsupported repository entries, a failed hand-over and a service error.
- **(2.8) Check before commit:** the deploy check (`checkApp`) runs on the workspace before anything is committed; a failing check fails the build with its reasons and commits nothing. Nothing changed by the agent also fails the build.
- **(2.8) Commits:** through the Git Data API with the builder identity, one full tree per commit and one non-forced ref update at the end: "Build the app's page and its look (HTML, CSS and images)" then "Add what the app does (JavaScript) and update its notes"; when one group is empty, a single "Build the app from the approved spec". With the template's first commit, the repository always holds at least two commits.
- **(2.8) Deploy message:** `ssd-workshop-deploys.fifo`, body exactly `{buildId, nickname, commit}`, deduplication id = build id, group = lower-cased nickname; tried 3 times, then the build fails with a plain reason.
- **(2.8) Usage:** the agent's tokens come from the result's `modelUsage`, or the per-answer usage (each message id counted once) when the run is cut off; `inputTokens` includes cache reads and writes, cost uses the build role's prices (cache writes at the 5-minute rate); one `ADD` on the participant's `USAGE` item; a failed usage write is logged and does not fail the build. The builder task adds them itself (Franck, 2026-11-24, decision card in the 2.8 thread; the alternative was the builder recording them on the build and 2.9's launcher adding them). Residual risk for 2.9: IAM cannot pin `sk = USAGE` (LeadingKeys covers `pk` only), so the 2.9 role, limited to `PARTICIPANT#*` and the usage attribute names, could also touch those counter names on a participant's other items (`PROFILE`, `MSG#...`); readers ignore attributes of unexpected types.
- **(2.8) Logs:** one JSON line per phase with counts only (files, commits, tokens, cost); never a token, nickname, build id, path or error message.
- **(2.8) Deployer tie to the push (2.5 must-do):** the deployer's start condition adds `#pushedCommit = :commit` and `pushedCommit` is in `DEPLOYER_BUILD_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES`; a message whose commit is not the pushed one (or a build with none) is dropped unpublished.
- **(2.8) Fake GitHub:** serves codeload tarballs of its commits and checks a fast-forward through all ancestors (not only direct parents), as GitHub does.
- **(2.8) Eval:** `npm run eval -- build [--model <eu.anthropic.* profile>] [--example <n>]` (`--model`/`--example` for build only; another model is priced at the build role's prices) runs the readiness file's three workshop example specs through the real runner and agent (through the Bedrock proxy, so the caller's keys never reach the agent either) with GitHub, the table and the queue in memory, then the deploy check on the pushed commit and Chromium at 375 x 740 under the apps' CSP with an in-memory data API. Pass: handed over, steps in order, a commit, check passed, no console error, nothing scrolling sideways, the `<title>` holds the spec's title and every `## Screens` item name appears in a heading, button, tab, link, label, legend, summary or `aria-label` (hidden ones count, case and spacing ignored).
- **(2.8) Template rule for screen names:** `templates/basic/AGENTS.md` rule 2 now asks for each screen to carry the name `SPEC.md` lists under "Screens" in a heading, tab or button. The first live eval run built a working Run Log whose home screen had no "Home" label (the title check failed); the agent had never been asked to name screens.
- **(2.8) Live eval runs (2026-11-24, `--example 1`, Run Log, `eu.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6`):** before the template rule: handed over, 2 commits, check passed, no console error, no overflow, but "missing Home" (1m17s, 103,359 tokens in, 8,818 out, $0.2536). After it: PASS (1m32s, 106,978 in, 9,772 out, $0.2672). A full `npm run eval -- build` on try 1's code (2026-11-24, `eu.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6`) passed 3/3: "1 fitness tracker (example)" 2m00s, 122,501 tokens in, 12,680 out, $0.3388; "2 movie tracker (example)" 1m57s, 128,694 in, 12,740 out, $0.3473; "3 nutrition calculator (example)" 1m31s, 111,016 in, 9,867 out, $0.2803; 5m28s and $0.9664 in all. The eval prints each example's number and its `name` from `platform/api/eval/readiness.json`.
- **(2.8) Live eval run through the Bedrock proxy (2026-11-24, try 2, `--example 1`, Run Log, `eu.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6`):** PASS: 3 files written, 2 commits, check passed, no console error, title and all screens shown (1m09s, 77,850 tokens in, 7,279 out, $0.2056); 8 calls forwarded (all `invoke-with-response-stream`, 200), one `GET /inference-profiles` refused.
- **(2.8) Bedrock proxy hardening (try 3, checker):** mainly for the eval on a developer machine, where any browser page could POST to 127.0.0.1. The proxy now refuses with 403 a request whose `Host` is not exactly `127.0.0.1:<port>` (DNS rebinding; the server is created with `requireHostHeader: false` so a missing Host gets the same 403, not Node's 400), one carrying an `Origin` header, a `content-type` other than exactly `application/json` (no charset, any case refused), an `accept` other than `application/json` or `application/vnd.amazon.eventstream` (absent is accepted and sent on absent, Bedrock's default; Node's `fetch` sends `*/*` unless told, which is refused), and a path without the run's prefix: 16 random bytes in hex (`/<32 hex>`), in `ANTHROPIC_BEDROCK_BASE_URL` only, stripped before the upstream call and never logged (a path without it logs as `<other>`; logs name only known operations and first segments). Upstream gets `content-type: application/json` as a constant. A Bedrock answer other than 2xx keeps its status but its body becomes `{"message":"Bedrock refused or could not answer the request."}` and its headers are dropped (`x-amzn-errortype` included), so no ARN or account id reaches the agent; Claude Code retries on the status alone. At most 4 requests are forwarded at once (`MAX_IN_FLIGHT`), 429 above ("busy" in the logs).
- **(2.8) Runner stops when the build is finished elsewhere (try 3, checker):** a refused feed write (the build is no longer `building`, for example marked failed by the 2.9 stuck-build check or an operator) stops the run at once: from the agent's `onFileWritten` it aborts the agent's controller and the runner stops waiting for it (a third racer beside the time limit), and from a step write (`writing`, `checking`, `committing`, the commit events) it ends the flow; no commit is pushed, no deploy message is sent, nothing more is written on the build (no `markFailed` over the other writer's outcome), the tokens already spent still go to the usage, and the outcome is `no-build` (logged on the phase, exit code 0 as for every outcome but `failed`).
- **(2.8) Live eval run, try 3 (2026-11-24, `--example 1`, Run Log, `eu.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6`):** PASS through the hardened proxy: Claude Code keeps the base URL's path, so the secret prefix stays (no fallback needed), and its calls carry `content-type: application/json` and an accepted `accept`: 8 `invoke-with-response-stream` calls forwarded (200), one `GET /inference-profiles` refused (403, under the prefix). 3 files written, 2 commits, check passed, no console error, title and all screens shown (1m36s, 96,212 tokens in, 12,571 out, $0.3223).
- **(2.8) Final live eval (2026-11-24, full `npm run eval -- build` on 3a34ac8, the final code before a log line, a test assertion and comments, `eu.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6`):** 3/3 PASS, each 3 files written, 2 commits, check passed, no console error, title and all screens shown: "1 fitness tracker (example)" 2m10s, 131,743 tokens in, 17,857 out, $0.4365; "2 movie tracker (example)" 2m09s, 193,362 in, 15,840 out, $0.4352; "3 nutrition calculator (example)" 2m01s, 128,800 in, 14,288 out, $0.3764; total 6m21s, $1.2481. Measured cost per build: $0.28 to $0.44 (the brief estimated $0.50 to $2).
- **(2.8) Deployer: only the pushed commit, read from the app repository's own main (try 3, replaces try 2's compare call):** after the start condition passes (the message's commit is the build's `pushedCommit`), the deployer downloads `https://codeload.github.com/ssd-workshop-apps/ssd-<lower-cased nickname>/tar.gz/refs/heads/main` (`appMainArchiveUrl`: the fully qualified ref, because a bare `tar.gz/main` would pick a tag named `main` first) with redirects refused (`downloadForDeployer`, `redirect: "error"`: a renamed or transferred repository must not answer) and the same size and unpacking limits. The commit is read ONLY from the archive's first entry, which must be a pax global header (type `g`, GitHub's `pax_global_header`) with exactly one `comment` record matching `^[0-9a-f]{40}$` (`readTarGzArchive`); a `comment` in a per-file header (`x`) or a later `g` is ignored. No such header, or a malformed one, fails the build at once ("GitHub's download of the app's main branch does not name its commit, so nothing was published.", not retried: codeload always writes it, so its absence is not a passing problem). Another commit than the message's (main moved on, or codeload still served its copy from before the push) is a passing problem, retried by SQS and failed on the last delivery ("The app's main branch on GitHub is not at the commit the builder pushed, so nothing was published."); a 404 (GitHub's short-lived one right after a push) is retried the same way with the download's reason. Equal: exactly the checked bytes are published (no second download by commit id). Nothing is published from a commit id, so a fork's commit served through the parent's address can never be published. The anonymous compare API call (60 an hour per shared Lambda address) and its dependency are gone; codeload downloads are not counted against the REST API limit.
- **(2.8) For 2.9 (notes, not implemented in 2.8):** (a) the claim accepts a build already `building` and resets its feed, so the launcher must never start a second task for a build that may still run (or 2.9 adds an attempt id to the claim); (b) a retry of a `failed` build is refused by the claim (`queued` or `building` only): `npm run builds -- retry` must reset the build to `queued` or create a new build; (c) passing `BUILDER_GITHUB_TOKEN` as a RunTask container override exposes it in `ecs:DescribeTasks` and in CloudTrail's RunTask `requestParameters`, so 2.9 must hand it over another way (for example a short-lived record only the task role can read, removed once read) or Franck must accept the risk: ASK FRANCK in 2.9's brief; (d) the task role's statements: `dynamodb:LeadingKeys` `BUILD#*` for builds and `PARTICIPANT#*` for usage, `dynamodb:ReturnValues` limited as the infra rules say (`NONE`), `GetItem` on `BUILD#*` only, with `StringEquals` `dynamodb:Select` = `SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES` (a GetItem without a `ProjectionExpression` returns the whole item, and `dynamodb:Attributes` alone may let it through), and `dynamodb:Attributes` from the split lists (`BUILDER_BUILD_READ_ATTRIBUTES` for GetItem, `BUILDER_BUILD_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` and `BUILDER_USAGE_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` for UpdateItem); Bedrock `InvokeModel` and `InvokeModelWithResponseStream` on the build profile and its foundation models only (the proxy needs nothing else); (e) no longer applies (try 3): the deployer makes no GitHub API call (it reads main's tarball from codeload, see "(2.8) Deployer: only the pushed commit"), so the REST API's anonymous rate limit does not reach it; 2.9's live check should still confirm codeload answers a fresh push within the deliveries (the retried mismatch covers a stale copy).
- **(2.9) Token hand-off (Franck, 2026-11-24, accepted risk; try 2's rewording approved by Franck, 2026-11-24):** the launcher passes the build's one-repository token (`builderToken`, `contents: write` on that participant's repository, one hour) to the task as a RunTask container override `BUILDER_GITHUB_TOKEN`; no one-time token record. The task definition holds no environment and no secret (tested on the synth output), and the override carries only `BUILD_ID`, `BUILDER_GITHUB_TOKEN`, `BUILDER_BASE_COMMIT` and `BUILDER_ATTEMPT_ID` (try 2; neither of the last two is secret), never the App's key or the organization-wide token (tested on the RunTask input). Accepted risk, as it stands after try 3 (all session and boundary denies pending Franck's yes; Franck applies the access stack after merge): who can read the token while it lives is Franck and admins of the shared account (other projects' principals) with `ecs:DescribeTasks` on the builds cluster or CloudTrail `LookupEvents` (RunTask `requestParameters`); no longer Claude sessions (`NoBuildTasksForSessions`), workload roles other than the launcher (`WatchBuildsLauncherOnly`), nor a session borrowing the launcher's role (`NoLauncherForSessions`). Try 2's wording claimed this already, but a session could still borrow the roles the boundary's denies let through by name (pass them to its own task or function, or rewrite the AI worker's code): closed in try 3 ("(2.9) Exception roles kept from sessions"). A holder can push to that one participant repository for the token's hour; that alone does not change the live app (the runner builds only from the prepared commit and its own launch, and the deployer publishes only `pushedCommit` named in a deploy message), unless the holder can also write `pushedCommit` on the build and send a deploy message, which only account admins can (sessions are denied the send, workload roles other than the builder too, and since try 3 sessions can neither pass the builder's role nor run a task on the builds cluster); outside the build pipeline sessions can still change served files through their deploy rights, see "(2.9) Sessions and live apps"). Try 1's wording ("cannot change the live app") was false while sessions could read tasks and send deploy messages (access review HIGH 1). Listed as accepted risk 16 in `infra/access/README.md`, with CloudTrail checks for `DescribeTasks` and `LookupEvents` (Franck's own retry calls appear there).
- **(2.9) Builds queues closed to sessions (Franck, 2026-11-24):** `NoBuildQueueForSessions` in `ssd-workshop-deploy-read` denies the Claude user `sqs:SendMessage`, `sqs:SetQueueAttributes` and `sqs:PurgeQueue` on `ssd-workshop-builds*` (launch queue, its dead-letter queue, the check queue); deploys from `main` still manage them. Retries run from Franck's machine: `AWS_PROFILE=franck npm run builds -- retry <nickname>`. Franck applies the access stack.
- **(2.9) Launcher design:** one function `ssd-workshop-builds-launcher` with two SQS sources (batch 1): the launch queue (`maxConcurrency` 5) and a check queue. A launch claims first: one transaction moves the build `queued` → `building` (condition: still `queued`, nickname and participant as stored) and puts a watch entry `LAUNCHES` / `BUILD#<id>`; the launch happens only when the profile's nickname equals the build's and the conversation's `buildId` equals the build's. Then the approved spec, the App credentials (Secrets Manager, read per launch), `prepareRepository`, `builderToken`, RunTask, and the task ARN on the entry and the build. Any failure after the claim stops a task already started, marks the build failed with a short reason and consumes the message (no second task for the same build); a failure before the claim throws a bare error and SQS retries. The watch partition saves a table scan; the launcher may write in it only `LAUNCHER_LAUNCH_ATTRIBUTES`.
- **(2.9) Stuck-build check every minute (EventBridge Scheduler approved by Franck, 2026-11-24):** EventBridge Scheduler (`ssd-workshop-builds-check`, `rate(1 minute)`) sends `{}` to the standard queue `ssd-workshop-builds-check` (retention 5 minutes), which feeds the launcher: a schedule cannot invoke a Lambda directly (no `lambda:InvokeFunction` in the boundary). Cost: about 44,640 schedule runs and messages a month (one a minute), inside EventBridge Scheduler's free tier (14 million invocations a month) and SQS's (1 million requests a month), so $0 a month. Franck approved EventBridge Scheduler by name on 2026-11-24 (checker 3, try 1, had flagged it); the alternative is an EventBridge rule's schedule, which the access stack's `events:source` denies refuse. The check: a stopped task (or one ECS no longer knows, `MISSING`) whose build is still `building` without `pushedCommit` → failed "The build stopped before it was finished." and the entry removed; an entry with no task after 20 minutes → failed; a task running over 30 minutes → stopped (the next check fails its build); every message in the launch dead-letter queue → its build failed if still `queued`; every message in the deploy dead-letter queue → its build failed if still `building`/`deploying` with that nickname and `pushedCommit`. Dead-letter messages are deleted once handled. **Handed over, no deploy (try 2, checker 1):** a stopped task whose build is still `building` with a `pushedCommit` (the deployer never took its message: lost, or the builder died after the hand-over) stays watched for `HANDED_OVER_GRACE_MS` (20 minutes) after the task's `stoppedAt` (more than twice the deploy message's last delivery, about 7 minutes, tested), then is failed with "The app could not be published because of a problem on the workshop's side." (condition `building`, same task, `pushedCommit` set); a task ECS no longer knows (no stop time) stopped long ago, so it is failed at once. A build the deployer took (`deploying`, or finished) is left alone and unwatched. `retry` recovers it (it removes `pushedCommit`).
- **(2.9) Retry resets the same build:** `npm run builds -- retry <nickname>` finds the participant's current build (nickname → participant → conversation `buildId`), accepts only `failed` or `queued`, refuses while the earlier task still runs (DescribeTasks) or while any task started for the build still runs (try 2: `ListTasks` with `startedBy` = the build id, which RunTask sets; ECS allows no other filter with `startedBy`, and the desired status defaults to `RUNNING`; Franck's own rights, the deploy principals' `ecs:List*`, no new grant), sets it back to `queued` (removing `failureReasons`, `finishedAt`, `pushedCommit`, `deployedCommit`, `taskArn`, `launchedAt`, `attemptId`, `feed`; condition: still in the status read) and sends a launch message with its own deduplication id `<buildId>-retry-<ms>` (the FIFO queue drops a repeat of the build id for 5 minutes). Same build id, so the participant's page keeps following it; same repository (`ssd-<nickname>`), since `prepareRepository` reuses it. `npm run builds -- list` prints date, status, nickname and build id, newest first.
- **(2.9) Builder image:** `platform/builder/Dockerfile`, two stages from `public.ecr.aws/docker/library/node:22.21.1-bookworm-slim` (Docker Hub's anonymous pull limit failed a build with 429), the builder bundled by esbuild (`platform/builder/bundle.ts`, the Claude Agent SDK kept external and copied with its linux-x64 binary), runs as `node`. x86_64, 1 vCPU, 2 GB. The deploy workflow builds and pushes it tagged with the commit, then passes the pushed digest as the CDK context `builderImageDigest`; the task definition pins the image by digest, so a later push to any tag cannot change what tasks run. A synth without the context uses a placeholder digest (CI synth only).
- **(2.9) Builds stack on the assets bucket:** the launcher's bundle is a file asset, so the builds stack moves from the inline-template synthesizer to `assetsBucketSynthesizer()` and deploys after the assets stack.
- **(2.9) Launcher bundle carries the template:** `templates/basic` is copied next to the code as `template/` (`afterBundling`); a synth test compares the files byte for byte. The template loader moved to `platform/api/github/template-files.ts` and `templateDir()` became a function, so the Lambda's CommonJS bundle holds no `import.meta` (esbuild warned; it would have thrown at load).
- **(2.9) Launch queue visibility:** 150 s, the launcher's timeout (120 s) plus 30 s; GitHub calls stop 20 s before the timeout so the failure write fits.
- **(2.9) Failure reasons' wording:** shown to the participant, without technical detail: "The approved spec could not be found, so the build did not start.", "The build could not be started: the build service had no room for it. Your facilitator can restart it.", "The build could not be started because of a problem on the workshop's side.", "The build stopped before it was finished.", "The build did not start in time.", "The build could not be started after several tries.", "The app could not be published because of a problem on the workshop's side."; GitHub problems as "GitHub: <message>" through `storedReason`.
- **(2.9) Roles:** the task role's DynamoDB writes return `NONE` only; the launcher reads with `LAUNCHER_READ_ATTRIBUTES` and `dynamodb:Select` `SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES`, writes builds with `LAUNCHER_BUILD_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES`, may RunTask only the builder task definition on `ssd-workshop-builds`, pass only the builder's two roles to `ecs-tasks`, and read and delete only the two dead-letter queues. Logs: `/ecs/ssd-workshop-builder` (two weeks).
- **(2.9) Sessions and workload roles kept off build tasks and the deploy queue (try 2, access review HIGH 1 and MEDIUM 4; Franck's yes, built so it is easy to drop: three statements in `ssd-workshop-deploy-read`, three in the boundary, their tests in `access-stack.test.ts`):** in `ssd-workshop-deploy-read`, for the Claude user only (`aws:PrincipalArn`): `NoBuildQueueForSessions` now also denies `sqs:ReceiveMessage`, `sqs:DeleteMessage` and `sqs:DeleteQueue`, and covers `ssd-workshop-deploys*` too (one statement rather than a sibling: same principal, shorter; it also keeps sessions from taking or dropping deploy messages); `NoBuildTasksForSessions` denies `ecs:DescribeTasks`, `ecs:RunTask`, `ecs:StopTask` and `ecs:ExecuteCommand` on `task/ssd-workshop-builds/*` and `task-definition/ssd-workshop-builder:*` (`ecs:ListTasks` stays: it shows no override); `NoLauncherSourcesForSessions` denied `lambda:Create/Update/DeleteEventSourceMapping` where `lambda:FunctionArn` is the launcher (replaced in try 3 by `NoEventSourcesForSessions`, below). In the boundary: `WatchBuildsLauncherOnly` denies `ecs:DescribeTasks` and `ecs:StopTask` on build tasks to every role but the launcher (it still needs both: the check describes and stops tasks); `DeploysFromTheBuilderOnly` denies `sqs:SendMessage` on `ssd-workshop-deploys*` to every role but `ssd-workshop-builder`; `BuildsFromTheirSendersOnly` denies `sqs:SendMessage` on `ssd-workshop-builds*` to every role but the AI worker (launch queue) and the check's scheduler role (it sends to `ssd-workshop-builds-check`). All three use `ArnNotLike` `aws:PrincipalArn` `arn:aws:iam::<account>:role*/<name>`: one pattern for the role with and without its path (a role name is unique in an account; look-alike names are tested), chosen over listing both forms because the boundary had only ~1,060 characters left. `aws:PrincipalArn` is a global key; `ecs:DescribeTasks`/`StopTask` are authorized on the `task` resource and `sqs:SendMessage` on the queue (reference read 2026-11-24). Policy sizes after: boundary 5,918, `-deploy-read` 4,670 of 6,144 (tested). Franck applies them.
- **(2.9) The build starts from the prepared commit (try 2, access review HIGH 2):** the launcher passes the commit `prepareRepository` left on `main` as the override `BUILDER_BASE_COMMIT` (not secret; an override rather than a stored attribute, so the builder's read list stays as it is). The runner fails the build ("The app's repository changed after the build was started, so it was not built. Your facilitator can restart it.") unless `main` still equals it, downloads that commit's tarball, and commits on top of it (the fast-forward-only push then refuses any later move of `main`). Tested: "WHEN main moved after the launch THEN the build fails and nothing is pushed".
- **(2.9) Each run bound to its launch (try 2, access review MEDIUM 3, checker 2):** the launcher's claim stores a random UUID `attemptId` on the build and passes it as `BUILDER_ATTEMPT_ID`; every runner write (claim, feed, hand-over, failure) is conditional on `#status = :building AND #attemptId = :attemptId`, so an earlier launch's task or a task someone else started can neither claim, feed, hand over nor fail the build. The runner's claim no longer accepts a `queued` build (only the launcher's claim starts a run). `attemptId` is added to `LAUNCHER_BUILD_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` and `BUILDER_BUILD_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` (compared only by the builder), and `retry` removes it. RunTask sets `startedBy` to the build id (RunTask allows 128 letters, digits, `-`, `_`, `/`; build ids are at most 64 of these) and gets the launch's deadline signal (`client.send(command, { abortSignal })`), so it cannot outlast the 20 s reserve; a RunTask cut off after ECS started a task leaves a task the build does not name, which the attempt id makes harmless (its claim fails because the build was marked failed) and `retry` waits for (ListTasks).
- **(2.9) Narrower trust (try 2, low findings):** the check's scheduler role trusts `scheduler.amazonaws.com` only with `aws:SourceArn` = `schedule/default/ssd-workshop-builds-check`; the builder's task and execution roles trust `ecs-tasks.amazonaws.com` only with `aws:SourceArn` like `task/ssd-workshop-builds/*` (was `arn:aws:ecs:<region>:<account>:*`). Needs a live check with the first build (infra/README.md). **Try 3 (checker 6):** the execution role is back to the form AWS documents for task execution roles, `aws:SourceArn` like `arn:aws:ecs:eu-central-1:<account>:*` (with `aws:SourceAccount`): ECS may name another ARN when it pulls the image and writes logs, and a failed start would only show live. The task role stays narrowed to `task/ssd-workshop-builds/*` (if the first build fails with "ECS was unable to assume the role", widen it the same way). Tested on the synth output.
- **(2.9) Accepted residual (try 2): the builder's task role may update any `BUILD#*` item** within its attribute list: IAM cannot pin a build id (no condition on the sort key's value per task). A compromised builder task (the coding agent has no way to use its credentials) could hand over another `building` build only by knowing its id and its `attemptId`. In accepted risk 16.
- **(2.9) Exception roles kept from sessions (try 3, access review HIGH; Franck's yes, 2026-11-24):** `NoLauncherForSessions` (Claude user only) now also covers the roles the boundary's denies let through by name, `role*/ssd-workshop-builder`, `role*/ssd-workshop-builder-execution`, `role*/ssd-workshop-api-ai-worker`, `role*/ssd-workshop-builds-check-scheduler`, and `role*/ssd-workshop-apps-deployer` (the apps bucket's only writer, 2.5), plus the functions `ssd-workshop-api-ai-worker` and `ssd-workshop-apps-deployer` (and `:*`): everything but its role and Lambda reads is denied, `iam:PassRole` included, so a session can neither run its own task or function as one of them nor rewrite or invoke their code. Extending the launcher's statement rather than a new one: same principal, same reads kept, shortest. `role*/<name>` in `Resource` covers the role with and without its path in one ARN (both forms tested, look-alikes such as `x-ssd-workshop-builder` and `ssd-workshop-builder-task` stay passable); the launcher's own two entries are unchanged. **Corrected after the merge (see "(2.9) follow-up: no `role*/` in a Resource"):** IAM rejects `role*/<name>` in a `Resource`; the entries are now `role/ssd-workshop/<name>`.
- **(2.9) No event source mappings, check schedule or builds-cluster tasks for sessions (try 3, access review LOW 2 and 3; Franck's yes, 2026-11-24):** `NoEventSourcesForSessions` denies the Claude user `lambda:CreateEventSourceMapping`, `UpdateEventSourceMapping` and `DeleteEventSourceMapping` with no other condition: the Service Authorization Reference (lambda.json, read 2026-11-24) has no key for a mapping's event source (only tags and `lambda:FunctionArn`), and a call by mapping UUID may carry no function ARN. Sessions never need mappings (deploys use the deploy role). `NoBuildCheckScheduleForSessions` denies `scheduler:Create*`, `Update*`, `Delete*` on `schedule/*/ssd-workshop-builds-check`. `NoBuildsClusterTasksForSessions` denies `ecs:RunTask` (any task definition) with `ecs:cluster` like `cluster/ssd-workshop-builds`. All three for the Claude user only (`aws:PrincipalArn`), so deploys from `main` are unaffected (tested). Policy sizes after try 3: boundary 5,918 (unchanged), `-deploy-read` 5,742 of 6,144 (tested). **Access review follow-up:** `NoBuildsClusterServicesForSessions` (Claude user only) denies `ecs:CreateService`, `ecs:UpdateService` and `ecs:CreateTaskSet` on `service/ssd-workshop-builds/*` and `task-set/ssd-workshop-builds/*` (ARN formats and resource types per ecs.json, read 2026-11-24: CreateService/UpdateService authorize on `service`, CreateTaskSet on `task-set`), so a session cannot start tasks on the builds cluster through a service either; no deploy policy grants task sets today, the deny keeps it so if one ever does. A separate statement because RunTask's deny keys on `ecs:cluster` with `Resource "*"`. `-deploy-read` is now 6,106 of 6,144 (38 left: the next deny for sessions needs another deploy policy or a shorter one).
- **(2.9) No queue attribute writes, deletes or purges for workload roles (access review follow-up):** the boundary's `NoExternalSharing` now also denies `sqs:SetQueueAttributes`, `sqs:DeleteQueue` and `sqs:PurgeQueue`: a role a session creates could otherwise set a queue `Policy` that opens `ssd-workshop-deploys*` or `ssd-workshop-builds*` to senders outside the account (or this account's other principals), or delete or purge those queues. No workload role in `infra/lib` uses them (grep, and the synth output's policies); CloudFormation changes queues as the deploy role, which has no boundary, and the deploy policies keep them (tested). Boundary 5,978 of 6,144 (tested).
- **(2.9) What sessions still can (try 3, residual, stated in accepted risk 16):** change what is served outside the build pipeline through their deploy rights (the apps bucket's policy then `PutObject`, the CloudFront distribution, the apps-index function; see the next line); write the table directly (their deploy rights): through the pipeline that changes a live app only together with a deploy message, which no session can send nor make a borrowed role send. A session can still drive the normal pipeline as a participant could (records it wrote plus a message on the AI queue, which stays open to sessions): the AI worker may then queue a build of a spec the session wrote, built and published by the platform's own roles; the session never sees that build's token. A session can also create its own function (with its own boundary role) and invoke it to receive and delete launch or deploy messages (`sqs:ReceiveMessage`/`DeleteMessage` on workshop queues under the boundary; the boundary has 166 characters left, too few for a receive deny naming the launcher and the deployer): a denial of service only, recovered by the check (deploy: failed after the grace) or `retry` (a swallowed launch leaves the build `queued`). Also a denial-of-service residual: sessions may still `ecs:DeregisterTaskDefinition` the builder's task definition (Resource `*` only, `ActionsWithoutResourceScope`) and delete images in `ssd-workshop-*` ECR repositories (their deploy rights), which stops new builds until the next deploy from `main` registers and pushes them again; nothing live changes.
- **(2.9) Sessions and live apps: a follow-up ticket (Franck, 2026-11-24, decision card in the 2.9 thread):** 2.9 keeps the build-pipeline denies (tries 2 and 3) and accepts that Claude sessions, holding deploy rights, can still change served app files outside the pipeline. A follow-up access ticket cuts Claude sessions to read-only plus narrow rights so only deploys from `main` change live apps (the alternatives were more Claude-user denies, with 38 characters left in `ssd-workshop-deploy-read`, or dropping the extra denies). **Done by ticket 2.12** (see the "(2.12)" entries below).
- **(2.9) Malformed watch entries (try 3, access review LOW 4):** the check asks ECS only about entries whose `taskArn` matches `arn:aws:ecs:<REGION>:<ACCOUNT>:task/ssd-workshop-builds/<32 hex>`, with the workshop's own region and account from `platform/api/settings.ts` (access review follow-up: was any region and any 12 digits, so a forged entry naming another region or account reached DescribeTasks); any other entry is removed (condition: it still names that task) without a build write, so one bad entry in the shared table cannot make DescribeTasks fail for every build.
- **(2.9) A launch that lost both writes (try 3, checker 5):** when `recordTask`'s build write and `failUnstarted` both fail, the entry names a task the launcher stopped and the build is `building` without `taskArn`. The check, once that task is stopped (or unknown to ECS), fails the build with "The build could not be started because of a problem on the workshop's side." (`failUnrecorded`, condition `#status = :building AND attribute_not_exists(#taskArn) AND #launchedAt = :claimedAt`: the entry's `claimedAt` is the claim's `launchedAt`, so an earlier launch's entry never fails a retry's new launch) and removes the entry. `launchedAt` was already in `LAUNCHER_BUILD_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES`; no IAM change.
- **(2.9) follow-up: no `role*/` in a Resource (the access stack update after the merge failed and rolled back):** IAM refused `ssd-workshop-deploy-read` ("IAM resource path must either be "*", root, or start with user/, federated-user/, role/, ..."): `role*/<name>` is valid only in a condition value (`ArnNotLike` `aws:PrincipalArn`, the boundary's three denies, unchanged), not in a `Resource`, and cfn-lint does not catch it. `NoLauncherForSessions` now names `role/ssd-workshop/ssd-workshop-builder`, `-builder-execution`, `-api-ai-worker`, `-builds-check-scheduler` and `-apps-deployer` (each with its path; the launcher keeps both forms). No path-less entry: neither principal may create, pass or change a role outside `role/ssd-workshop/` (`WorkshopRolesWithBoundaryOnly`, `WorkshopRolesHousekeeping`, `PassWorkshopRolesToWorkshopServices`), and the workshop's roles all carry that path (Aspect). The exact form is 12 characters longer per entry, which took `-deploy-read` to 6,166: `NoBuildTasksForSessions`, `NoBuildsClusterTasksForSessions` and `NoBuildsClusterServicesForSessions` moved unchanged to `ssd-workshop-deploy-ec2` (same two principals). New test: every IAM ARN in a `Resource`/`NotResource` of every policy has a resource part `*`, `root` or one of IAM's resource types (`role/`, `user/`, `policy/`, ...).
- **(2.9) follow-up: ECR Public login in the deploy workflow (the first deploy after the merge failed):** building the builder image got `429 Too Many Requests` from `public.ecr.aws` (anonymous pulls from GitHub's shared runners are rate-limited too). The workflow now logs in first (`aws ecr-public get-login-password --region us-east-1 | docker login --username AWS --password-stdin public.ecr.aws`, as the deploy role; ECR Public's API is only in us-east-1) and tries `docker build` up to 3 times (pauses 30 s and 60 s). New statements in `ssd-workshop-deploy-ec2`: `EcrPublicLogin` (`ecr-public:GetAuthorizationToken`) and `EcrPublicBearerToken` (`sts:GetServiceBearerToken`, no condition: the first version required `sts:AWSServiceName` `ecr-public.amazonaws.com` and the live login was still denied, 2026-11-24, reproduced with the Claude user; a bearer token alone grants nothing), both `Resource "*"` (neither action has a resource type; actions and key per ecr-public.json and sts.json, read 2026-11-24). Not in the boundary. The Claude user carries the same policy and so gets them too: harmless (a token that pulls public images; no ECR Public write is granted). Sizes after: boundary 5,978, `-deploy` 5,732, `-edge` 5,916, `-ec2` 4,405, `-read` 5,155 of 6,144 (tested). Franck applies the access stack before re-running the deploy.
- **(2.9) follow-up: the first builds-stack deploy rolled back (2026-11-24):** (1) EventBridge Scheduler refused the check's role ("The execution role you provide must allow AWS EventBridge Scheduler to assume the role"): its trust named the schedule's ARN as `aws:SourceArn`, but Scheduler names the schedule group, so the trust now names `schedule-group/default` of this account (any default-group schedule of the account may use the role, which may only send to the check queue). (2) The rollback could not delete the two new event source mappings: CloudFormation reads a mapping by UUID first and Lambda checked `lambda:GetEventSourceMapping` on Resource `*` with no `lambda:FunctionArn` (reproduced with the Claude user), so `WorkshopEventSourceMappings`' condition never matched. New in `ssd-workshop-deploy-ec2`: `ReadEventSourceMappings` (Get on `*`, read only: other projects' mapping settings become readable, no secrets in them) and `ChangeTaggedEventSourceMappings` (Delete/Update on `event-source-mapping:*` with `aws:ResourceTag/project` `ssd-workshop`; unverified live whether Delete by UUID is checked on the ARN). The two mappings the rollback could not delete were already gone afterwards (removed with the launcher function; Franck checked, 2026-11-24). Sessions still cannot change mappings (`NoEventSourcesForSessions`).
- **(2.9) follow-up: the first live build's RunTask was refused (2026-11-25):** the launcher logged `ClientException` and nothing more. Most likely cause: the builder task role's trust pinned `aws:SourceArn` to `task/ssd-workshop-builds/*` (flagged unverified in try 2), which ECS cannot match when it checks the role at RunTask. The task role now trusts ECS in this account (`arn:aws:ecs:eu-central-1:<account>:*`, the AWS-documented form, as the execution role since try 3); only the launcher may RunTask and pass the role, which is what keeps other tasks off it. The launcher also logs AWS's reason (`detail`, at most 300 characters) for an ECS `ClientException`, `InvalidParameterException` or `AccessDeniedException`: ECS messages name resources and settings, never override values, so the token stays out of the logs.
- **(2.9) New dependency `@aws-sdk/client-ecs`** (`^3.1176.0`) in `platform`: RunTask, DescribeTasks and StopTask for the launcher and the `builds` command.
- **(2.12) Claude sessions read-only (Franck's choice of 2026-11-24, built in 2.12):** the user `ssd-workshop-claude` now carries only `ssd-workshop-deploy-read` and a new managed policy `ssd-workshop-session`, never `ssd-workshop-deploy`, `-deploy-edge` or `-deploy-ec2`; the deploy role keeps its four policies unchanged in effect. Every Allow the user holds is a read (the test's `isRead`) or one of six listed exceptions (Bedrock `InvokeModel` and `InvokeModelWithResponseStream`, SES `SendEmail` (try 3 dropped `SendRawEmail`), and the log reads `FilterLogEvents`, `StartQuery`, `StartLiveTail`), tested with mutations. Franck applies the stack after the merge.
- **(2.12) Bedrock and SES moved off the deploy role:** `Bedrock` and `SesForTesting` move from `ssd-workshop-deploy-edge` to `ssd-workshop-session`, unchanged. No deploy step needs them (`deploy.yml` runs unit tests, pushes the builder image, `cdk deploy` and curl/`describe-stacks` smoke checks; creating a role that may call Bedrock needs no Bedrock right of the creator), so the deploy role no longer keeps a copy.
- **(2.12) No table item reads for sessions:** no `GetItem`, `BatchGetItem`, `Query`, `Scan`, `PartiQLSelect`, export or stream record read on `ssd-workshop-*` tables (the table holds participants' email addresses), only `DescribeTable`, `DescribeContinuousBackups`, `DescribeTimeToLive` and `ListTagsOfResource`. Live checks that read or write items (`scan --select COUNT`, the manual publish's `put-item`/`get-item`) now run as `AWS_PROFILE=franck` in `infra/README.md`.
- **(2.12) The session reads (each with its Service Authorization Reference resource type, read 2026-11-24):** CloudFormation `Describe*`/`Get*`/`List*` on `stack/ssd-workshop-*/*` (outputs, events, templates; `cdk diff` cannot create a change set, a write: it falls back to a template diff, or run it with `--method template`); CloudWatch Logs `DescribeLogStreams`, `GetLogEvents`, `FilterLogEvents`, `GetLogGroupFields`, `GetQueryResults`, `ListTagsForResource`, `StartQuery`, `StartLiveTail` on the log groups `ssd-workshop-*`, `/aws/lambda/ssd-workshop-*` and `/ecs/ssd-workshop-*`; Lambda `GetFunction`, `GetFunctionConfiguration`, `GetPolicy`, `GetAlias`, `ListAliases`, `ListVersionsByFunction`, `ListTags` on `function:ssd-workshop-*` (no `lambda:List*` account-wide, no invoke) and `GetEventSourceMapping` for `ssd-workshop-*` functions (`lambda:FunctionArn`); SQS `GetQueueAttributes`, `GetQueueUrl`, `ListQueueTags`, `ListDeadLetterSourceQueues` (never `ReceiveMessage`); ECS `DescribeClusters`, `DescribeServices`, `DescribeTasks` on `ssd-workshop-*` (build tasks stay denied by `NoBuildTasksForSessions`); ECR `DescribeRepositories`, `DescribeImages`, `ListImages`, `ListTagsForResource`; S3 `GetObject*`/`GetBucket*`/`Get*Configuration`/`List*` on `ssd-workshop-*` buckets of this account (try 2: not `Get*`, which names `s3:GetDataAccess`); Secrets Manager `DescribeSecret`, `ListSecretVersionIds` (never `GetSecretValue`); Scheduler `GetSchedule`, `GetScheduleGroup`, `ListTagsForResource`. Step Functions, EventBridge rules, SNS and SSM: none granted (no workshop stack uses them).
- **(2.12) Session reads in eu-central-1 only, stacks in every region:** the session policy's ARNs name `${AWS::Region}` (the stack's region, eu-central-1), except CloudFormation stacks (`ssd-workshop-edge` lives in us-east-1); the deploy policies keep `*` for the deploy role.
- **(2.12) No image pulls for sessions:** ECR describe/list only; no `GetAuthorizationToken`, `BatchGetImage` or `GetDownloadUrlForLayer` (a session builds images locally and never needs the pushed ones).
- **(2.12) Log queries:** `StartQuery` and `StartLiveTail` (named "Start", but they only read the workshop's log groups) and `FilterLogEvents` (a read without a read prefix) are listed as exceptions in the test rather than widening the read classifier; `StopQuery` is not granted (it takes only `Resource *`; a query ends on its own); `GetQueryResults` is scoped to the workshop's log groups as the reference lists, unverified live (if `aws logs get-query-results` is refused after Franck applies, it needs `Resource *` and Franck's yes).
- **(2.12) What sessions lose besides deploys:** `ecs:DescribeTaskDefinition` (so accepted risk 15 now concerns the deploy role only), `ecr:GetAuthorizationToken`, the `logs:CreateLogDelivery`/`DeleteLogDelivery` and `ecs:DeregisterTaskDefinition` exceptions (risk 3: deploy role only), `RunTask` (risk 1: deploy role and launcher only), and every write path of accepted risk 16's "(2.9) live-app residual" (apps bucket policy, CloudFront, functions, the table, the AI queue, own functions and roles), which is closed for sessions.
- **(2.12) The 2.6 and 2.9 session denies stay** in `ssd-workshop-deploy-read` as defence in depth (a later grant on the session policy cannot reopen the launcher, the builds queues, build tasks or event source mappings); tested.
- **(2.12) `ProtectAccessIdentities` names `policy/ssd-workshop-session` explicitly** (in `ssd-workshop-deploy`, now 5,788 of 6,144 characters) rather than a wildcard such as `policy/ssd-workshop-*`, which would be shorter but would also cover any workshop-named managed policy a stack might create later. Six managed policies now; sizes: boundary 5,978, `-deploy` 5,788, `-deploy-edge` 5,310, `-deploy-ec2` 3,697, `-deploy-read` 5,155, `-session` 6,039 (after merging main on 2026-11-25; the size test also counts room for four test addresses) (tested).
- **(2.12) Franck's operational commands unchanged:** `npm run builds`, `conversation`, `flags`, `allowlist` and the GitHub secret load already run as `AWS_PROFILE=franck`; `infra/README.md` now writes that prefix on each of them. Sessions keep `npm run smoke -- bedrock|quota|ses` and the evals (Bedrock only).
- **(2.12) No role assumption for sessions (try 2, checker HIGH):** the user lost `ssd-workshop-deploy`'s `NoSelfAssume` with the deploy policies, and in the same account a role whose trust policy names `user/ssd-workshop-claude` needs no identity-based Allow. `ssd-workshop-session` now denies `sts:AssumeRole`, `TagSession`, `SetSourceIdentity` and `GetFederationToken` on every resource (`NoAssumeForSessions`, no principal condition: only the user carries the policy); tested with the evaluator, also against a loose `sts:*` Allow.
- **(2.12) No credential-returning reads for sessions (try 2, checker MEDIUM):** `isRead` counts every `Get`/`Describe`/`List` as harmless, so the test's `SESSION_FORBIDDEN` now also lists `ecr:GetAuthorizationToken`, `ecr-public:`/`codeartifact:GetAuthorizationToken`, `sts:GetFederationToken`, `sts:GetSessionToken`, `sso:GetRoleCredentials`, `s3:GetDataAccess`, `ec2:GetPasswordData`, `ec2:GetConsoleOutput` and `ec2:GetConsoleScreenshot`, matched against granted wildcards (`ecr:Get*` fails). `lambda:GetFunctionUrlConfig` is not listed (a URL's settings, no secret; URLs are denied anyway).
- **(2.12) No user data for sessions (try 2):** `ec2:Describe*` on `-deploy-read` would let sessions read project-tagged instances' user data and launch template versions (`NoUserDataReads` there denies them only outside the tag). Closed rather than accepted: `NoUserDataForSessions` in `ssd-workshop-session` denies `DescribeInstanceAttribute`, `DescribeLaunchTemplateVersions`, `DescribeSpotFleetRequests` and `DescribeSpotInstanceRequests` to the user (`-deploy-read` has 38 characters left); the deploy role keeps them on the project's resources.
- **(2.12) Bedrock models in EU regions only for sessions (try 2):** the session policy's foundation-model ARN is `arn:aws:bedrock:eu-*::foundation-model/anthropic.*`, as the API stack's worker; EU inference profiles route only to EU regions. **Replaced after merging main (2026-11-25):** Franck's "Other providers' models for benchmarks" (2026-11-25) wins: the session's `Bedrock` statement allows any foundation model and the `eu.*` and `global.*` profiles, as main's `-deploy-edge` did.
- **(2.12) Build-task session denies on the session policy (merge with main, 2026-11-25):** main moved `NoBuildTasksForSessions`, `NoBuildsClusterTasksForSessions` and `NoBuildsClusterServicesForSessions` from `-deploy-read` to `-deploy-ec2`, which the user no longer carries; they now sit on `ssd-workshop-session` without the principal condition (only the user carries it). `ecs:Describe*` is in `OnlyReadsForSessions`' list, so without them sessions could read a running build's token. The ECR Public login stays on `-deploy-ec2` (deploy role only).
- **(2.12) Budget address hidden (try 2):** `BudgetEmail` is `NoEcho`, because sessions may `DescribeStacks` on `ssd-workshop-*`, which includes `ssd-workshop-access` and would show the parameter. Updates keep the existing value (console "Use existing value", shown as `****`).
- **(2.12) Log query cost (try 2):** Logs Insights queries (`StartQuery`, per GB scanned) and live tails (`StartLiveTail`, per minute) cost a little per use; at workshop log volumes this stays in cents and the `ssd-workshop-monthly` budget alert covers it. No limit in IAM.
- **(2.12) Leftovers from sessions with deploy rights (try 2):** resource policies or roles an earlier session may have written naming the user, and resources no stack owns, cannot be fixed in the template; `infra/README.md` has Franck's one-time read-only audit (run as `AWS_PROFILE=franck` after applying).
- **(2.12) Catch-all read-only deny for sessions (try 3, access review):** in the same account a resource policy naming the user (left from the sessions that had deploy rights) needs no identity Allow, so `ssd-workshop-session` now carries `OnlyReadsForSessions`: Deny with `NotAction` on `Resource "*"`, no condition. The `NotAction` holds 50 entries: wildcard groups per service whose fixed part starts with a read verb (`cloudformation:Describe*`/`Get*`/`List*`, `ec2:Describe*`, `ecs:Describe*`/`List*`, `acm:Describe*`/`List*`, `route53:Get*`/`List*`, `servicequotas:Get*`/`List*`, `s3:GetObject*`/`GetBucket*`/`Get*Configuration`/`List*`, `secretsmanager:Describe*`/`List*`, `logs:Describe*`/`Get*`/`List*`, `ce:GetCost*`, `iam:Get*`/`List*`, `cloudfront:Get*`/`List*`, `lambda:Get*`/`List*`, `sqs:Get*`/`List*`, `ecr:Describe*`/`List*`, `dynamodb:Describe*`/`List*`, `scheduler:Get*`/`List*`), exact reads (`cloudformation:ValidateTemplate`, `bedrock:GetFoundationModel`/`GetInferenceProfile`/`ListFoundationModels`/`ListInferenceProfiles`, `ses:GetSendQuota`, `secretsmanager:GetRandomPassword`, `apigateway:GET`) and the exceptions by exact name (`bedrock:InvokeModel`, `InvokeModelWithResponseStream`, `ses:SendEmail`, `logs:FilterLogEvents`, `StartQuery`, `StartLiveTail`). Every entry was matched against the Service Authorization Reference (servicereference.us-east-1.amazonaws.com, read 2026-11-24): each names at least one real action and expands only to reads or those exceptions. Bedrock reads are the four used, not `bedrock:Get*` (which also names `GetDocumentContent`, `GetSession`). Tested: the structure of every entry (and mutations: `sqs:*`, `ec2:Get*`, `dynamodb:Get*`, `ses:SendRawEmail`, a malformed pattern, `ce:G*`, a narrower Resource), no entry matching `SESSION_FORBIDDEN`, and per request with a loose `Allow *` on `*` added to the user (standing for a resource policy): `s3:PutObject` on the site bucket, `sqs:SendMessage` to an `ssd-workshop-*` queue, `sns:Publish`, `lambda:UpdateFunctionCode`/`InvokeFunction`, `iam:PassRole`, `dynamodb:PutItem`, `ses:SendRawEmail`, `kms:Decrypt` and every forbidden read are refused. `ssd-workshop-session` is 5,277 of 6,144 characters with room for four 40-character test addresses.
- **(2.12) Calls sessions still make (try 3):** checked request by request with the catch-all in place: `npm run smoke -- bedrock` (`bedrock-runtime` Converse = `bedrock:InvokeModel` on the `eu.anthropic.*` profile and the EU foundation model), `smoke -- quota` (`servicequotas:ListServiceQuotas`, `ListAWSDefaultServiceQuotas`), `smoke -- ses` (SESv2 `SendEmail`, simple content, on the two identities and the configuration set), `npm run eval` (spec and build suites: Converse, and `InvokeModel`/`InvokeModelWithResponseStream` through the builder's Bedrock proxy), plus `describe-stacks`, stack events of workshop stacks, `logs` filter, tail, query and query results, `s3` get and list on workshop buckets, `ec2 describe-subnets`, `route53 list-resource-record-sets` on the zone, `cloudfront list-invalidations`, `apigatewayv2` GET, `dynamodb describe-table`, `iam get-role`/`list-attached-role-policies` on `/ssd-workshop/`, `ce get-cost-and-usage`/`get-cost-forecast`, `lambda get-function-configuration`, `ecs list-clusters`, `acm list-certificates`, `ses get-send-quota`, `bedrock list-inference-profiles`. `smoke -- github` and `signin` are Franck's (secret value; no AWS call), unchanged. `sts get-caller-identity` needs no permission and cannot be denied.
- **(2.12) Test guards on the user's IAM shape (try 3):** `ClaudeUser.Properties` has exactly `UserName`, `ManagedPolicyArns`, `Tags`; no managed policy has `Users`, `Groups` or `Roles`; the template's only `AWS::IAM::*` resources are the six managed policies, `DeployRole`, `ClaudeUser` and `GitHubOidcProvider`. One mutation per path (inline `Policies`, `Groups`, `PermissionsBoundary` on the user; `Users`/`Groups`/`Roles` on a managed policy; an extra `AWS::IAM::Policy`, `UserPolicy`, `Group`, `UserToGroupAddition`, `ManagedPolicy`, `Role`). `SESSION_FORBIDDEN` adds `ec2:GetLaunchTemplateData`, `lightsail:GetInstanceAccessDetails`, `redshift:GetClusterCredentials`, `redshift:GetClusterCredentialsWithIAM`, `cognito-identity:GetCredentialsForIdentity`, `cognito-identity:GetOpenIdToken`, and a wildcard Allow on the user that is not in the test's pinned `SESSION_WILDCARDS` (the 15 granted today) fails, so a future `ec2:Get*` needs a look first.
- **(2.12) Budget address kept from sessions (try 3):** `budgets:ViewBudget` also authorizes `DescribeSubscribersForNotification` (the alert address); it is left out of the catch-all's `NotAction`, so the user is denied it although `-deploy-read` still allows it (the deploy role keeps it). `NoAccessStackEventsForSessions` denies `cloudformation:DescribeStackEvents`, `DescribeEvents` and `DescribeStackResourceDrifts` on `stack/ssd-workshop-access/*` (old events and drift results may show the address) and, for `DescribeEvents`, on every `changeSet/*` (a change set's ARN does not name its stack, so sessions lose change set events of workshop stacks too). `DescribeStacks` and `GetTemplate` stay (the parameter is `NoEcho`; the template holds only `!Ref BudgetEmail`).
- **(2.12) Session test emails only to chosen addresses (try 3):** `SesForTesting` drops `ses:SendRawEmail` (the smoke test and `platform/api/sign-in/mailer.ts` both use SESv2 `SendEmail` with `Simple` content; confirmed) and adds `ForAllValues:StringLike` `ses:Recipients` = `!Ref SessionTestRecipients` and `Null` `ses:Recipients` = `"false"` (the reference lists `ses:Recipients`, ArrayOfString, on `SendEmail`). The new parameter `SessionTestRecipients` is a `CommaDelimitedList`, default `success@simulator.amazonses.com` (the SES mailbox simulator); Franck enters his own test addresses in the console at apply time, so no address is in the repository (tested: the template holds no address but the sender and the simulator). Not `NoEcho`: the addresses are readable by sessions anyway in the policy (`iam:GetPolicyVersion` on `policy/ssd-workshop-*`), and sessions need to know them; the parameter description says to enter test addresses only. `ses:FromDisplayName` is not pinned (skipped: the mailer's display name was not checked against a live send). Boundary `SendCodes` (the sign-in code role) is unchanged and still sends to anyone. Phase 0's criteria for `npm run smoke -- ses <address>` (any address arrives) now hold from a session only for addresses in `SessionTestRecipients`, and for any address with `AWS_PROFILE=franck`. Participants' addresses must never be entered in the parameter (sessions can read it).
- **(2.12) Cross-project reads through `-deploy-read` (try 3):** denied to sessions: `ec2:DescribeVerifiedAccess*` (trust providers may hold OIDC client secrets; own Deny `NoVerifiedAccessForSessions`, since `ec2:Describe*` stays in the catch-all) and `ses:GetEmailIdentity`/`ListEmailIdentities` (left out of the catch-all; no smoke or eval code calls them). Accepted and kept: `ce` cost reads, the rankly.games zone reads, the other `ec2:Describe*` (accepted risk 6).
- **(2.12) One-time audit extended (try 3):** `infra/README.md`'s audit now also flags role trusts, bucket policies (every bucket) and queue policies (every enabled region) that name the user's unique id (`AIDA...`, from `aws iam get-user`) or any principal (`"Principal": "*"`, `{"AWS": "*"}`), and resources no stack owns (checked with `describe-stack-resources --physical-resource-id` in eu-central-1 and us-east-1): `ssd-workshop-*` functions, ECS services on `ssd-workshop-*` clusters, EC2 instances tagged `project=ssd-workshop`, Scheduler schedules and EventBridge rules named `ssd-workshop-*`, and roles under `/ssd-workshop/`. Session check expectations now read "explicit deny" (the catch-all) instead of "no identity-based policy allows".

## Phase 1: sign in and talk

- **Paid services (Franck, 2026-11-23):** API Gateway HTTP API, Lambda and DynamoDB on demand are approved for phase 1 (estimate under $2 a month at rest, about $10 to $20 of Bedrock for tests and evals).
- **Model (Franck, 2026-11-23):** phase 1 uses Claude Sonnet 4.6 (`eu.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6`), set in one config value; Sonnet 5.5's quota in the account is 0, and raising it is Franck's call.
- **Spec delta, CHANGED (Franck, 2026-11-23, PR #10):** section 5, Security: "facilitators only see their own group" becomes "facilitators see all participants; participants see only their own data", matching the global facilitator dashboard of section 4.
- **Allow-list loading (Franck, 2026-11-23):** Franck imports it with `npm run allowlist -- import <file.csv>` (`email,group,role`) from his own machine; the file stays outside the repository and the addresses never pass through a Claude session or GitHub. An admin page follows in phase 3.
- **Code email without Reply-To (2026-11-23):** `ssd-workshop@rankly.games` receives no mail and the system expects no replies, so the email has no Reply-To and ends with "Questions? Ask your facilitator." A Reply-To on a different domain than the sender is a spam signal. Other measures against Junk: "SSD Workshop" display name, subject with the code, HTML and text parts, a line on why the person gets it, a Junk-folder hint on the code screen, and IT allowlisting the sender (asked by Franck).
- **Asynchronous AI replies:** a message is stored and answered by a worker Lambda, and the page polls every 2 seconds, because API Gateway stops requests after 30 seconds and Lambda response streaming needs function URLs, which the access stack denies.
- **API at `/api/*`:** CloudFront routes `workshop.rankly.games/api/*` to the HTTP API, so pages and API share one origin (no CORS, no new DNS record). The API id becomes a constant in `infra/lib/config.ts` after its first deploy, since the us-east-1 edge stack cannot read it across regions.
- **Flags before the dashboard:** until phase 3, flagged messages are approved or rejected with `npm run flags -- approve|reject <nickname>`.
- **(1.1) API stack:** `ssd-workshop-api` (eu-central-1, `infra/lib/api-stack.ts`) holds the table, the HTTP API and its Lambda functions; it uses the assets-bucket synthesizer (the Lambda code is a file asset) and deploys after `ssd-workshop-cdk-assets`. Names in `infra/lib/config.ts`: `TABLE_NAME`, `API_NAME`, `HEALTH_FUNCTION_NAME`.
- **(1.1) Table:** `Table` (`AWS::DynamoDB::Table`, not `TableV2`'s global table), single-table design with string keys `pk`/`sk`, on demand, point-in-time recovery, AWS-owned encryption (no KMS key, no cost), TTL on `expiresAt` (epoch seconds) for codes and sessions, `RemovalPolicy.RETAIN`.
- **(1.1) Deletion protection on:** the table also refuses a direct `DeleteTable` (participants' work lives there). Turning it off is one stack update while the stack owns the table. If the first deploy of `ssd-workshop-api` fails and rolls back, the table stays behind outside any stack (retain plus deletion protection; resource import is denied), and every later deploy fails on its name: Franck removes the empty table by hand (`infra/README.md`, "If the first API deploy fails").
- **(1.1) Key scheme:** `EMAIL#<email>` + `ALLOW` / `CODE` (and `CODE_REQUESTS` from 1.4), `SESSION#<token hash>` + `SESSION`, `PARTICIPANT#<id>` + `PROFILE` / `MSG#<message id>` / `USAGE`, documented in `platform/api/types.ts` and built only by `platform/api/keys.ts` (tested: emails trimmed and lower-cased, ids without `#`, no email in error messages). A participant's profile, messages and usage share one partition, so a read always names the caller's own id. Message ids start with the ISO creation time, so the conversation queries in order and one message is a direct GetItem. Later tickets add fields and item types (e.g. a nickname claim for uniqueness in 1.5).
- **(1.1) Usage:** running totals per participant in one `USAGE` item (tokens and US dollars); each assistant message also carries its own tokens and cost.
- **(1.1) HTTP API:** `HttpApi` named `ssd-workshop-api`, no CORS (one origin from 1.2), the execute-api endpoint kept on (CloudFront's origin in 1.2), one `$default` stage with auto-deploy and throttling at 50 requests per second, burst 100 (about three times what 40 participants polling every 2 seconds need). No access logging for now: the Lambda logs suffice, and stage access logs may need API Gateway's service-linked role, which the deploy rights create only for ECS (`iam:CreateServiceLinkedRole`); to be checked before a ticket turns them on.
