- **(1.1) Health function:** `ssd-workshop-api-health`, Node.js 22, arm64, 256 MB, 10 seconds, its own role `ssd-workshop-api-health` (only `logs:CreateLogStream`/`PutLogEvents` on its log group, no managed policy) and its own log group `ssd-workshop-api-health` (two weeks, deleted with the stack). It answers 200 `{"ok":true}` with `content-type: application/json` and `cache-control: no-store`. The table name is not passed yet; the first function that reads the table gets it and its grant.
- **(1.1) Lambda bundling:** `NodejsFunction` with esbuild run locally (no Docker), minified, no source maps; handlers live in `platform/api/handlers/`. `esbuild` (`^0.28.2`, the version tsx already installs) is an `infra` dev dependency so CDK finds it in CI and tests. A test loads the bundled `index.js` and calls it.
- **(1.1) Response type:** `platform/api/http.ts` declares the HTTP API's Lambda response shape instead of adding `@types/aws-lambda` for one interface.
- **(1.1) Names and tags test:** `AttributeName` and `StageName` (`$default`) are not physical names, nor a Lambda permission's `FunctionName` (a reference); the table, API and stage are in `MUST_BE_TAGGED` (API Gateway v2 tags are a map, which `tagsOf` now reads), route, integration and Lambda permission in `NOT_TAGGABLE`.
- **(1.1) API Gateway tag on create (access stack, Franck 2026-11-23):** the first `ssd-workshop-api` deploy was refused at `apigateway:POST` on `/tags/<API ARN>`: CloudFormation's API Gateway v2 handler tags the new API through the tags endpoint, which `ApiGatewayTaggedCreate` does not cover. New statement `ApiGatewayTagOnCreateThroughCloudFormation` in `ssd-workshop-deploy-edge`: `apigateway:POST`/`PUT` on `/tags/*` only with the project request tag and `aws:CalledVia` CloudFormation (resource import is denied, so only APIs a workshop stack created). No `Null aws:ResourceTag/project` guard: the tags resource has no resource-tag key, and a stage inherits its API's tag. The failed deploy rolled back with nothing left behind. Franck applies the update with a change set, then re-runs the Deploy run.
- **(1.1) API Gateway stage tagging (access stack, Franck 2026-11-23):** with the tags endpoint allowed, the re-run created the API but CloudFormation's stage creation was refused at `apigateway:TagResource` on `/apis/<id>/stages`, an action not yet listed in the Service Authorization Reference. New statement `ApiGatewayTagStagesOnCreate`: `apigateway:TagResource` on `/apis/*` only with the project request tag, the project resource tag (stages inherit the API's) and `aws:CalledVia` CloudFormation. The run rolled back with nothing left behind.
- **(1.1) Health check after every deploy:** the deploy workflow reads `ssd-workshop-api`'s `ApiEndpoint` output (stack name from `config.ts` through `print-deploy-settings.ts`, `api_stack`) and requires `GET /api/health` to answer 200 `{"ok":true}`, five tries 10 seconds apart; the same command is in `infra/README.md`.
- **(1.2) `/api/*` behaviour:** CloudFront sends `/api/*` to the HTTP API's execute-api address (`HttpOrigin`, HTTPS only to the origin) with the managed `CachingDisabled` cache policy, the managed `AllViewerExceptHostHeader` origin request policy (cookies, query strings and every header except `Host`, which API Gateway needs as its own), all seven methods (`GET`, `HEAD`, `OPTIONS`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `POST`, `DELETE`; the body passes through) and the managed `SecurityHeadersPolicy`, as on the pages. Managed policies only: no new untaggable CloudFront resource. Pages and API share one origin, so no CORS and no new DNS record.
- **(1.2) API id as a constant:** `API_ID = "ut2mzqmbb4"` and `API_ORIGIN_DOMAIN` in `infra/lib/config.ts`, because the edge stack (us-east-1) cannot read the api stack's output across regions (`crossRegionReferences` is not allowed). If `ssd-workshop-api` is ever recreated, the API gets a new id: update `API_ID` in the same pull request, or `/api/*` breaks (the post-deploy check through CloudFront then fails).
- **(1.2) HTTPS only for `/api/*`:** the viewer protocol policy is `https-only`, not `redirect-to-https` as for the pages: a redirect would turn a `POST` into a `GET` and lose its body. Plain HTTP to `/api/*` gets 403 from CloudFront.
- **(1.2) Zone diff in the deploy workflow:** before `cdk deploy`, the workflow saves the rankly.games zone (`aws route53 list-resource-record-sets`, zone id from `config.ts` through `print-deploy-settings.ts`, `hosted_zone_id`), sorted with `jq -S` by name, type and set identifier, to `$RUNNER_TEMP` (never committed); after the deploy it takes a second snapshot and fails with `::error::` if `diff` finds any change. The after-check also runs when the deploy failed (`!cancelled()`, as long as the first snapshot succeeded). A later ticket that changes the workshop record on purpose must adjust this check. On a change the log shows only the names and types of the changed records, never their values, because the zone also holds other projects' records. A change by another project during the deploy also fails the check; re-running the deploy clears it.
- **(1.2) Health check through CloudFront:** after every deploy, `GET https://workshop.rankly.games/api/health` must answer 200 with exactly `{"ok":true}`, ten tries 30 seconds apart (CloudFront can take minutes to roll out a behaviour). The direct execute-api check from 1.1 stays: it tells a broken API apart from a broken CloudFront route.
- **(1.2) Direct execute-api address stays reachable (Franck 2026-11-23):** the API's own address keeps answering next to `https://workshop.rankly.games/api/`. Accepted: every route checks the session itself and the stage is throttled. A secret origin header (CloudFront adds it, the API refuses requests without it) can follow as its own ticket.
- **(1.4) Code request answer:** every well-formed `POST /api/sign-in/code` gets `202`, headers `content-type: application/json` and `cache-control: no-store`, body `{"message":"If this address is on the workshop list, a sign-in code is on its way. It can take a minute; check your Junk folder too."}`: the same bytes, at the same time (see "Fixed answer time"), for a listed address, one not on the list, one over its hourly limit, a lost race, a table or SES failure and work still running at the deadline (tested).
- **(1.4) Code request failures:** a DynamoDB or SES error still gets the neutral answer (never a 500: an error that only listed addresses can reach would reveal who is on the list); the function logs one line with the failing step and the error's `name` only. An outage is therefore visible in the log group, not to the person, who can ask again.
- **(1.4) Malformed code request:** `400` with `{"message":"Send a JSON body with one email address."}`, never echoing the input, for a missing or non-JSON body (base64 bodies are decoded first), a body over 2 KB, JSON that is not an object, a missing or non-string `email`, an address over 320 characters or one the allow-list import would refuse. The check is syntactic only and says nothing about the list.
- **(1.4) One email rule:** the allow-list import's address rule moved to `platform/api/email.ts` (`isValidEmail`), used by the import, the code request and `smoke signin`, so every stored address passes at sign-in; `platform/src/allowlist/logic.ts` re-exports it.
- **(1.4) Code rate limit:** at most 5 codes per address in any sliding 60 minutes, kept in a `CODE_REQUESTS` item next to the code (`EMAIL#<email>`, `sentAt` epoch seconds of the window's sends, `version`, TTL `expiresAt` one hour after the newest send), so it outlives the 10-minute code. Only allow-listed addresses are ever written (no stranger's address is stored). A send is counted before it is made, so a failed send still uses one of the five; a refused (sixth) request is not recorded, so asking again does not extend the wait. Two requests at once: each write names the version it read (conditional `PutItem`), and the loser sends nothing.
- **(1.4) Code and its hash:** `crypto.randomInt(0, 1_000_000)`, zero-padded to six digits. Stored as `scrypt$<salt>$<hash>` (scrypt N=2^14, r=8, p=1, 16-byte random salt, 32-byte key); `verifyCode` (constant-time compare) is ready for 1.5. No secret pepper: that would need Secrets Manager or KMS, new paid services. Accepted risk: a 6-digit code's hash can be brute-forced from a leaked table (one million scrypt tries, hours of CPU), which matters little because a code lives 10 minutes and 1.5 burns it after 5 wrong tries.
- **(1.4) Codes item (a new code cancels none):** every unexpired code stays valid, so a stranger who knows a colleague's address cannot cancel the code they are typing. The `CODE` item holds `codes`, a list of at most 5 entries `{ codeHash, expiresAt }` (now + 600 s; expired entries pruned on each write), one `attempts` counter shared by all the codes (kept while earlier codes live, reset when none does), a `version` (conditional `PutItem`, re-read and retried at most 3 times on a conflict, then `conflict` and no send) and TTL `expiresAt` = the latest code's expiry. `verifyAnyCode` (for 1.5) checks every unexpired entry with no early exit. Remaining risk, accepted: someone could still use up a participant's 5 codes for the hour ahead of them; the participant can then use any of those 5 codes from their inbox, and a facilitator reset can come with the phase 3 dashboard.
- **(1.4) Code email wording:** subject "Your SSD Workshop sign-in code: 123456"; text and HTML say "Your SSD Workshop sign-in code is:", the code (large monospace in HTML), "Enter it on the sign-in page. The code is valid for 10 minutes.", "You get this email because someone asked to sign in to the SSD Workshop with this address. If it wasn't you, you can ignore this email." and "Questions? Ask your facilitator." From `"SSD Workshop" <ssd-workshop@rankly.games>`, configuration set `ssd-workshop-email`, no Reply-To, no images, no links, inline styles only.
- **(1.4) Code request logs:** one JSON line per request, `{"event":"sign-in-code","outcome":...}` with outcome `sent`, `not-listed`, `rate-limited`, `conflict`, `invalid-request` or `failed` (plus `step` and the error name, kept only if it looks like an identifier, or `Timeout` when the work was still running at the deadline; the dropped work then logs nothing more). The log type has no field for an address, a code or the body; a capture test proves none reaches `console` or stdout/stderr.
- **(1.4) API settings as constants:** `platform/api/settings.ts` holds region, table, sender, display name and configuration set as constants, not environment variables, so a function cannot be pointed elsewhere by configuration; a test checks them against `infra/lib/config.ts`.
- **(1.4) Sign-in code function:** `ssd-workshop-api-sign-in-code` (Node.js 22, arm64, 512 MB so scrypt runs at a predictable speed, 10 s timeout, well above the 3 s answer time), own role and log group; rights: its logs; `dynamodb:GetItem` with `ForAllValues:StringLike dynamodb:LeadingKeys ["EMAIL#*"]`; `dynamodb:PutItem` with the same condition plus `ForAllValues:StringEquals dynamodb:Attributes` = the CODE and CODE_REQUESTS attribute names (`pk`, `sk`, `type`, `codes`, `attempts`, `sentAt`, `version`, `expiresAt`; `SIGN_IN_CODE_PUT_ATTRIBUTES` in `infra/lib/config.ts`, tested against the store's writes); and `ses:SendEmail` on the two identities and the configuration set with `ses:FromAddress = ssd-workshop@rankly.games`. The function is reachable without sign-in, so it cannot read or write sessions, participants or messages. IAM has no condition on the sort key, so it could still put an `ALLOW` item, but not its `email` attribute: the allow-list check now requires `email` (only the import writes it). Residual risk, accepted for now: a PutItem replaces the whole item, so code running as this role could overwrite an existing `EMAIL#<x>/ALLOW` entry with only `pk`/`sk`/`type` and take that person off the list (denial of service; it can never add anyone or raise anyone's rights). The shipped code never does it, and IAM cannot condition on the sort key; moving codes to their own `CODE#` partition is the fix if it is ever needed. No access stack change (the boundary allows `dynamodb:*` on the table).
- **(1.4) AWS SDK bundled:** `NodejsFunction` leaves `@aws-sdk/*` out of the bundle by default on Node 18+ runtimes; `externalModules: []` bundles it (about 630 KB), so the function runs the SDK version the tests ran with and the synth test can load the bundle on its own.
- **(1.4) Fakes, not a mocking library:** the tests use in-memory fakes behind the small `SignInStore` and `CodeMailer` interfaces and stub clients for the DynamoDB and SES adapters; no `aws-sdk-client-mock` dependency.
- **(1.4) `smoke signin`:** `npm run -s smoke -- signin <address>` POSTs `{"email"}` to `https://workshop.rankly.games/api/sign-in/code` with Node's `fetch` (no AWS credentials), prints the masked address, the status and the answer (redacted, at most 500 characters), and exits 0 only on 202. The address is checked with the API's own rule first.
- **(1.4) Fixed answer time:** a listed address does more work (scrypt, writes, SES) than one not on the list, so timing or a Lambda timeout (a 503 only listed addresses could reach) could reveal list membership. Every request that passes the syntax check therefore gets its neutral 202 exactly `ANSWER_AFTER_MS` = 3000 ms after the handler starts: the work races against that deadline, and work still running then is dropped (logged as `failed`, its step, `Timeout`); dropped work is frozen with the Lambda environment and may resume in the next request on that instance (a late email, no log line), while that next request's answer time is unaffected. SDK clients are bounded (`maxAttempts: 2`, `NodeHttpHandler` with `connectionTimeout` 1000 ms and `requestTimeout` 1500 ms, no proxy in Lambda), so a slow call fails rather than hangs. The `400` for malformed input is not padded: it depends only on the input's syntax, never on the list. Cost: 3 s of a 512 MB function per request (about $0.00002 on arm64), negligible at workshop volume. Tested with fake timers (listed, unlisted, rate-limited and failing requests answer at exactly 3000 ms; a mailer that never resolves still gets the 202 at the deadline; the 400 is immediate).
- **(1.4) Code route throttle:** `POST /api/sign-in/code` has its own throttle on the `$default` stage, 5 requests/s with a burst of 10 (`SIGN_IN_CODE_THROTTLE` in `infra/lib/api-stack.ts`, stage `RouteSettings`, tested), below the stage-wide 50/100: the route needs no sign-in and every well-formed request holds a Lambda for 3 s, so a flood at the stage limit would keep about 150 executions busy. The whole workshop is about 15 people; a throttled request gets API Gateway's 429, which does not depend on list membership. Reserved concurrency was left out because it takes from the account-wide Lambda concurrency pool shared with other projects (Franck's call).
- **(1.4) Notes for 1.5:** verify must burn the whole `CODE` item after 5 wrong tries (shared `attempts`), delete it on success, check that the `ALLOW` entry (with its `email` attribute) still exists at verify time, and bump `version` on every write to the `CODE` item (the code request writes it conditionally on `version`). The sign-in page (1.6) shows a `429` from the code route as "Please try again in a moment", not as an error.
- **(1.5) Routes:** `POST /api/sign-in/verify` (no session; body `{"email","code"}`), and three protected routes served by one function `ssd-workshop-api-participant`: `GET /api/me` (own record `{"id","nickname" or null,"role","group","step"}`), `POST /api/me/nickname` (`{"nickname"}`) and `GET /api/participants/{id}`. All answers are JSON with `cache-control: no-store`.
- **(1.5) One session check:** `platform/api/session/session.ts` `requireSession` reads the cookie from API Gateway's `cookies` list, hashes the token, reads `SESSION#<hash>` (consistent read), compares `expiresAt` with the clock (TTL deletes late), reads the participant's `PROFILE` and returns `{participant, role}`, or `401 {"message":"Please sign in."}` for no cookie, a malformed or unknown (forged) token, an expired session or a missing profile. A table error is a `500 {"message":"Something went wrong. Please try again in a moment."}`, not a 401 (a failure should not sign anyone out). The role comes from the profile, which is refreshed from the allow-list at each sign-in: a role change in the allow-list takes effect at the next sign-in.
- **(1.5) Session cookie:** `__Host-ssd_session=<token>; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Path=/; Max-Age=28800` (the `__Host-` prefix makes browsers refuse it without Secure, with a Domain or another Path). Token: 32 bytes of `crypto.randomBytes`, base64url (43 characters). Stored only as SHA-256 hex in `SESSION#<hash>`/`SESSION` with `tokenHash`, `participantId`, `createdAt` and `expiresAt` (= sign-in + 28,800 s, also the TTL). A fast hash is right here, unlike the code's scrypt: the token holds 256 random bits, so a leaked hash cannot be reversed by guessing. Sessions last 8 hours from sign-in and are not extended by use; there is no sign-out route yet (a page cannot clear an HttpOnly cookie itself); one can come with the pages if needed.
- **(1.5) Code check rules:** the shared `attempts` counts wrong tries over all live codes of an address; the 5th wrong try deletes (burns) the whole `CODE` item, a match deletes it too (no code can be used twice), and an expired code (`expiresAt` <= now) never matches. Every wrong try is counted with one atomic UpdateItem `ADD attempts :one, version :one` (condition `attribute_exists(pk)`, `ReturnValues: UPDATED_NEW`), so parallel guesses are never lost to a race: a burst gets at most 4 refusals before the burn, and the add that returns `attempts` >= 5 deletes the item at the version it returned (if that delete loses a race, the item keeps `attempts` >= 5 and the next read burns it; reads always treat `attempts` >= 5 as burned). A wrong try on an item that vanished meanwhile is refused (`no-code`) and creates nothing. Using a matched code deletes `CODE` conditional on the `version` read; on a conflict the check reads again, at most 3 times, then refuses (`conflict`). Every write bumps or removes `version` (the code request writes `CODE` conditionally on it). A code is 6 ASCII digits after trimming; anything else is a malformed request.
- **(1.5) One refusal, one answer time:** an address not on the list (or whose entry lost its `email`), no live code, an expired, wrong or burned code, a lost race, a table failure and work still running at the deadline all get `401 {"message":"That code is not right or has expired. Ask for a new one."}`, and every well-formed request (success included) is answered exactly `ANSWER_AFTER_MS` = 3000 ms after the handler starts, with the bounded SDK clients of 1.4 (now shared in `platform/api/aws.ts`), tested with fake timers. Malformed input gets `400 {"message":"Send a JSON body with your email address and the 6-digit code."}` and a missing or other content type `415`, both at once and never echoing the input (they depend only on syntax). If the work runs out of time after using the code, the person asks for a new one (rare; accepted).
- **(1.5) JSON content type required on writes:** `POST /api/sign-in/verify` and `POST /api/me/nickname` need `content-type: application/json` (parameters allowed), else `415 {"message":"Send the request as JSON (content-type: application/json)."}`: a cross-site form cannot send that type without a CORS preflight, which the API never grants, on top of SameSite=Strict. The 1.4 code route is unchanged (it sets no cookie and acts on no session).
- **(1.5) Participant record:** on the first successful sign-in the function makes a random UUID (`newParticipantId` in `keys.ts`), links it from the allow-list entry with an UpdateItem of `participantId` only (conditional on the entry existing and having no link, so the import's fields stay and two first sign-ins at once end on one participant), then upserts `PARTICIPANT#<id>`/`PROFILE` with `type`, `id`, `role` and `group` from the entry, `createdAt` and `step` (`nickname`) only if new (`if_not_exists`), `ReturnValues: UPDATED_NEW`. A returning person reuses the linked participant, with role and group refreshed. The allow-list import keeps `participantId` on re-import (its UpdateItem never names it; tested in `platform/src/allowlist/store.test.ts`). No email in the profile, the session, any answer or log.
- **(1.5) Where to go after sign-in:** verify answers `200 {"next":"<step>"}` with the cookie: `nickname` for a new person (or one who never picked one), otherwise the step they are at.
- **(1.5) Nickname rules:** `^[A-Za-z0-9-]{3,20}$` after trimming spaces, then a reserved list compared in lower case (`RESERVED_NICKNAMES` in `platform/api/participant/participant.ts`): admin, administrator, api, apps, assets, claude, facilitator, facilitators, globe, help, intro, login, logout, me, null, root, sign-in, sign-out, signin, static, support, system, undefined, workshop, www, x (site paths and names that could pass for staff or the system). Refusals have distinct reasons: `400 {"reason":"rules"}` "A nickname has 3 to 20 letters, digits or dashes.", `400 {"reason":"reserved"}` "That nickname is reserved. Please pick another one.", `409 {"reason":"taken"}` "That nickname is already taken. Please pick another one."; a body without a string `nickname` gets `400`. A saved nickname answers `200` with the record (step `clarify`).
- **(1.5) Nickname uniqueness:** a `NICK#<lower-case nickname>`/`NICK` claim (`type`, `nickname` as typed, `participantId`, `createdAt`) is put with `attribute_not_exists(pk)` in one `TransactWriteItems` with the profile update (`SET nickname, step = clarify` if the profile exists, has no nickname and is at step `nickname`). A cancelled claim is `taken`; a cancelled profile update is `already-set`.
- **(1.5) Nickname set once:** a second attempt gets `409 {"reason":"already-set","message":"You already have a nickname, and it can't be changed."}`. Changing it later (and freeing the old claim) can come with the facilitator dashboard if needed.
- **(1.5) Own record only:** `GET /api/participants/{id}` answers the caller's own record for their own id; any other id, existing or not, gets the same `404 {"message":"Not found."}` without reading it, for every role (facilitator views of others come in phase 3).
- **(1.5) Sign-in verify function:** `ssd-workshop-api-sign-in-verify` (Node.js 22, arm64, 512 MB for scrypt, 10 s), own role and log group; rights: its logs; `dynamodb:GetItem` in `EMAIL#*`; `dynamodb:UpdateItem` in `EMAIL#*` with `dynamodb:Attributes` `pk`, `sk`, `attempts`, `version`, `participantId`; `dynamodb:DeleteItem` in `EMAIL#*`; `dynamodb:UpdateItem` in `PARTICIPANT#*` with `pk`, `sk`, `type`, `id`, `role`, `group`, `step`, `createdAt` (never `nickname`); `dynamodb:PutItem` in `SESSION#*` with the session's attribute names (lists in `infra/lib/config.ts`, tested against the store's writes). It cannot write `email`, `role` or `group` of an allow-list entry, so it cannot add anyone to the list or change a listed person's role or group there. By design (this is the sign-in) it can write `role` and `group` on any `PARTICIPANT#*` profile and create a session for any participant id. Residual risks, accepted like 1.4's: code running as this role (a bug or a compromised function) could give any profile any role, sign in as anyone by putting a session, delete an `ALLOW` entry or point another entry's `participantId` elsewhere (IAM has no sort-key condition, and an attribute list on DeleteItem would not prevent it); the shipped code takes role and group only from the allow-list entry, puts a session only after a matched code, deletes only `CODE` items at a version it read or was returned, and links only an entry with no link. The fix if ever needed is the same: codes in their own `CODE#` partition. Route throttle like the code route: 5 requests/s, burst 10 (`SIGN_IN_VERIFY_THROTTLE`).
- **(1.5) Profile step after sign-in:** the profile upsert keeps `ReturnValues: UPDATED_NEW` (`SET step = if_not_exists(step, :first)` counts as an update of `step`, so it comes back even when unchanged). Not `ALL_NEW`: it would return `nickname` and any other attribute outside the role's `dynamodb:Attributes` list (AWS pairs such a list with `ReturnValues` `NONE`, `UPDATED_OLD` or `UPDATED_NEW`). If `step` is ever missing or unknown in the answer, the sign-in still succeeds with `{"next":"nickname"}` instead of failing; the live check in `infra/README.md` proves a returning person gets `{"next":"clarify"}`.
- **(1.5) Participant function:** `ssd-workshop-api-participant` (Node.js 22, arm64, 256 MB, 10 s), own role and log group; rights: its logs; `dynamodb:GetItem` in `SESSION#*` and `PARTICIPANT#*`; `dynamodb:PutItem` in `NICK#*` with the claim's attribute names; `dynamodb:UpdateItem` in `PARTICIPANT#*` with `pk`, `sk`, `nickname`, `step` only (a transaction is authorized item by item; there is no `TransactWriteItems` action). It cannot create sessions or touch the allow-list. One Lambda permission scoped to this API (`<api id>/*/*/*`, `scopePermissionToRoute: false`) instead of one per route, because CDK writes the `{id}` path parameter literally into the per-route source ARN (whether API Gateway's invoke ARN would match it is unverified until a deployed request proves it). No access stack change: the boundary already allows `dynamodb:*` on `table/ssd-workshop-*`, and the deploy rights already create functions, roles and routes.
- **(1.5) Accepted risk, codes burned by a stranger:** anyone who knows a listed address can burn that person's live codes with 5 wrong guesses (the spec's burn rule) and, with the 1.4 limit of 5 codes per hour, keep them out for about an hour; the answers reveal no membership. Accepted for a ~15-person workshop; a facilitator reset comes with the phase 3 dashboard.
- **(1.5) Accepted risk, sessions not re-checked:** a session is not re-checked against the allow-list, so a removed or demoted person keeps their session and role for up to 8 hours, and there is no sign-out route. Harmless in phase 1 (every role sees only its own record); must be revisited before phase 3 gives roles views of others. Closed by ticket 3.1 (decisions "(3.1) Session check" and "(3.1) Sign-out route").
- **(1.5) Notes for 1.6:** the verify route is throttled too (5 requests/s, burst 10), so the sign-in page shows a `429` from `POST /api/sign-in/verify` as "Please try again in a moment", not as an error (like the code route's). On `{"next":"nickname"}` the page takes the step from `GET /api/me` before showing the nickname form (it is the source of truth if the verify answer ever falls back to `nickname`).
- **(1.5) Logs:** one JSON line per request: `{"event":"sign-in-verify","outcome":...}` (`signed-in`, `not-listed`, `no-code`, `wrong-code`, `burned`, `conflict`, `invalid-request`, `unsupported-media-type`, `failed` with step and error name or `Timeout`) and `{"event":"participant","route":"me"|"nickname"|"participant","outcome":...}` (`ok`, `unauthorized`, `not-found`, `rules`, `reserved`, `taken`, `already-set`, `invalid-request`, `unsupported-media-type`, `failed` with the error name). No address, code, token, token hash, nickname, id or body; a capture test proves it.
- **(1.5) Shared helpers:** the JSON body reader (size limits, base64), `jsonResponse` and the content-type check moved to `platform/api/http.ts`, used by the code request too; `codesFromItem` is shared by both sign-in stores; the in-memory `FakeTable` (`platform/api/fakes.ts`) backs the verify, session and participant tests.
- **(1.6) Pages from TypeScript, built at synth time:** sources in `platform/web/` (`index.html`, `style.css`, `src/*.ts`); `buildWeb` (`platform/web/build.ts`) copies the two static files and bundles `src/main.ts` with esbuild (already used for the Lambda code; now also a `platform` dev dependency, same version) into one minified `app.js`. The site stack calls it at synth time into `cdk.out/ssd-workshop-web` and uploads only that folder, so `npm run synth`, CI and the deploy workflow's `cdk deploy` always publish a fresh build, nothing compiled is committed, and no TypeScript, test or build file reaches the bucket (tested). esbuild rather than `tsc`: one file instead of one per module, and a library call from the stack; type checking stays with `tsc`.
- **(1.6) DOM types only for the pages:** `platform/web/tsconfig.json` adds `DOM` to `lib` for `platform/web/**`, which the root `tsconfig.json` now excludes; `npm run typecheck` runs both projects. Node.js code never sees browser globals.
- **(1.6) One page, screens shown and hidden:** all screens are static sections of `index.html` (hidden until `app.js` shows one); the script only toggles `hidden` and sets text with `textContent` (no `innerHTML`). No script, style or event handler inline (tested on the built page). Without JavaScript only a "This page needs JavaScript" notice shows; forms use `method="post"`, so even then an address could never land in a URL.
- **(1.6) Heading:** the page's `<h1>` is "SSD Workshop" on every screen (each screen has an `<h2>` that takes the focus or whose field does); the deploy smoke check now looks for "SSD Workshop" (`deploy-workflow.test.ts` updated) and `infra/README.md`'s first check for `<h1>SSD Workshop</h1>`.
- **(1.6) Flow:** a page load calls `GET /api/me`: 200 goes to the person's step (no nickname yet: nickname screen), 401 to the email screen. After a right code the page always reads `GET /api/me` and goes where it says (the source of truth, whatever `next` the verify answer gave). A nickname refused as `already-set` also re-reads `GET /api/me`. A 401 on the nickname route goes back to the email screen with "Please sign in again." The answer rules are pure functions in `platform/web/src/flow.ts`, unit tested.
- **(1.6) Email address in memory only:** kept in one variable between asking for a code and a right code, then dropped and the field cleared; never in the URL, `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, a cookie or the console (URL, storage and cookies asserted in the browser test; the page has no `console` call, checked by review). The deploy smoke check also asks for `app.js` (200), since the heading "SSD Workshop" alone matched the old placeholder too. "Send me a new code" reuses it; "Use a different email" drops it.
- **(1.6) Neutral message:** after any `202` from the code route the code screen shows the page's own copy of the API's neutral sentence, ignoring the body, so the page cannot differ for listed and unlisted addresses. A `429` from the code or verify route shows "Please try again in a moment." as information (not in the error colour); a network failure shows "We couldn't reach the workshop. Check your connection and try again."; anything else "Something went wrong. Please try again in a moment."
- **(1.6) Quick checks before calling the API:** the email field must look like `x@y.z` (the API applies the full rule) and the code must be 6 digits after removing spaces; otherwise the page says so without a request.
- **(1.6) Wording:** buttons "Send me a code", "Sign in" (waiting: "Checking…" plus "Checking your code. This takes a few seconds."), "Send me a new code", "Use a different email", "Save my nickname"; code screen hint "Not in your inbox? Check your Junk folder."; nickname screen: "Others in the workshop see your nickname, never your email address. You can't change it later." and the highlighted rule "**No confidential or customer data** in your nickname or anywhere in the workshop."
- **(1.6) Step screen:** heading "Step 1: Clarify", "Hi <nickname>, you're signed in." and one line on what step 1 will do; ticket 1.8 fills this section in. A person at a later step (none exist before later tickets) sees "Welcome back" without that line.
- **(1.6) Pages' headers policy:** a custom CloudFront `ResponseHeadersPolicy` `ssd-workshop-pages-v1` on the default (pages) behaviour replaces the managed `SecurityHeadersPolicy` there: `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; object-src 'none'` (`PAGES_CONTENT_SECURITY_POLICY` in `infra/lib/config.ts`), HSTS one year (no subdomains, no preload, as before), `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, and `X-Frame-Options: DENY` (was `SAMEORIGIN`; nothing frames the site, matching `frame-ancestors 'none'`). `X-XSS-Protection` is dropped: browsers no longer use it and OWASP advises against it; the CSP covers it. All headers override the origin's. `/api/*` keeps the managed policy. No access stack change: `cloudfront:CreateResponseHeadersPolicy` is already allowed; update and delete are not, so the policy is retained and a change of any header bumps `PAGES_HEADERS_POLICY_VERSION`, which changes both the name (`-v2`) and the logical id (`PagesHeadersV2`; a new name alone would be an in-place update), so CloudFormation creates a replacement and leaves the old policy for Franck to delete. No cost.
- **(1.6) Browser tests in `npm test`:** `platform/web/pages.test.ts` drives Chromium through the `playwright` library from vitest (one test command; no `@playwright/test` runner), at 375 × 667, against the built site served by a small `node:http` server that sends the real CSP, with `/api/*` mocked by `page.route`. Any console error (a CSP refusal included) fails the test, except Chromium's own line for a non-2xx answer. `playwright` is pinned exactly (`1.63.0`, a `platform` dev dependency) so its browser build matches; CI and the deploy workflow run `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium` before `npm test`.
- **(1.6) Cache:** the page, stylesheet and script keep the 60-second cache of 0.4, so for up to a minute after a deploy a browser may combine a new page with an old script; acceptable while nothing is live in a session. Fingerprinted file names can come if it ever matters.
- **(1.7) Hand-off through a queue (SQS approved by Franck, 2026-11-23, as a new paid service at about $0 a month):** the conversation function cannot invoke the worker (the boundary has no `lambda:InvokeFunction`, and invoke permissions for other principals are denied), so a stored message goes to the SQS queue `ssd-workshop-ai-messages` (SQS-managed encryption, 1 day retention) and an event source mapping (batch size 1) runs `ssd-workshop-api-ai-worker`. The queue message holds only `{"participantId","messageId"}`, never the text. No queue policy (identity-based rights only; `sqs:AddPermission` is denied). No access stack change: the boundary and deploy rights already cover `sqs:*` on `ssd-workshop-*`, event source mappings of `ssd-workshop-*` functions and their tags, and `bedrock:InvokeModel` on `eu.anthropic.*` profiles and `anthropic.*` models.
- **(1.7) Queue timings:** worker timeout 300 s; visibility timeout 330 s (just above the timeout, not AWS's six times: with batch size 1 a longer one only delays the retry past the stale limit), so SQS delivers a crashed job again about 5.5 minutes later while its message is still pending; a job whose worker crashed 3 times goes to `ssd-workshop-ai-messages-dlq` (14 days). The last run starts by about 660 s and ends by about 960 s, within `STALE_PENDING_SECONDS` = 1200 s (20 minutes, was 10; `infra/lib/app.test.ts` checks the sum). The AI calls are cut off 30 s before the Lambda timeout (`WRITE_RESERVE_MS`), which leaves time for the final write and its retries. Risk: the worker has no reserved concurrency in the shared account, and a throttled invoke still uses up a receive, so in a burst at the account's Lambda limit a job could reach the dead-letter queue; the participant then sees "failed" and can send again.
- **(1.7) At most 5 workers at once:** `maxConcurrency` 5 on the SQS event source (AWS's minimum is 2), so a burst of messages waits in the queue (without using up receives) instead of hitting Bedrock throttling; a message waiting there still counts towards the 20-minute stale limit, which leaves about 4 minutes of slack.
- **(1.7) Failures and retries:** a Bedrock error (after the SDK's 3 attempts with backoff), the deadline, an empty answer or a failed history read ends the message as `failed` at once with "Sorry, something went wrong. Please send your message again." and frees the conversation (no SQS retry, which would wait for the visibility timeout). The final write is tried 3 times (`FINISH_ATTEMPTS`, waits of 200 ms and 800 ms; a lost race is not retried); if the last try fails, the tokens already spent are added to `USAGE` on their own (one `ADD`; its own failure is ignored) and the error is thrown, so SQS delivers the job again; a failed read of the message is thrown too. A redelivered job runs from the start, and its Bedrock calls are counted again (they are spent too). The handler logs a thrown error as `{"event":"ai-worker","outcome":"error","error":<name>}` and throws a bare `Error("job failed")`, so the Lambda runtime never writes the original message or stack to CloudWatch. A pending message older than 20 minutes (`STALE_PENDING_SECONDS`, only a job that crashed 3 times or waited long in the queue leaves one) is shown as `failed` and no longer blocks a new message; its late job is skipped.
- **(1.7) Idempotent worker:** the worker skips a job whose message is not `pending` (or is stale), and stores the outcome in one `TransactWriteItems`: the message's status (condition: still `pending`), the reply (condition: none yet), the conversation (condition: this message is still in flight) and the usage `ADD`. A job delivered twice is therefore answered and counted once (two Bedrock calls at most, if both deliveries run at the same moment).
- **(1.7) Conversation items:** participant messages at `PARTICIPANT#<id>`/`MSG#<message id>` (`type`, `author`, `step`, `text`, `status` pending/done/declined/flagged/failed, `createdAt`, `pendingSince`, after an approval `approvedTextHash`, then the guardrail's `verdict`, `verdictReason`, `suggestion` and `guardrail*` tokens and cost); the reply at `MSG#<message id>-r`, which sorts right after its message (`kind` answer/decline/failure, `text`, `replyTo`, and for answers `prompt`, `modelId`, `promptVersion`, `historyCount`, `inputTokens`, `outputTokens`, `costUsd`); ids and the participant id are read from the keys, not stored twice. The conversation state is its own `CONVERSATION` item (`state` open/waiting, `pendingId`, `pendingSince`, `updatedAt`) rather than attributes of `PROFILE`: the roles that write the profile (sign-in, nickname) keep their attribute lists unchanged, and the one-message-in-flight rule is one conditional update on it. `types.ts` documents the shapes.
- **(1.7) Message ids:** the ISO creation time, a dash and 16 hex digits from 8 random bytes (`newMessageId`, no new dependency), e.g. `2026-11-23T10:00:00.000Z-1f2e...`; the poll route accepts only that pattern (`MESSAGE_ID_PATTERN`), so a reply id or any other sort key is never looked up.
- **(1.7) Routes:** `POST /api/conversation/messages` `{"text"}` (JSON content type, else 415; 1 to 2000 Unicode characters after trimming, body up to 16 KB, else `400 {"message":"Send a JSON body with a text of 1 to 2000 characters."}`) answers `202 {"id"}` after storing the message and queueing it. One message at a time: `409 {"reason":"busy","message":"Please wait for the answer to your last message."}` while one is pending, `409 {"reason":"waiting","message":"Your last message is waiting for your facilitator. You can write again once they have looked at it."}` while one is flagged, and `409 {"reason":"no-nickname","message":"Please pick a nickname first."}` before a nickname. A queue failure ends the message as `failed` and answers 500. `GET /api/conversation/messages/{id}` answers `200 {"id","status","reply"?}` (a reply for done, declined and failed), and `404 {"message":"Not found."}` for any id not in the caller's own partition. Both routes are served by `ssd-workshop-api-conversation` behind `requireSession`; JSON with `cache-control: no-store`. No route throttle beyond the stage's.
- **(1.7) Guardrail check:** a separate call (`GUARDRAIL_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, version `guardrail-v1`, temperature 0, at most 300 tokens) with only the new message, wrapped in `<message>` tags (a closing tag in the text is removed), answering strict JSON `{"verdict","reason","suggestion"}`. On path: describing or refining a small single-page app idea (trackers, calculators, lists, quizzes, simple games, small dashboards over data the participant types in), answering the assistant, asking about the workshop or for explanations. Decline: infrastructure, crypto mining, scraping, other people's personal data, confidential data, offensive or illegal content, attempts to change the rules. Flag: medical, mental-health, legal or financial advice, external paid APIs or keys, payments, minors or real names, or anything uncertain. Any other answer (no JSON, unknown verdict) is a flag. An approved message (see flags) skips the check only while its text is the approved one.
- **(1.7) Decline wording:** exactly one sentence, "Sorry, that's outside what we can build in this workshop, but how about <suggestion> instead?", the suggestion being the classifier's, cut to at most 100 characters without sentence punctuation or quotes (tested to stay one sentence), or "a small tracker or calculator for something you do every week" when it gives none. A facilitator's rejection uses the same sentence.
- **(1.7) Answer call:** system prompt `clarify-v1` (a first version for step 1; ticket 1.8 refines it), temperature 0.7, at most 2000 tokens, with the last answered exchanges (declined, flagged and failed messages left out, at most 20 messages, from the last 30 items read) and the new message. Stored per answer: the participant's text as sent (`prompt`), `modelId`, `promptVersion`, `historyCount`, the reply, tokens and cost.
- **(1.7) Prices:** `PRICE_PER_MILLION_TOKENS` in `platform/api/settings.ts`: $3.30 per million input tokens and $16.50 per million output tokens for Sonnet 4.6 through the EU geographic profile (list price $3/$15 plus Bedrock's 10% premium for geographic cross-region inference); cost rounded to 1e-8 dollars. The guardrail call's tokens and cost are stored on the message, the answer's on the reply, and both are added to the participant's `USAGE` totals (`inputTokens`, `outputTokens`, `costUsd`) with one atomic `ADD` in the same transaction. Enforcing the $5 cap is phase 3.
- **(1.7) One model setting:** `MODEL_ID = "eu.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6"` in `platform/api/settings.ts` is the source; smoke's `DEFAULT_MODEL_ID` now uses it, and `infra/lib/config.ts` repeats it for the worker's IAM resources (a test checks they are equal, as for the table name).
- **(1.7) Conversation function:** `ssd-workshop-api-conversation` (Node.js 22, arm64, 256 MB, 10 s), own role and log group; rights: its logs; `dynamodb:GetItem` in `SESSION#*` and `PARTICIPANT#*`; `dynamodb:UpdateItem` in `PARTICIPANT#*` with `CONVERSATION_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` (`pk`, `sk`, `type`, `author`, `step`, `text`, `status`, `createdAt`, `pendingSince`, `state`, `pendingId`, `updatedAt`, `kind`, `replyTo`); `sqs:SendMessage` on the queue. One Lambda permission scoped to this API (`scopePermissionToRoute: false`, path parameter route).
- **(1.7) AI worker:** `ssd-workshop-api-ai-worker` (Node.js 22, arm64, 256 MB, 300 s), own role and log group, no route; rights: its logs; `dynamodb:GetItem` and `dynamodb:Query` in `PARTICIPANT#*`; `dynamodb:UpdateItem` in `PARTICIPANT#*` with `AI_WORKER_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` (the message, reply, conversation and usage attributes; never `step`, `role`, `group`, `nickname`, `id` or `approvedTextHash`); `bedrock:InvokeModel` on `inference-profile/eu.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6` in eu-central-1 and `arn:aws:bedrock:eu-*::foundation-model/anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6` (the EU profile routes to the model in several EU regions); the consume rights on its queue (CDK's `SqsEventSource` grant). Bedrock client: 3 attempts, 3 s to connect, 120 s per request, plus the job's deadline.
- **(1.7) Updates only, the approval tied to the text and written by nobody but the facilitator:** both functions write with `UpdateItem` (inside transactions), never `PutItem`, so they cannot replace or empty another item of a participant partition. An approval stores `approvedTextHash`, the SHA-256 (hex) of the approved message text (no plain `approved` flag), and the worker skips the guardrail only when the message's current text has that hash; neither role may write `approvedTextHash` (tested on the attribute lists and the synthesized policies), so only `npm run flags -- approve` (Franck's credentials) can set it, and a text rewritten after the approval (both roles may write `text`, `status` and the conversation's `pendingId`) is checked again. Residual risk, accepted like 1.4's and 1.5's: code running as either role could change the listed attributes on any item of any participant partition (e.g. a profile's `type`, or the conversation function's `step`), or fake a reply; it can never change a role, group or nickname, read emails or get a text past the guardrail without a facilitator's approval of that exact text.
- **(1.7) `npm run flags -- list | approve <nickname> | reject <nickname>`:** root script `npm run -s flags -w platform --` (`platform/src/flags/`: `command.ts` tested against the in-memory fakes, `store.ts`, `main.ts`), run with the owner's own credentials. The nickname (any letter case) is resolved through its `NICK#` claim. `list` prints "Waiting for a decision: N" and, for each waiting participant (alphabetical by nickname, a paginated `Scan` of `CONVERSATION` items in state `waiting`), the nickname, `  Message: <the flagged text>` and `  Flagged because: <the guardrail's reason>`, so a decision is never blind; control and format characters in them (escape sequences, line breaks, direction overrides) are printed as spaces. `approve` sets the flagged message back to `pending` with `approvedTextHash` = SHA-256 of the text it read and the conversation to `open` (one transaction, conditional on it still being flagged and waiting), then queues it, and the worker answers it without a new check while the text is unchanged; `reject` declines it with the one-sentence decline and frees the conversation. Exit 1 for an unknown nickname, nothing waiting, a decision made meanwhile, or an AWS error (printed through `redactError`). Message texts and reasons appear only on the owner's own terminal (`list`), never in a log; no command prints an id or an email address (a participant could still type one into a message, which `list` shows as typed).
- **(1.7) `npm run eval -- guardrails`:** root script `npm run -s eval -w platform --` (`platform/src/eval/`: `logic.ts` with the suite switch, the file check and the scoring, tested with a fake classifier; `main.ts` calls Bedrock with the caller's credentials, `maxAttempts` 4). It runs the real `checkGuardrail` on every example of `platform/api/eval/guardrails.json` (`category`, `text`, `expected`: 8 on-path, 9 decline over infrastructure, crypto mining, scraping, other people's data and offensive, 3 flag), one at a time, prints one PASS/FAIL line per example and a summary with tokens and cost (about $0.05), and exits 1 on any mismatch or error. `clarify` and `spec` suites can be added to `SUITES` later.
- **(1.7) Logs:** one JSON line per request, `{"event":"conversation","route":"send"|"poll"|"unknown","outcome":...}` (`accepted`, `ok`, `unauthorized`, `not-found`, `invalid-request`, `unsupported-media-type`, `busy`, `waiting`, `no-nickname`, `failed` with the error name), and one per job, `{"event":"ai-worker","outcome":...,"verdict"?,"error"?}` (`done`, `declined`, `flagged`, `failed`, `skipped`, `invalid`, and `error` with the error name when the job is thrown back to SQS). Never the text, the prompt, the reply, the suggestion, an id, the nickname or the token; a capture test proves it.
- **(1.7) Dependency:** `@aws-sdk/client-sqs` `^3.1175.0` (same range as the other SDK clients) to send to the queue from the conversation function and the flags command; `@aws-sdk/client-bedrock-runtime` was already there (smoke). Fakes, not a mocking library, as in 1.4 (`platform/api/conversation/fakes.ts`).
- **(1.7) Notes for 1.8:** the page polls `GET /api/conversation/messages/{id}` every 2 seconds; a `flagged` status means "waiting for your facilitator" (it has no reply until a decision), and the 409 reasons `busy`, `waiting` and `no-nickname` tell the page what to show. There is no route yet to read the conversation's history and state on a page load: 1.8 adds one (`GET /api/conversation`) when it builds the page.
- **(1.8) Clarify prompt `clarify-v2`:** a new idea (the first message, or a changed one) gets one sentence restating it, then 3 to 5 questions as a numbered list (`1. ...`) about who it is for, the main things it does, the information it keeps and what a first version leaves out. After an answer the AI checks those four big points: if all are known it offers the spec instead of asking, otherwise it asks only about the missing ones (numbered, fewer is fine). A changed idea is followed and the old one dropped. Small details (units, first day of the week, whether past entries are listed, wording, colours) are never asked about: the spec picks defaults and step 2 can change them. Plain text, no Markdown, about 120 words at most. `platform/api/conversation/answer.ts`.
- **(1.8) "Ready" signal:** when the AI offers to write the spec it ends its reply with the line `[READY_FOR_SPEC]`; the worker strips it (small variations such as `[ready for spec]` too) and stores `offersSpec: true` (DynamoDB `BOOL`) on the reply. `offersSpec` is added to `AI_WORKER_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` (not to the conversation function's list). A reply that is only the marker counts as empty (neutral failure). The stored text, which the model sees as history, never holds the marker.
- **(1.8) Answer temperature 0.4 (was 0.7):** `ANSWER_TEMPERATURE`; at 0.7 the model added "just one more thing" questions in about one run of three instead of offering the spec. With 0.4 and the prompt above, 6 live eval runs in a row passed 5/5.
- **(1.8) `npm run eval -- clarify`:** `platform/api/eval/clarify.json` holds 5 examples sent to the real answer call (`answerMessage`, clarify prompt, no guardrail call): the three ideas (fitness tracker, movies tracker, nutrition calculator) as first replies, which must hold 3 to 5 numbered items numbered 1, 2, 3..., each with a question mark, and no offer; a change of mind (a scripted movie-watchlist exchange, then "Actually, forget that. I'd rather build an app that reminds me when to water my house plants."), whose reply must name the new idea (a word starting with `plant` or `water`) and whose 1 to 5 numbered questions must not name the old one (whole words `movie`, `film`, `watchlist`, `rating`, `rate`, `stream`, `genre`, `poster`, plural allowed; the intro sentence may mention it); and a fully answered run log, whose reply must offer the spec, ask no numbered question and name the "Write my spec" button. Deterministic checks rather than a judge call: cheap, repeatable and easy to read. Each result prints the reply under its PASS/FAIL line. About $0.02 a run.
- **(1.8) `GET /api/conversation`:** answers `200 {"step","state","messages":[{"id","author","text","status"?,"offersSpec"?}],"pendingId"?}` for the caller only (`requireSession`, Query of their own `PARTICIPANT#` partition), `cache-control: no-store`. The last 50 messages and replies (`HISTORY_PAGE_LIMIT`, newest 50 items, shown oldest first). A reply's id is `<message id>-r`; participant messages carry their status, a stale pending one shows as `failed` followed by the neutral failure reply; `pendingId` is the message in flight while it is pending (not stale) or flagged, `state` is `waiting` while a facilitator decides. Prompts, tokens, costs and guardrail reasons are not returned.
- **(1.8) Write my spec, `POST /api/conversation/step` `{"step":"spec"}`:** JSON only (415 otherwise; any other body `400 {"message":"Send a JSON body with the step \"spec\"."}`). Moves the profile's `step` from `clarify` to `spec` in one transaction with a condition on the conversation item (exists, and no message in flight or only a stale pending one), so a message sent meanwhile cannot slip through. Refusals: `409 too-early` "Tell the AI about your idea and answer its questions first; then it can write your spec." (no answered message among the last 50 items), `409 busy` / `409 waiting` (same messages as for sending), `409 no-nickname`, `409 wrong-step` "You are not at step 1 any more." (step `approved`). Already at `spec`: `200 {"step":"spec"}` again (a double press is harmless).
- **(1.8) Least privilege for the new routes:** both are served by `ssd-workshop-api-conversation` (no new function). It gains `dynamodb:Query` on `PARTICIPANT#*` (`dynamodb:LeadingKeys`; the caller's own partition is chosen by `requireSession`); the step move uses the existing `UpdateItem` right and attribute list (`step`, `updatedAt` and the condition's `pk`, `pendingId`, `state`, `pendingSince` were already on it). No access stack change.
- **(1.8) Write my spec on the page:** shown once at least one message was answered (the participant steers and may ask for the spec early); a quiet outlined button with "Ready when you are: keep answering, or ask for your spec now." until the AI's latest reply offered, then the filled main button with "The AI has enough to write your spec." Disabled while a message is in flight. A refusal shows the API's message under the form.
- **(1.8) Polling:** after a send the page shows "The AI is writing…" with three animated dots (still for reduced motion), disables Send, and polls `GET /api/conversation/messages/{id}` every 2 seconds; a reply stops polling and the page re-reads `GET /api/conversation` (which carries `offersSpec`). A `flagged` answer hides the dots and shows "Your message is waiting for your facilitator. You can write again once they have looked at it."; the page then checks every 10 seconds until the decision. A network error, 429 or 5xx keeps polling; 404 re-reads the conversation; 401 goes back to sign-in. A `busy` or `waiting` refusal on send re-reads the conversation (another tab sent a message). A page load or reload reads the conversation and resumes polling a pending message, or shows the facilitator notice.
- **(1.8) Step 1 screen:** "Step 1: Clarify", "Hi <nickname>! Describe your app idea in a sentence or two. The AI asks a few questions to make it clear; answer, change your mind or steer as you like.", the Behind the scenes box, the conversation as bubbles (yours on the right, the AI's on the left, line breaks kept), the message box "Your message" (up to 2000 characters) with **Send**, then Write my spec. Replaces 1.6's placeholder line; the "Welcome back" heading for later steps is gone.
- **(1.8) Behind the scenes box:** a row of four labelled steps, "Your message → Safety check → Claude on Bedrock → Reply" (HTML list and CSS, no image), and one sentence: "A quick check first makes sure your message fits the workshop. Then Claude, an AI model running on Amazon Bedrock, reads your idea and the conversation so far, and asks about what is still unclear." While waiting, "Safety check" and "Claude on Bedrock" are highlighted (the page cannot tell which of the two is running); for a flagged message "Safety check"; once a reply is there "Reply". The diagram-led panels are phase 5.
- **(1.8) Step 2 screen:** a separate screen "Step 2: Spec" with "Hi <nickname>. Next, the AI writes a short spec of your app: what it does, its screens, the data it keeps and a diagram." Shown after Write my spec and on any load at step `spec` (or `approved`, until 1.9); ticket 1.9 fills it in.
- **(1.8) Browser tests:** the 2-second polling is tested with Playwright's `page.clock` (installed, then paused, so only `runFor` moves page time): no poll before 2 s, one per 2 s, none after the reply, one per 10 s while flagged.
- **(1.9) Spec format:** short Markdown: a title line `# <app name>`, one Mermaid flowchart (```` ```mermaid ```` fence, `flowchart TD`, screens as boxes, the data kept as cylinders, labelled arrows, 4 to 10 nodes, plain-word labels without symbols, no styles, classes, links or subgraphs), then exactly `## What it does`, `## Screens` (2 to 4 views of the one page, `- Name: ...`) and `## Data it keeps`, about 300 words, no technical words. `checkSpec` (`platform/api/conversation/spec.ts`) checks the shape (title, exactly one flowchart, the three sections in order and not empty, nothing else, at most 8,000 characters); Mermaid itself checks that the diagram parses (the page, and the eval). The diagram comes before the sections in the text and on the page (diagrams lead).
- **(1.9) Prompt `spec-v1`, one pipeline:** step 2 messages go through the same queue, worker and guardrail as step 1; the worker picks the prompt by the message's step. The system prompt carries the current spec (`<current_spec>`), or says there is none yet; the history is the last answered exchanges as in step 1 (step 1's conversation, then step 2's). A question gets an answer of up to about 120 words and no spec; a change gets one or two sentences, then the whole new spec between a line `<spec>` and a line `</spec>`; unsure means answer and ask. Temperature 0.4 (as clarify), at most 3,000 output tokens (`SPEC_MAX_TOKENS`; step 1 keeps 2,000).
- **(1.9) The spec block never reaches the conversation:** the worker splits it off (`extractSpec`, like 1.8's marker): the reply stores only the words around it (or "Here is your spec. Ask me anything about it, or ask for a change." when there are none) plus `specVersion`, so the model never sees old specs as history and the page shows the spec once, above the conversation. A block that is not closed (cut off), a new spec of the wrong shape, or no spec while there is none yet, ends the message as `failed` with the neutral reply (error names `MalformedSpec`, `MissingSpec`); the spec stays as it was.
- **(1.9) Versions:** each new spec is its own item `PARTICIPANT#<id>`/`SPEC#<4-digit version>` (`type` spec, `specVersion`, `text`, `messageId`, `createdAt`, later `approvedAt`), written once by the worker in the transaction that stores the answer (condition: not there yet), with the reply's `specVersion` and the conversation's `latestSpecVersion`. Every version is kept; versions run from 1 to 9,999. Readers require `specVersion`, which only the worker's role may write, so the conversation function cannot make a spec version.
- **(1.9) Write my spec asks for the first version:** after the move to step 2, `POST /api/conversation/step` stores the message "Please write my spec." (`WRITE_SPEC_REQUEST`) at step `spec` and queues it, answering `200 {"step":"spec","id"}`. The worker skips the guardrail check for exactly this text at step 2: it is the platform's own words, so whoever sends it, nothing else gets past the check (one model call saved). The message and its reply show in step 2's conversation. Pressed again at step 2 while there is no spec and nothing in flight (the first try failed), it asks again; with a spec, or with the request in flight, it answers `200 {"step":"spec"}` and asks nothing. The step 2 page shows **Write my spec** again in that case.
- **(1.9) `GET /api/conversation/spec`:** the caller's newest version, or `?version=n` (a plain whole number from 1 to 9999; anything else, or a version the caller does not have, is `404 {"message":"Not found."}`, as is "no spec yet"): `200 {"version","text","createdAt","approved"}`, `cache-control: no-store`. A query parameter rather than a path parameter, so the Lambda permission stays as it is.
- **(1.9) Approve spec, `POST /api/conversation/spec/approve` `{"version": n}`:** the version the participant sees (JSON only, 415 otherwise; any other body `400 {"message":"Send a JSON body with the version of the spec you approve."}`). One transaction: the profile's `step` from `spec` to `approved`, the conversation's `approvedSpecVersion` (conditions: that version is `latestSpecVersion`, none was approved, and no message in flight; a stale pending one is let go by removing `pendingId`, so its late answer cannot land), and `approvedAt` on the version item. Answers `200 {"step":"approved","version"}`; refusals `409` with `reason` `stale` "There is a newer version of your spec. Please have a look at it, then approve it.", `no-spec` "There is no spec to approve yet." (also at step 1), `busy` / `waiting` (as for sending), `approved` "Your spec is approved, so it stays as it is for now. The build comes next." (another version after an approval). The same version again is `200` (a double press is harmless).
- **(1.9) After approval, no more messages:** `POST /api/conversation/messages` at step `approved` answers `409 approved` (message above), and the store refuses too: `createMessage`'s condition now includes `attribute_not_exists(approvedSpecVersion)` on the conversation, so a send racing the approval cannot slip through. Write my spec answers `409 wrong-step`. Phase 2's build flow decides how changes reopen.
- **(1.9) History route additions:** `GET /api/conversation` messages carry `step` (a reply its message's step) and replies `specVersion`; the body adds `latestSpecVersion` and `approvedSpecVersion` when set. Step 1's screen shows the whole conversation as before; step 2 shows only step 2's messages.
- **(1.9) Least privilege:** no new function or IAM action. `AI_WORKER_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` gains `specVersion`, `messageId` and `latestSpecVersion` (never `approvedSpecVersion` or `approvedAt`, tested); `CONVERSATION_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` gains `approvedSpecVersion`, `approvedAt` and `latestSpecVersion` (a name in the approval's condition; never `specVersion`, tested). The worker reads the newest version with its existing `Query` right, the conversation function the spec with its existing `GetItem` and `Query` rights. Residual risk, as in 1.7: code running as the conversation role could set `approvedSpecVersion` or `latestSpecVersion` on any participant's conversation, or change the `text` of an existing version item; it cannot make a new version (no `specVersion`) or get a text past the guardrail.
- **(1.9) Step 2 screen:** "Step 2: Spec", one line on what happens, then the spec card ("Version N", the title, the diagram as a picture on white, "Diagram as text" in a closed details element, then the sections as headings, paragraphs and lists; Markdown bold marks are dropped), then a Behind the scenes box ("Your message → Safety check → Claude on Bedrock → Answer or new version", "Claude, an AI model running on Amazon Bedrock, writes the spec from your conversation in step 1. A question gets an answer and leaves the spec as it is; a change saves a new version, and every version is kept."), step 2's conversation, the box "Ask a question or ask for a change" with **Send**, and "Happy with it? Approve your spec. You can still ask questions or ask for changes until you do." with **Approve spec** (disabled while a message is in flight). Waiting text "The AI is writing your spec…" before the first version, "The AI is writing…" after. After approval: "Spec approved. Next comes the build: the AI turns this spec into your app. Your facilitator will tell you when the build starts." at the top, the spec marked "Version N (approved)", no message box and no button. The page is 48rem wide at step 2 (30rem elsewhere) so the diagram is large on a laptop. Polling as in step 1. Older versions are kept and readable through the API but not listed on the page.
- **(1.9 fix) A new spec version is brought into view:** Franck's live check: after "Add a session history screen" the AI said "Here is the updated spec!" and version 2 was saved and drawn, but at the top of the page, above a long conversation, so on a phone nothing changed where he was looking. Now, when a reply saves a new version, the page scrolls the spec to the top of the screen and moves the focus to it, and the reply that saved the version on screen carries a "See version n of the spec" button that scrolls back to it later (older replies carry none: older versions are not shown on the page). A question leaves the reader where they are. (`platform/web/src/main.ts`, `pages.test.ts` "WHEN a change saves a new version ...")
- **(1.9) Diagram drawing, dependency `mermaid` 12.3.0 (pinned, released 2026-11-03):** the standard Mermaid renderer; nothing smaller draws Mermaid. It is bundled by esbuild into its own script `diagram.js` (about 5.4 MB, 1.5 MB gzipped), loaded only when a spec is shown, so the sign-in and step 1 pages stay small. A dev dependency of `platform` (only the site build and the eval use it). `checkSpec` also refuses a diagram with a `click`, `href` or `call` line (defence in depth for the "loose" mode, before facilitators can view participants' specs).
- **(1.9) Diagram under the Content-Security-Policy, no policy change:** Mermaid styles its SVG with a `<style>` element and style attributes, which `style-src 'self'` refuses (60 violations in a first try). While Mermaid draws (in an off-screen element), `diagram.ts` turns `setAttribute("style", ...)` into CSSOM writes (allowed) and `document.createElement("style")` into an inert SVG `<desc>` placeholder; `securityLevel: "loose"` skips Mermaid's final DOMPurify pass, whose parse of the SVG reports every style attribute as a violation. The finished SVG gets its `<style>` back and a fixed size, and is shown only as an `<img>` from a `data:` URL (`img-src 'self' data:` already allows it): an image is its own document, where no script runs, so "loose" adds no way to run code; the source is the participant's own spec. If a diagram cannot be drawn, the page says "The diagram could not be drawn. Ask the AI to fix it, or read it as text below." Labels are SVG text (`htmlLabels: false`), font Arial. `PAGES_HEADERS_POLICY_VERSION` stays 1.
- **(1.9) `npm run eval -- spec`:** `platform/api/eval/spec.json` holds 6 examples sent to the real answer request (`answerRequest`, the same the worker sends), scored on the raw answer: first specs for the three ideas (fitness tracker, movies tracker, nutrition calculator, after a scripted step 1), a change ("Please add a screen that shows my longest run."), each needing the shape above, keywords in the spec, and a diagram that Mermaid parses and draws; and two questions about a given spec, which must be answered with no new spec. Mermaid cannot parse flowcharts in Node.js without a DOM ("DOMPurify.addHook is not a function"), so the eval builds the pages and runs the page's own `diagram.js` in Playwright's Chromium (already a dev dependency; no jsdom). About $0.045 a run; 6 live runs in a row passed 6/6.
- **(1.9) Browser tests:** step 2 in `pages.test.ts` against a mocked API, under the real Content-Security-Policy (no violation allowed): the diagram is a decoded `data:` image with drawn pixels, inside the card and above the sections, at 1280 px and 375 px; Write my spec to the first version; a question keeps "Version 1" and a change shows "Version 2"; Approve spec and the approved state after a reload; a refused approval; an undrawable diagram.
- **(1.8 follow-up) `npm run conversation -- reset <nickname> [--yes]`** (asked by Franck, 2026-11-23, after his first test conversation came back on reload): root script `npm run -s conversation -w platform --` (`platform/src/conversation/`: `command.ts` tested against the API's in-memory fakes through the real history and send routes, `store.ts`, `main.ts`), run with the owner's own credentials like `flags`. Without `--yes` it only prints the step and how many messages, replies and spec versions it would delete (a dry run first, since a reset cannot be undone). With `--yes`: one transaction deletes the `CONVERSATION` item (condition: unchanged since it was read, i.e. the same `pendingId`, or none, and the same `updatedAt`, which every sent or answered message changes; no item read: still none) and sets the profile's `step` back to `clarify` (condition: the profile exists and is at clarify, spec or approved), then `BatchWriteItem` deletes the `MSG#` and `SPEC#` items listed before it (batches of 25, unprocessed items retried 4 more times with waits of 200 to 1600 ms). It refuses while the AI is answering (a pending message younger than `STALE_PENDING_SECONDS`, so up to 20 minutes after a worker crash); a flagged message waiting for a facilitator is cleared with the rest; a stale one does not block (its late job finds no message and is skipped). Kept: profile, nickname, sessions and the `USAGE` totals, since that money was spent and phase 3's $5 cap counts it. A run that stops part way is finished by running it again (the step is already clarify and the conversation item gone). Output: counts and the nickname as typed, never a text or an id; an error prints only its name (an AWS error message can quote a key), not through `redactError`. No new dependency (`@aws-sdk/client-dynamodb` is there), no infra change (no Lambda role gets delete rights).

## Phase 0: foundations

- **AWS access:** least-privilege CloudFormation stack `ssd-workshop-access` (`infra/access/access-stack.yaml`): permissions boundary, GitHub OIDC deploy role limited to `main`, IAM user for Claude's sessions, $100/month budget. Rights are limited to `ssd-workshop-*` names, the `project=ssd-workshop` tag and DNS under `workshop.rankly.games`; the stack protects itself from the deploy role.
- **No CDK bootstrap roles:** CDK uses `CliCredentialsStackSynthesizer` with our own `ssd-workshop-cdk-assets-*` bucket, so deploys run with the least-privilege role instead of the account-wide `cdk-hnb659fds-*` roles. Stacks without file assets send their template inline instead (see "Synthesizers (0.4)").
- **Licence:** MIT, since the repository is public for the pitch.
- **Platform language:** TypeScript for infrastructure, API and web pages, so the platform is one language end to end; advanced participant apps use Python. (Franck, 2026-11-22)
- **SES:** already in production mode for rankly.games, so codes can go to any allow-listed address; sender `ssd-workshop@rankly.games`. (Franck, 2026-11-22)
- **Access stack split:** the deploy rights are managed policies of at most 6,144 characters each (IAM's cap): `ssd-workshop-deploy` and `ssd-workshop-deploy-edge`, plus `ssd-workshop-deploy-read` since try 3 of ticket 0.3.
- **Workspaces:** npm workspaces `infra` and `platform`; `templates/` holds only a README and becomes a workspace when it gets code.
- **Checks at the root:** one ESLint flat config (`@eslint/js` recommended + typescript-eslint strict), one strict `tsconfig.base.json` with a root `tsconfig.json` covering `infra/` and `platform/`, and vitest run from the root with `--passWithNoTests`. A workspace gets its own `tsconfig.json` when it gets code.
- **Tool versions:** ESLint 10, TypeScript 5.9 (typescript-eslint supports TypeScript below 6.1), vitest 5 (vitest 3 pulls a critical `tinypool` advisory).
- **Deny rules:** `.claude/settings.json` denies `cdk deploy/destroy/bootstrap`, `aws iam`, every CloudFormation stack write (not only `ssd-workshop-access`, since workshop stacks deploy from `main`), Route53 record changes, pushes to `main`, force pushes, and reading `.env*`, `secrets/`, keys and `~/.aws/credentials`.
- **Plugin for every session:** `.claude/settings.json` enables `spec-driven@cadenceworks` from the `cadenceworks-dev/claude-workflow` marketplace for local sessions; cloud sessions take it from the claude.ai account.
- **Path rules:** `.claude/rules/infra.md`, `platform.md` and `templates.md`.
- **Allowed without asking:** `.claude/settings.json` lets Claude sessions run `npm ci`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` without a permission prompt, since they touch only the working tree and the npm cache.
- **Engines:** `package.json` requires Node 22.21 or newer (built-in `proxyEnv`, used by the smoke script behind a proxy) and npm 10 or newer, the versions CI and the cloud sessions use.
- **Limits of the deny rules:** `Read(...)` denies cover Claude's file tools; a shell `cat .env` or an `aws --profile x iam ...` form is not guaranteed to be caught. The real controls are that no secrets live in the repository and that the AWS user's least-privilege policy refuses what it must not do.
- **CI:** one GitHub Actions job `ci` on every pull request (npm ci, lint, type check, tests, `cdk synth`, `cfn-lint` on the access stack), so branch protection needs one required check. `permissions: contents: read` only, no AWS credentials, checkout without persisted credentials; cfn-lint pinned (`cfn-lint==1.61.0`) and installed with pip; actions pinned to major versions.
- **CDK runner:** `tsx` runs `infra/bin/app.ts` (`cdk.json` `app`), because it runs our ESM + `NodeNext` TypeScript with no extra config, where ts-node needs an ESM loader setup.
- **Access stack test:** `infra/access/access-stack.test.ts` parses the template with the `yaml` package (dev dependency of `infra`; built-in types and custom tags for `!Ref`/`!Sub`/`!GetAtt`/`!Equals`, where `js-yaml` needs `@types/js-yaml` and a schema) and checks both deploy policies statically, with every unscopable write on a commented allow-list keyed by statement and action.
- **Access stack tightened (ticket 0.3):** `CreateServiceLinkedRole` moved to its own statement so the other role housekeeping rights no longer reach `aws-service-role/*`; CloudFront function rights limited to `function/ssd-workshop-*`; `ProtectThisStack` denies every non-read CloudFormation action on `ssd-workshop-access` (adds cancel, rollback and stack policy to update, delete and change sets).
- **Access stack, try 2 of ticket 0.3 (the stack is live, so Franck applies this with "Update stack"):**
  - *Tags:* `cloudfront:TagResource` and `acm:AddTagsToCertificate` moved from the create statements (request tag only, so the role could tag any distribution or certificate and then change it) to the change statements, which need `aws:ResourceTag/project=ssd-workshop`. AWS authorizes the tag action again when a resource is created with tags (CreateDistributionWithTags, RequestCertificate with tags); ACM documents that the resource tag then holds the tags being applied, CloudFront is proven by ticket 0.4. Other tag rights: `ec2:CreateTags` only through `ec2:CreateAction` or the resource tag, `lambda:TagResource` on functions by name and on event source mappings, `iam:TagRole` on `ssd-workshop-*` roles (never the deploy role).
  - *API Gateway:* the region-wide `ApiGatewayInRegion` is replaced by `POST /restapis` and `POST /apis` with the request tag, and writes under `/restapis/*` and `/apis/*` with the resource tag (child resources inherit the API's tag for authorization). Not granted: `/tags/*` (changing an API's tags after creation), `/account` (account-wide CloudWatch role), domain names, API keys, usage plans.
  - *Own identities:* `ProtectAccessIdentities` denies every IAM action except `Get*`/`List*` on the role `ssd-workshop-deploy`, the user `ssd-workshop-claude` and the policies `ssd-workshop-boundary`, `ssd-workshop-deploy` and `ssd-workshop-deploy-edge`, because `role/ssd-workshop-*` also matches the deploy role.
  - *PassRole:* own statement, `iam:PassedToService` limited to `lambda`, `ecs-tasks`, `ec2`, `states`, `scheduler` and `events` (`.amazonaws.com`). Not `cloudformation`: the CLI-credentials synthesizer passes no stack role.
  - *Service-linked roles:* `iam:AWSServiceName` `ecs.amazonaws.com` only (ECS builds); spot or Auto Scaling roles are added if the k3s cluster needs them.
  - *Other writes:* event source mappings need `lambda:FunctionArn` `ssd-workshop-*`; `lambda:TagResource` (unnamed) only on event source mapping ARNs; `scheduler:CreateScheduleGroup` only on `schedule-group/ssd-workshop-*`; `ecs:CreateCluster` and `ecs:RegisterTaskDefinition` only by name (`cluster/ssd-workshop*`, `task-definition/ssd-workshop*`, both have a resource type), stricter than a request-tag condition; `ec2:RunInstances` needs the project tag on the subnet, security groups and launch template, and allows any AMI and new network interfaces in this account; key pairs are no longer allowed (k3s nodes use Systems Manager). Still unscoped because AWS offers only `Resource *`: `ecs:DeregisterTaskDefinition`, `logs:CreateLogDelivery`, `logs:DeleteLogDelivery`.
  - *Names:* log groups are `ssd-workshop-*`, `/aws/lambda/ssd-workshop-*` and `/ecs/ssd-workshop-*` (deploy policy and boundary) instead of `*ssd-workshop*`; `WorkshopStacks` lost the `changeSet/*` resource, since change set actions authorize against the stack ARN.
  - *Test:* a name counts as scope only when it starts with `ssd-workshop` after the resource type; a tag-adding action with only a request tag always fails; `ec2:CreateAction` counts only for creates that need the project tag; each hole class has a test that runs the old pattern and expects a failure; the policy sizes are checked against 6,144 characters.
- **Access stack, try 3 of ticket 0.3: shared account (Franck, 2026-11-22: the workshop stays in the rankly.games account, which also runs the rankly.games, www, play and letters distributions; Franck applies this with "Update stack").** Every hole IAM can close is closed; what remains is in `infra/access/README.md`, "Accepted risks".
  - *Reads:* a third managed policy `ssd-workshop-deploy-read` (attached to the deploy role and the Claude user, protected by `ProtectAccessIdentities`) holds the reads, because `ssd-workshop-deploy` would pass 6,144 characters. Account-wide only reads without secrets (stack list, template validation, `ec2:Describe*`, `ecs:List*`, certificates, hosted zone list, Bedrock models and inference profiles, service quotas, SES account and identities, bucket and log group names, costs). Removed account-wide: `lambda:List*`, `ecs:Describe*`, `apigateway:GET`, `cloudformation:Describe*`/`ListExports`/`GetTemplateSummary`, `iam:Get*`/`List*`, `cloudfront:Get*`/`List*`, `ses:Get*`/`List*`, `bedrock:Get*`/`List*` (models and inference profiles only), `logs:Describe*` (log groups only), `ssm:DescribeParameters`, `budgets:View*` (the workshop budget only), `sts:GetCallerIdentity` (needs no permission). Reads on workshop scope instead: stacks `ssd-workshop-*` (CDK names the stack), functions, ECS and other named resources by name, IAM on `/ssd-workshop/` roles and instance profiles and `ssd-workshop-*` policies, APIs and distributions by project tag, the rankly.games zone. Explicit denies: `ec2:DescribeInstanceAttribute`, `DescribeLaunchTemplateVersions`, `DescribeSpotFleetRequests` and `DescribeSpotInstanceRequests` outside the project tag (user data; the last three have no resource tag, so they are denied everywhere) and `apigateway:GET` on `/apikeys*`.
  - *ECS task definitions:* `ecs:DescribeTaskDefinition` supports only `Resource *` and returns every task definition's environment, so it is not granted; the first ticket that deploys one brings the choice to Franck (grant on `*`, or a CloudFormation service role).
  - *CloudFront parts without tags:* create and read only (`CreateOriginAccessControl`, `CreateCachePolicy`, `CreateOriginRequestPolicy`, `CreateResponseHeadersPolicy`); no update or delete on `*`, so the other distributions' policies and origin access controls are safe. Ticket 0.4 uses AWS-managed cache and response-headers policies and `RemovalPolicy.RETAIN` on its origin access control.
