  - *EC2 instances:* `RunInstances` on images only with `ec2:Owner` `amazon` (k3s nodes use Amazon Linux 2023); instance, volumes and new network interfaces with the project request tag; subnets, security groups, launch templates and existing network interfaces with the project resource tag. `RunInstances` is no longer in the request-tag statement on `*`.
  - *IAM path:* workload roles and instance profiles live under `/ssd-workshop/`; every role right (create, policies, housekeeping, `PassRole`, boundary `PassRole`) is on `role/ssd-workshop/*` and `instance-profile/ssd-workshop/*`, which structurally excludes the deploy role (path `/`). `iam:UpdateAssumeRolePolicy` stays: CloudFormation changes a role's trust in place when CDK's `assumedBy` changes, and dropping it would not close outside trust anyway, since `CreateRole` sets any trust policy.
  - *No self-assume:* explicit deny of `sts:AssumeRole`, `sts:TagSession` and `sts:SetSourceIdentity` for both principals, because a role whose trust policy names a principal of the same account needs no identity allow.
  - *Boundary:* `ecs:RunTask` only on `task-definition/ssd-workshop-*` with `ecs:cluster` `cluster/ssd-workshop-*`; stop and describe on `task/ssd-workshop-*/*`; image pulls on `repository/ssd-workshop-*`; invalidations on tagged distributions; `cloudwatch:PutMetricData` only in the namespace `ssd-workshop`; `ssm:UpdateInstanceInformation` only on instances tagged with the project; `PassRole` only `/ssd-workshop/` roles to `ecs-tasks.amazonaws.com`. The boundary goes through the same scope test as the deploy policies, with its own exception list.
  - *Budget:* `budgets:ModifyBudget` removed; only `ViewBudget` on `ssd-workshop-monthly`.
  - *S3:* every bucket grant (deploy and boundary) also needs `aws:ResourceAccount` = this account, because bucket names are global.
  - *Names:* `ssd-workshop-*` with the dash everywhere (ECR repositories, ECS clusters, services, tasks and task definitions were `ssd-workshop*`); the test rejects names without the dash.
  - *DNS:* record types limited to `A`, `AAAA`, `CNAME`, and a `Null` guard on the record-name and record-type keys (`ForAllValues` is true when the key is missing).
  - *Sharing outside the account:* denied for both principals: S3 bucket and object ACLs, Secrets Manager, ECR, DynamoDB, SSM and CloudWatch Logs resource policies, Lambda function URLs and layer permissions, SQS and SNS `AddPermission`, DynamoDB exports, log subscription filters; `lambda:AddPermission` only for API Gateway, EventBridge, SNS and S3; EventBridge targets only in this account; SNS subscriptions only to workshop Lambda functions and queues. Kept, and listed as accepted risks: `s3:PutBucketPolicy` (CDK writes the bucket policy for the CloudFront origin access control), SQS and SNS policies set as attributes.
  - *Size:* `Bedrock` and `SesForTesting` moved from `ssd-workshop-deploy` to `ssd-workshop-deploy-edge` to keep each policy under 6,144 characters.
  - *Recommended to Franck:* enable the free IAM Access Analyzer external-access analyzer for the account, to see any role or resource policy shared outside it.
- **Access stack, try 4 of ticket 0.3 (last try, approved by Franck; the workshop stays in the shared rankly.games account; Franck applies this with "Update stack").** Every hole IAM can close is closed; the rest is listed in `infra/access/README.md`, "Accepted risks" (now 14 items).
  - *EC2 parents:* a request tag is true for every resource a call names, also existing parents. The EC2 rights move to a fifth managed policy `ssd-workshop-deploy-ec2` (attached to both principals, protected by `ProtectAccessIdentities`). Request-tag statements list only ARN types that are new in every call of their actions (`Ec2NewTopLevel`, `Ec2NewInVpc`, `Ec2NewAttached`); the parents (VPC, subnet, security group, route table, Elastic IP, network interface, launch template) need the project resource tag (`Ec2ParentsTagged`). Resource types per action were read from the AWS Service Authorization Reference (machine-readable `servicereference.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/v1/ec2/ec2.json`, 2026-11-22). `CreateVolume` from any snapshot is denied. `ec2:CreateNetworkInterface` is added (request tag on the interface, resource tag on subnet and security groups).
  - *RunInstances network interfaces:* only with the project resource tag. A network interface that `RunInstances` creates has no resource tag, so the k3s ticket creates the node's interface first and passes its id in the launch template (rules in `.claude/rules/infra.md`). This rules out Auto Scaling groups for k3s nodes until revisited.
  - *ECS:* deny `ecs:RunTask` and `ecs:StartTask` unless `ecs:cluster` is `cluster/ssd-workshop-*`. `CreateService`/`UpdateService` authorize on the service ARN, which holds the cluster name (`service/ssd-workshop-*/*`), so no extra deny is needed; `CreateTaskSet` is not granted.
  - *EventBridge:* deny `events:PutRule` when `events:source` names anything other than `ssd-workshop.*`, and when the pattern has no source (a pattern without one, or a schedule rule, matches every project's events). Schedules use EventBridge Scheduler; AWS service events (ECS task state changes, CloudTrail) are not available as rules.
  - *ACM:* `RequestCertificate` needs `acm:DomainNames` within `workshop.rankly.games` and `*.workshop.rankly.games`, with a `Null` guard.
  - *SES:* sends only on `identity/ssd-workshop@rankly.games` and `identity/rankly.games` (any region of this account; SES authorizes the domain identity when the address is not verified on its own), still pinned by `ses:FromAddress`. No configuration set is granted.
  - *Reads:* `ec2:DescribeVpnConnections` denied everywhere (VPN pre-shared keys); `ses:GetAccount` removed (contact addresses).
  - *Sharing and egress:* added to `NoExternalSharing`: `logs:CreateExportTask`, `s3:PutBucketLogging`, `s3:PutInventoryConfiguration`, `s3:PutAnalyticsConfiguration`, `s3:PutReplicationConfiguration` and, for the deploy principals, `s3:PutBucketNotification` (none has a condition key for the destination). The boundary gets the same deny list except `s3:PutBucketNotification`, which CDK's bucket notifications handler (a workshop role) needs: accepted risk 12.
  - *Policy layout:* the sharing denies moved from `ssd-workshop-deploy` to `ssd-workshop-deploy-edge` (which lost the EC2 statements) to keep every policy under 6,144 characters. Sizes: boundary 3,634, deploy 5,170, edge 4,527, ec2 3,114, read 2,264.
  - *Test:* a request tag on `Resource "*"` counts as scope only for actions on a commented list (`cloudfront:CreateDistribution`, `acm:RequestCertificate`, which authorize no existing resource); an `EC2_PARENTS` table fails any request-tag statement that lists a parent ARN of its actions; new checks for ECS clusters, EventBridge sources, ACM names, SES identities, the boundary's sharing denies, VPN and SES account reads, and GitHub workflows (only `deploy.yml`, on push to `main`, may request `id-token: write` or `write-all`).
  - *Accepted risks added or reworded:* CloudFront origins and aliases, the OIDC subject matching other `main` events, `lambda:AddPermission` without source conditions, participant code under workshop roles (README intro corrected), web-identity trust in risk 2, the `From:` header with `SendRawEmail` in risk 5, bucket notifications from workload roles.
- **Access stack, tag on create (ticket 0.3, after try 4; found by the ticket 0.4 thread).** CloudFormation creates a distribution with `CreateDistributionWithTags` and a certificate with tags; AWS then also checks `cloudfront:TagResource` / `acm:AddTagsToCertificate`, which were allowed only on resources already tagged, so the first edge deploy would fail. `TagOnCreateThroughCloudFormation` allows both with the project request tag, no project tag on the resource yet, and `aws:CalledVia` CloudFormation; `NoResourceImport` denies change sets that import existing resources, the only CloudFormation path to an existing resource. Needs live proof in ticket 0.4 that CloudFormation sets `aws:CalledVia` for these calls; if it does not, the deploy fails safely (no wider right is granted). Sizes: deploy 5,338, edge 4,854.
- **Deploy role trust: `main` only.** The role trusts only the subject `repo:kifbv/ssd-workshop:ref:refs/heads/main`; the `environment:*` entry is gone. Ticket 0.5's deploy job therefore uses no GitHub environment (an environment changes the OIDC subject to `environment:<name>`, which the role refuses).
- **After applying the access stack:** Franck deactivates the admin access keys used so far once the `ssd-workshop-claude` key works (README step 7).
- **CDK and strict types:** aws-cdk-lib's declarations break `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, so a synthesizer is asserted to `IStackSynthesizer` at the call site instead of turning the flag off. `@types/node` 22 is a root dev dependency for Node built-ins in tests.
- **CDK stacks (0.4):** three stacks: `ssd-workshop-cdk-assets` (eu-central-1, assets bucket), `ssd-workshop-site` (eu-central-1, private site bucket and its pages) and `ssd-workshop-edge` (us-east-1, certificate, CloudFront distribution and the `workshop.rankly.games` A/AAAA records). Deploy order assets, site, edge. Replaces the empty "first stack" of 0.3.
- **Distribution and record in the us-east-1 stack (0.4):** the brief put CloudFront and the DNS record in the site stack; they live with the certificate in `ssd-workshop-edge` because CloudFront and Route53 are global and the certificate must be in us-east-1. This avoids CDK cross-region references, which write SSM parameters under `/cdk/exports/` through a Lambda that the boundary and the deploy policy forbid. Nothing is passed between regions: the edge stack imports the site bucket by its fixed name and regional domain name.
- **Site bucket policy (0.4):** `s3:GetObject` only for the `cloudfront.amazonaws.com` service principal with `AWS:SourceAccount` = the account and `AWS:SourceArn` like `arn:aws:cloudfront::<account>:distribution/*`, plus the TLS-only deny. Any distribution of the account could read it, not only ours, because the distribution's id is in another region's stack; that includes the account's existing non-workshop distributions (rankly.games, www, play, letters and others), if one were pointed at this bucket with an Origin Access Control. Accepted because the bucket's content is public through CloudFront anyway. Narrowing `AWS:SourceArn` to our distribution needs its id, so it is a later second step (after the first deploy, the id goes into the site stack). Until then the site bucket must only ever hold public content: nothing private, no participant data.
- **Synthesizers (0.4):** only the site stack uses `CliCredentialsStackSynthesizer` with the assets bucket. That synthesizer uploads every stack's template to the assets bucket and deploys with `TemplateURL`, which cannot work for the stack that creates the bucket, nor for the us-east-1 stack (bucket in eu-central-1). The assets and edge stacks use `LegacyStackSynthesizer`: the CLI sends their template inline with the caller's credentials, no bootstrap roles, no `BootstrapVersion` parameter. They must have no file assets (a test guards it) and stay under 50 KB.
- **Fixed account (0.4):** the account id, region, hosted zone id and domain are constants in `infra/lib/config.ts`, so synth and tests run without AWS credentials and bucket names are literal (`ssd-workshop-site-<account>`). `config.ts` is the only place that holds the account id, zone id and boundary ARN, so the workshop can move to a dedicated AWS account by changing that one file: code and tests derive them from it (including the assets bucket name in the site stack's synthesizer, which no longer uses `${AWS::AccountId}`), and `infra/README.md` reads them from it instead of repeating them.
- **Role path and boundary for every role (0.4):** one Aspect, `WorkshopIamAspect` (`infra/lib/aspects.ts`), applied to the whole app, sets `Path: /ssd-workshop/` (`IAM_PATH` in `infra/lib/config.ts`) and the `ssd-workshop-boundary` ARN (`BOUNDARY_ARN`) with `addPropertyOverride` on every `AWS::IAM::Role`, including the raw role `BucketDeployment` creates, and the same path on every `AWS::IAM::InstanceProfile`. The 0.3 access policy creates, changes and passes only `role/ssd-workshop/*` and only with the boundary. It replaces `PermissionsBoundary.of(stack)`, so there is one mechanism; tests check every role in the synthesized templates and the aspect on an L2 role (with `path: "/"`), a raw `CfnRole` and an instance profile. The path overrides any path a construct sets.
- **Origin Access Control retained (0.4):** the OAC `ssd-workshop-site` has `RemovalPolicy.RETAIN` (`DeletionPolicy` and `UpdateReplacePolicy` `Retain`, tested), because origin access controls have no tags and the deploy rights can only create them. Changing or deleting it needs Franck, or a replacement under a new name (e.g. `ssd-workshop-site-2`). Retain also holds when a failed first create rolls back, so a failed first edge deploy leaves the OAC behind and the retry fails on the name: Franck deletes it, or the code takes a new name.
- **Bucket ownership (0.4):** both buckets set `ObjectOwnership.BUCKET_OWNER_ENFORCED` explicitly (ACLs off, the S3 default since 2023), because the access policy denies ACL writes; tested.
- **Pages upload (0.4):** `platform/web` is copied to the site bucket by `BucketDeployment` with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=60`, so a merged change is live within minutes without a CloudFront invalidation. Its Lambda function and role are named `ssd-workshop-site-deploy`, its AWS CLI layer `ssd-workshop-awscli` (CloudFormation would name the layer after its logical id, outside the deploy policy) and its logs go to the `ssd-workshop-site-deploy` log group with one week of retention. No `autoDeleteObjects` (extra Lambda); buckets are retained on stack deletion.
- **CloudFront settings (0.4):** Origin Access Control named `ssd-workshop-site` (CDK would generate a name outside `ssd-workshop-*`), redirect HTTP to HTTPS, `index.html` as default root object, managed `CachingOptimized` cache policy, `PriceClass_100` (North America and Europe), HTTP/2 and HTTP/3, TLS 1.2 (2021) minimum, IPv6 with an AAAA record. No custom cache or response headers policies and no CloudFront Functions.
- **Security headers (0.4):** the default behaviour uses the managed `SecurityHeadersPolicy` (`ResponseHeadersPolicy.SECURITY_HEADERS`): HSTS (one year, no `preload`), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` and `X-XSS-Protection`. Managed, so no new resource or cost; a Content-Security-Policy needs a custom policy and waits until the pages have scripts.
- **Names test (0.4):** the names test walks every resource's properties at any depth and requires every `*Name` key (such as `OriginAccessControlConfig.Name`) to start with `ssd-workshop-`, except DNS names (`DomainName`, `DNSName`, a record's `Name`), the bucket reference `DestinationBucketName` and `PolicyName` of inline role policies (not a resource of their own; their role is named).
- **Other sessions' worktrees (0.4):** `.claude/` is ignored by ESLint, excluded from vitest by a root `vitest.config.ts` (covered by the type check) and `.claude/worktrees/` is in `.gitignore`, so another session's checkout under `.claude/worktrees/` never changes lint, test or git results.
- **Placeholder page (0.4):** one self-contained `platform/web/index.html` ("SSD Workshop, coming soon" and one line on the workshop), system fonts, light and dark colours, no scripts or external requests.
- **Post-deploy checks (0.4):** the read-only commands for the criteria that need a live deploy are in `infra/README.md`; the zone snapshot for criterion 5 (`zone-before.json`) must be taken by Franck, or by a session with the read-only command, before ticket 0.5 is merged, and written outside the repository (`/tmp/`) so it is never committed, as is `zone-after.json`.
- **Deploy trigger (0.5):** `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` runs on `push` to `main` only: no `pull_request`, no `workflow_dispatch`, no tags. The role refuses other refs anyway once the 0.3 access stack update is applied; a redeploy of the same commit is a "Re-run all jobs" of the last run.
- **No GitHub environment (0.5):** the deploy job has no `environment:`, so its OIDC subject is `repo:kifbv/ssd-workshop:ref:refs/heads/main`, the only one the role trusts once the ticket 0.3 access stack update removes `environment:*`. Manual approval gates or environment secrets would need that line back; not needed while every merge is Franck's.
- **Deploy permissions and concurrency (0.5):** workflow-level `id-token: write` and `contents: read` only, checkout without persisted credentials, no `secrets.` references; `concurrency: deploy` with `cancel-in-progress: false`, so deploys run one at a time in merge order and a running CloudFormation update is never cancelled.
- **Role ARN from config.ts (0.5):** `DEPLOY_ROLE_NAME`/`DEPLOY_ROLE_ARN` live in `infra/lib/config.ts`; `infra/bin/print-deploy-settings.ts` (run with `tsx`) writes the role ARN, credentials region, site URL and the page's `<h1>` text to `$GITHUB_OUTPUT`, so the workflow holds no account id and the smoke check follows the page.
- **Deploy steps (0.5):** `npm ci`, `npm test` (cheap guard before touching AWS), settings, `aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@v4` (session name `ssd-workshop-deploy-<run id>`), `npx cdk deploy --all --require-approval never` from `infra/`, smoke check. Lint, type check and synth are not repeated: CI ran them on the pull request and `cdk deploy` synthesizes.
- **Smoke check (0.5):** after the deploy, `curl` must get 200 from `https://workshop.rankly.games/` with the `<h1>` text of `platform/web/index.html`; 10 attempts 30 seconds apart (about five minutes, longer than the 60-second page cache), then the run fails. This is the proof of criterion 4 on every merge.
- **Deploy timeout (0.5):** 45 minutes for the job, since the first deploy creates a certificate (DNS validation) and a CloudFront distribution; later deploys take a few minutes.
- **`yaml` dev dependency (0.5):** `"yaml": "^2.9.1"` in `infra/package.json` devDependencies, exactly as ticket 0.3 adds it, so the merge is trivial; the root vitest run resolves it from `infra/lib` through workspace hoisting. The tests parse `deploy.yml` and `ci.yml` as data instead of matching text. Small, no dependencies, widely used.
- **Trust test and merge order (0.5):** this pull request has no access stack test; ticket 0.3 owns the access stack. Criterion 2 is proven by 0.3's test "trusts only GitHub Actions of kifbv/ssd-workshop on main (no GitHub environments)" plus the applied stack update. Until that update is applied, the live role also trusts `environment:*`, which a workflow on any branch can name, so this pull request must merge after 0.3 and after Franck applies that stack update.
- **Smoke checks (0.6):** `npm run -s smoke -- bedrock | quota | ses <address>` at the root runs `platform/src/smoke/main.ts` with `tsx`; the rules (arguments, quota matching, masking, latency) are pure functions in `logic.ts` with vitest tests, the AWS calls stay thin. Unknown command or missing address prints usage and exits 1, before any AWS call.
- **Smoke run silently:** npm echoes every script's command line (`> tsx src/smoke/main.ts ses jane@...`), which would print the recipient address. The root script calls the workspace with `npm run -s`, which silences the inner echo; the outer echo can only be silenced by the caller, so the command is documented (usage text, `main.ts` header, here) as `npm run -s smoke -- ses <address>`.
- **SES configuration set (0.6, Franck 2026-11-22):** the rankly.games identity sends through its default configuration set `rankly-dev` (another project's), which the workshop principals may not use (live run: `AccessDenied` on `configuration-set/rankly-dev`). The access stack creates its own configuration set `ssd-workshop-email` (tagged `project=ssd-workshop`; the deploy rights cannot create SES resources) and the SES statements (`SesForTesting`, boundary `SendCodes`) allow exactly that set next to the two identities; every workshop email names it (`ConfigurationSetName`), so SES never falls back to `rankly-dev`. Franck applies this with a stack update.
- **Smoke model:** defaults to `eu.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6` (EU inference profile; the spec says Sonnet), overridable with `SMOKE_MODEL_ID`; an id that does not start with `eu.anthropic.` is refused. Converse API, one-word prompt, `maxTokens` 16. Sonnet 5.5 is not usable in this account (2026-11-22, live run): Bedrock answers "anthropic.claude-sonnet-5-5 is not available for this account" and its applied cross-region tokens-per-minute quota in eu-central-1 is 0 (AWS default 6,000,000). Sonnet 4.6 has 6,000,000 tokens and 50 requests per minute.
- **Smoke quotas:** matched by name on Service Quotas code `bedrock`: `Cross-region model inference tokens|requests per minute for Anthropic Claude <Family> <x.y>`, optional ` V1`; the `Global cross-region` and `1M Context Length` variants are left out, since `eu.*` profiles are geographic cross-region. Prints the applied value next to the AWS default. Sonnet 5.5 has no requests-per-minute quota in the list, so the script says so.
- **Smoke email:** SESv2 `SendEmail` from `ssd-workshop@rankly.games`, subject "SSD Workshop smoke test", a random sample 6-digit code. The recipient is printed masked (`f***@e***.com`); error messages are printed with credentials in URLs replaced (`scheme://***@`) and addresses masked; the MessageId is printed. Limit: the script masks only its own output; npm's command echo shows the full address unless the command is run with `npm run -s` (see "Smoke run silently").
- **Smoke proof without a real inbox:** the SES mailbox simulator (`success@simulator.amazonses.com`) only proves that SES accepted the send from `ssd-workshop@rankly.games`. The two `ses` acceptance criteria (arrival at a verified and at an unverified real address) need Franck to run the command and check the inboxes.
- **Smoke dependencies:** `@aws-sdk/client-bedrock-runtime`, `@aws-sdk/client-service-quotas`, `@aws-sdk/client-sesv2` (official AWS SDK v3 clients) as platform dependencies; `tsx` as a platform dev dependency, as in `infra`.
- **AWS SDK HTTP handler:** the clients get an explicit `NodeHttpHandler` (`@smithy/node-http-handler`, a direct platform dependency for this) when a proxy is set, because the Bedrock runtime client defaults to an HTTP/2 handler that ignores an `httpsAgent` passed as plain options and bypassed the proxy (live run 2026-11-22).
- **AWS SDK behind a proxy:** the SDK ignores `HTTPS_PROXY`; when it (or lowercase `https_proxy`) is set, the clients get an `https.Agent` with Node's built-in `proxyEnv` (Node 22.21+), so no proxy-agent dependency is needed.
- **OIDC subject in ID form (fix after 0.5):** the first deploy was refused because GitHub sends this repository's subject as `repo:kifbv@7999713/ssd-workshop@1467373954:ref:refs/heads/main` (CloudTrail, 2026-11-22). The access stack's `GitHubRepo` default is now `kifbv@7999713/ssd-workshop@1467373954`; the trust is still `main` only, and it also survives a rename of the owner or repository.
- **`iam:GetRole` without the path (fix after 0.5):** the first deploy then failed at the site stack's first role: CloudFormation calls `GetRole` before `CreateRole`, and IAM checks a role that does not exist yet as `role/ssd-workshop-site-deploy`, without `/ssd-workshop/`. The deploy policy now allows only `iam:GetRole` on `role/ssd-workshop-*`; every write stays on the `/ssd-workshop/` path.
- **Certificates through CloudFormation (fix after 0.5):** the edge stack then failed at `acm:RequestCertificate`: CloudFormation requests the certificate without tags and tags it afterwards, so `CertificatesTagged` (request tag required) refused it. A direct request with the tags was allowed (probe certificate requested from the session and deleted at once). New `CertificatesThroughCloudFormation`: the same names, no request tag, only with `aws:CalledVia` CloudFormation; tagging stays with `TagOnCreateThroughCloudFormation`.
- **Allow-list command layout (1.3):** `platform/src/allowlist/`: `logic.ts` (pure: arguments, CSV rules, path check, output text), `store.ts` (interface `upsert`/`listAll`, implemented on DynamoDB), `command.ts` (the whole flow with file reads, store and output passed in, tested against an in-memory store) and `main.ts` (wiring only). Root script `npm run -s allowlist -- import <file.csv> | list`, like smoke.
- **Allow-list dependency (1.3):** `@aws-sdk/client-dynamodb` `^3.1175.0` (same range as the other SDK clients); no `lib-dynamodb`, the two calls are short with plain attribute values.
- **Allow-list table name (1.3):** `TABLE_NAME = "ssd-workshop-data"` is a platform constant; a test imports `infra/lib/config.ts` and checks both are equal.
- **Allow-list upsert (1.3):** one `UpdateItem` per row on `allowListKey(email)` setting `type`, `email`, `group`, `role` and `createdAt = if_not_exists(createdAt, now)`, so a re-import changes group and role only and keeps `createdAt` and `participantId`; `ReturnValues: UPDATED_OLD` tells added (no old values) from updated. "Updated" counts every existing email, even when its group and role did not change.
- **Allow-list list (1.3):** a paginated `Scan` with filter `type = allow`, projecting only `group` and `role` (the addresses are never read); fine for tens of entries. Output is a table `Group / Role / Count`, groups alphabetical, roles in the order participant, facilitator, admin, then a `Total` line; group names go through the email masking in case an address ever lands in that column.
- **Allow-list CSV rules (1.3):** header `email,group,role` required as the first non-blank line (case and spaces ignored), UTF-8 BOM stripped, CRLF accepted, blank lines skipped, no quoted fields (a row without exactly 3 fields is rejected). Email: trimmed, lower-cased, ASCII only: local part `[a-z0-9._%+-]+`, domain labels `[a-z0-9-]+` separated by dots, alphabetic TLD of 2+ letters (no `_` in the domain; no accents or brackets anywhere); kept no wider than smoke's `redactEmails`, and a test checks every accepted form is fully masked by `redactError`. Role exactly `participant`, `facilitator` or `admin` after trim and lower-case. Group trimmed, non-empty, rejected when it holds an `@` (`group looks like an email address`), so an address pasted in the wrong column is never stored or printed; otherwise kept as written with no character restriction (the CSV format already excludes commas). A later line with an email seen earlier in the same file is rejected as `duplicate of line N`. A file without the header (or empty) imports nothing and exits 1.
- **Allow-list output (1.3):** `Added: N`, `Updated: N`, `Rejected: N`, then one `Line N: <reasons>` per rejected line (reasons `invalid email`, `empty group`, `unknown role`, `expected 3 fields, found N`, `duplicate of line N`, several joined with commas). Line numbers are 1-based file lines. No message ever holds an address; AWS errors are printed through smoke's `redactError` (addresses masked).
- **Allow-list exit codes (1.3):** 0 when the import ran, even with rejected rows (the valid rows are stored and every rejection is printed); 1 on a usage error, a refused or missing file, a file without the header, or an AWS error. If AWS fails halfway, the counts so far are printed before the error (re-running is safe: upserts are idempotent).
- **Allow-list file outside any repository (1.3):** the path must be absolute; a relative path is refused ("give an absolute path to the CSV file", exit 1), because through the root script `INIT_CWD` is the repository root wherever the command was typed, so `../people.csv` would silently read another file. The repository root is the nearest folder above `main.ts` whose `package.json` declares workspaces (`realpathSync.native`). The CSV is refused if it is inside that root as written (so a file that does not exist yet is refused too) or after `realpathSync.native` (symlinks), comparing paths case-insensitively (macOS ignores case and realpath does not fix it; a false refusal is harmless); if it has more than one hard link (`nlink > 1`, realpath cannot follow them); or if its real folder or any folder above it holds a `.git` entry (a folder in a clone, a plain file in a work tree or submodule; `existsSync` plus `lstatSync`, walking up to the filesystem root). No git subprocess: no git environment variable (`GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, `GIT_DISCOVERY_ACROSS_FILESYSTEM`), "dubious ownership" error or missing git can make the check pass a file that is in a repository. A path that is not a regular file is refused after the repository checks ("Refused: the CSV path is not a file."), before the hard-link check whose count means nothing for a directory. All checks run before the file is read and before any AWS client is created; a missing file is "CSV file not found."
- **CSV files ignored by git (1.3):** `*.csv` is in `.gitignore` as a second barrier (no tracked `.csv` exists).
